# Clicky - Agent Instructions

<!-- This is the single source of truth for all AI coding agents. CLAUDE.md is a symlink to this file. -->
<!-- AGENTS.md spec: https://github.com/agentsmd/agents.md, supported by Claude Code, Cursor, Copilot, Gemini CLI, and others. -->

## Overview

macOS menu bar companion app. Lives entirely in the macOS status bar (no dock icon, no main window). Clicking the menu bar icon opens a custom floating panel with companion voice controls. Uses push-to-talk (ctrl+option) to capture voice input, transcribes it with Apple Speech (this fork's provider; AssemblyAI streaming and OpenAI are the alternatives), and sends the transcript + a screenshot of the user's screen to Claude. Claude responds with text (streamed via SSE) and voice (ElevenLabs TTS, played as it streams in). A blue cursor overlay can fly to and point at UI elements Claude references on any connected monitor.

All API keys live on a Cloudflare Worker proxy, so nothing sensitive ships in the app.

## Architecture

- **App Type**: Menu bar-only (`LSUIElement=true`), no dock icon or main window
- **Framework**: SwiftUI (macOS native) with AppKit bridging for menu bar panel and cursor overlay
- **Pattern**: MVVM with `@StateObject` / `@Published` state management
- **AI Chat**: Claude (Sonnet 5.5 default, Opus 5.5 optional, both at low effort) via Cloudflare Worker proxy with SSE streaming. `ClaudeVoiceModel` in `ClaudeAPI.swift` holds the model IDs, and a choice saved by an older version moves to the latest model of its family. Use only the latest model of each family.
- **Prompt caching**: the app marks a cache breakpoint on the `/chat` system prompt (`ClaudeAPI.streamingRequestBody`), and the Worker puts one on the system prompt of every route that calls Anthropic, so later calls within 5 minutes read it at a tenth of the input price
- **Speech-to-Text**: Apple Speech, selected by `VoiceTranscriptionProvider` = `apple` in Info.plist. Setting it to `assemblyai` (real-time streaming over a websocket, `u3-rt-pro` model) or `openai` (upload on release) switches providers
- **Text-to-Speech**: ElevenLabs `eleven_v4_turbo` via Cloudflare Worker proxy, streamed as 24 kHz PCM and played as it arrives, so speech starts about 0.25 seconds after the request instead of after the whole line is generated
- **Screen Capture**: ScreenCaptureKit (macOS 14.2+), multi-monitor support
- **Voice Input**: Push-to-talk via `AVAudioEngine` + pluggable transcription-provider layer. System-wide keyboard shortcut via listen-only CGEvent tap.
- **Element Pointing**: Claude embeds `[POINT:x,y:label:screenN]` tags in responses. The overlay parses these, maps coordinates to the correct monitor, and animates the blue cursor along a bezier arc to the target.
- **Concurrency**: `@MainActor` isolation, async/await throughout
- **Task agent harness**: the task agent's loop runs in a Go helper process (`harness/`, bundled as `Contents/Helpers/clicky-agent-harness`), not in Swift, and by default the voice command brain runs there too (`harness/brain.go`, `harness/brainloop.go`, `harness/brainsession.go`, and the `brainRunsInTheHarness` flag, which an install with no app secret overrides). The app and the harness talk in JSON lines on stdin and stdout (`AgentHarness.swift`, `HarnessVoiceCommand.swift`, `harness/protocol.go`). The harness runs Claude through the Anthropic Go SDK with its base URL set to the Worker's `POST /v1/messages` passthrough, which holds the API key, and asks the app for the tools that touch the Mac or Notion, and for a voice command, the scene, video, and agent tools the router offers. The agent's whole definition lives in `harness/taskagent.go`. It also runs skills written as Markdown (`harness/skills.go`), is an MCP client for the servers the learner lists in `mcp.json` (`harness/mcp.go`), and stops in code for the learner's approval before any tool that reads private data or moves data out (`harness/approval.go`), and decides the learner's voice answer to such a request (`harness/voiceapproval.go`). For a voice command it also reverses the newest change when the learner says "undo that", so the model never works out the reverse of an angle (`harness/brainundo.go`). Go is needed to build the app
- **Analytics and updates**: none. The fork removed the upstream PostHog analytics, its email signup form, and the Sparkle updater, whose feed pointed at another app

### API Proxy (Cloudflare Worker)

The app never calls external APIs directly. All requests go through a Cloudflare Worker (`worker/src/index.ts`) that holds the real API keys as secrets.

| Route | Upstream | Purpose |
|-------|----------|---------|
| `POST /chat` | `api.anthropic.com/v1/messages` | Claude vision + streaming chat |
| `POST /v1/messages` | `api.anthropic.com/v1/messages` | A passthrough for the agent harness's Anthropic SDK. The body, the stream, the status, and the content type pass through unchanged, with the Worker's key and `anthropic-version`. Only this route forwards the caller's `anthropic-beta` header, since it requires the app secret and `/chat` does not. It shares one forwarding function with `/chat` (`forwardToAnthropic`) |
| `POST /tts` | `api.elevenlabs.io/v1/text-to-speech/{voiceId}/stream` | ElevenLabs TTS audio, streamed. An `output_format` query such as `pcm_24000` passes through, and without one the audio is MP3 |
| `POST /transcribe-token` | `streaming.assemblyai.com/v3/token` | Fetches a short-lived (480s) AssemblyAI websocket token |
| `POST /live-scene` | `api.openai.com/v1/responses`, or `api.anthropic.com/v1/messages` when OpenAI answers a 401, 403, 404, 429, or 5xx or cannot be reached | GPT Ultrafast, at medium reasoning effort, writes a three.js page and a quiz from the question and screenshot: GPT-6.1 Sol for a 3D model and GPT-6.2 Sol for a simulation. The body takes `kind`, `"model"` (the default, so an older app still works) or `"simulation"`, which adds the simulation rules to the prompt and makes the reply check require the page's `controls`, `setControl`, `run`, and `readouts`. When OpenAI cannot answer, the Worker builds the same scene on Sonnet 5.5 at Sonnet's default effort (`low`), ignoring `LIVE_SCENE_EFFORT`, and the page check and the retry once then run on Sonnet, so the retry never waits on OpenAI again. A 400 or 422 from OpenAI, and any benchmark `BENCH_MODEL`, never fall back |
| `POST /judge-answer` | `api.anthropic.com/v1/messages` | Sonnet grades a spoken answer as correct, named only, wrong, or a request for help, or hears a request to skip, repeat, get a hint, or change the view instead (`skip_question`, `repeat_question`, `wants_hint`, `scene_command`, where the skip, repeat, and scene command ones may have an empty `spokenReply`) |
| `POST /voice-command` | `api.anthropic.com/v1/messages` | Haiku 5.5 (low effort, thinking off) picks the app's tools for a spoken request, usually a follow-up command. The app sends `{ messages, tools: [{ name, description, inputSchema, readOnly? }] }`, the Worker adds a `confirmation` string to each tool (required unless `readOnly`), caches the system prompt and the tool list, and returns `{ content: [text or tool_use blocks], stopReason }`. The app runs the tools and calls again with the tool results while a lookup or a failure needs the model. With `brainRunsInTheHarness` on, the harness runs this loop and the route is not called |
| `POST /video-quiz-plan` | `api.openai.com/v1/responses`, or `api.anthropic.com/v1/messages` when OpenAI answers a 401, 403, 404, 429, or 5xx | GPT-6.2 Sol Ultrafast, or Sonnet 5.5 when OpenAI is out of credit, rate limited, down, or refuses the key, reads a video's timestamped transcript once, decides how many moments are worth a question, and picks them. Returns `{ moments: [{ pauseAtSeconds, sourceLineSeconds, question, answerLabel, functionWords, explanation, subject }] }`. Each moment lands on a transcript line boundary, at least 20 seconds in, and the moments are spread across the video |
| `POST /video-frame-parts` | `api.anthropic.com/v1/messages` | Sonnet finds the subject in a frame grabbed from the playing video, or a screen capture of a page image, and places up to 8 part labels, dropping any label that is not a short plain name. Returns `{ subjectFound, subjectBox, isArtwork, wholePictureIsSubject, pictureShowsDepth, parts: [{ label, x, y, isQuizAnswer }] }` in frame pixels, or `{ subjectFound: false }` for a chart or a person talking. `isArtwork` is true for a painting, print, drawing, or sculpture, including art of one animal such as Dürer's Young Hare, and the app then narrates facts about the art and never animates it. `wholePictureIsSubject` is true for a painted scene, such as a landscape or a fresco of several figures, and its box covers the whole painting. `pictureShowsDepth` is true for a cutaway, a 3D render, or a photo, and always false for a painted scene |
| `POST /landmark` | `api.anthropic.com/v1/messages` | Sonnet says whether a paused frame shows one famous real place, and returns `{ isFamousPlace, name, nameTakesThe, latitude, longitude, heightMeters, radiusMeters }`, where `nameTakesThe` says whether the spoken line puts "the" before the name, as in "the Colosseum" but not "Big Ben" |
| `POST /try-it-item` | `api.anthropic.com/v1/messages` | Sonnet 5.5 (low effort) reads a store page's text and its main photo for something the learner wants to try, and returns `{ title, whyThisSize, itemCorners: { topLeft, topRight, bottomRight, bottomLeft }, item }`, where `item` names the kind and holds that kind's one object: `{ kind: "wallArt", wallArt: { sizeFoundOnPage, size: { label, widthCentimeters, heightCentimeters, includesFrame }, otherSizes } }`, `{ kind: "furniture", furniture: { sizeFoundOnPage, size: { label, widthCentimeters, heightCentimeters, depthCentimeters }, otherSizes } }`, `{ kind: "clothing", clothing: { garment, sleeves, chosenSizeLabel, photoShows, printDescription } }`, or `{ kind: "other" }`. The model's JSON schema is a union of one variant per kind, with the corners outside it since repeating them in each variant made Anthropic refuse the grammar as too large, and the parser refuses a reply that carries another kind's object. `itemCorners` are the item's outer corners in the photo. `sizeFoundOnPage` is true only when the page text states the size, and with it false the reply skips the size and corner rules, since the app places nothing. The request is `{ pageURL, pageTitle, pageText, learnerRequest?, picture: { jpegBase64, width, height } }`, where `jpegBase64` is at most 4,000,000 characters, with the page title, page text, and learner request in `page_title`, `page_text`, and `learner_request` tags with every `<` written as `‹`. `learnerRequest` is the learner's words, up to 500 characters, which say which item they mean when the page shows several. `checkTryItItem` keeps wall art sizes from 5 to 1000 cm and furniture sizes from 10 to 600 cm in width and height and from 1 to 600 cm in depth (a floor mirror or a picture ledge is only a few centimeters deep), refuses an empty size label when the size is stated, keeps at most 8 other sizes, cuts nothing else, and needs convex corners covering at least 4% of the photo for wall art, furniture, and clothing, a reply that breaks a rule is asked for again once with the rule named (a bad `otherSizes` entry is named by its 1-based position and never by its label, since a model-written label in the retry prompt could carry instructions), and a second failure answers 422. `worker/src/testdata/try-it-item-replies.json` holds one response for each kind, plus wall art and furniture whose page states no size, which `tryItItem.test.ts` and `RoomArtTests.swift` both read, so the Worker's response and the app's reader cannot drift. Requires the app secret |
| `POST /room-wall` | the local depth service (`DEPTH_SERVICE_URL`) | Forwards `{ jpegBase64, maskPngBase64?, anchor: { u, v } }` to the depth service's `/wall` and returns its wall plane (`center`, `right`, `up`, `focalPx`, `principal`, `distanceMeters`, `wallAngleDegrees`, `planeInlierShare`) after checking it. 503 when `DEPTH_SERVICE_URL` is unset. 422 `{ error }` when the service answers 422 (too little bare wall, or a plane with a NaN or infinite number), with the service's error, or when the wall measures nearer than 0.3 m, farther than 12 m, or under 30% of its points on one plane. 502 when the service is down, slow (60 s), answers any other error, or replies with something that is not the plane. Requires the app secret |
| `POST /room-floor` | the local depth service (`DEPTH_SERVICE_URL`) | The same request as `/room-wall`, where the anchor is the pixel the furniture's front-bottom center stands on. Forwards it to the depth service's `/floor` and returns the floor's placement frame (`center`, `right` on the floor and level with the camera, `up` the floor normal, `forward` = up x right on the floor and away from the camera, `focalPx`, `principal`, `distanceMeters`, `floorTiltDegrees`, `planeInlierShare`) after checking it. Shares its forwarding and error mapping with `/room-wall` (`worker/src/depthService.ts`). 503 when `DEPTH_SERVICE_URL` is unset. 422 `{ error }` when the service answers 422 (too little bare floor, a wall or table instead of a floor, or a plane with a NaN or infinite number), with the service's error, or when the floor measures nearer than 0.3 m, farther than 12 m, tilted over 30 degrees from the camera's vertical (the service's own limit is 25, so this trips only for a service that changed), or under 30% of its points on one plane. 502 when the service is down, slow (60 s), answers any other error, or replies with something that is not the plane. Requires the app secret |
| `POST /room-furniture-composite` | `api.openai.com/v1/images/edits` | `gpt-image-2.6-sunburst` (the newest GPT image model, in the variant OpenAI names for editing precision) at medium quality draws a furniture product into a camera keyframe of the learner's room, for any furniture page with a photo and a size. The request is `{ keyframeJPEGBase64, keyframeWidth, keyframeHeight, productJPEGBase64, maskPNGBase64, itemTitle, itemSizeWords }`: the keyframe, the product cut out on white, and a PNG the keyframe's size that is white where the item and its contact shadow may be drawn (the projected box of its real size plus a shadow margin) and black elsewhere, with `itemSizeWords` such as "55 cm wide, 45 cm tall, 55 cm deep". Each image is at most 4,000,000 base64 characters, each side 1 to 4096, and the title and size words at most 300 characters each, written into the prompt in `item_title` and `item_size` tags with every `<` as `‹`. The Worker reads the mask (8-bit, non-interlaced gray, RGB, gray with alpha, or RGBA, by its first channel), which must be the keyframe's size and mark at least one pixel, and sends OpenAI the room as the first image, the product as the second, and an RGBA mask whose alpha is 0 where the app's mask is white. OpenAI follows a mask only as guidance, so the prompt also names the mask's box in the keyframe's pixels: without it, the live check drew a table about 15 percent too wide with two legs past the mask. The output size is the keyframe's own when OpenAI allows it (1280x720 is), else the nearest allowed size of the same shape (sides multiples of 16, at most 3840, 655,360 to 8,294,400 pixels), and a keyframe wider than 3:1 or taller than 1:3 gets a 400. Returns `{ imageJPEGBase64, width, height }` at the size OpenAI drew. A bad body or mask answers 400 before any model call, and a failed, refused, or timed out (120 s) edit answers 502 with a plain error. Logs `[/room-furniture-composite] usage` with the model, the tokens, and the seconds. On 2026-11-22 a 1280x720 call took 13 to 19 s and cost about $0.045 (about 2,000 to 2,400 image input tokens at $8 per million, 450 text tokens at $5, and 812 output tokens at $30). Requires the app secret |
| `POST /try-it-model` | fal.ai's queue (`queue.fal.run`), then fal's file storage | Turns a furniture product photo into a GLB with fal's hosted image-to-3D, TRELLIS 2 (`fal-ai/trellis-2`) unless `TRY_IT_MODEL_ENDPOINT` names another fal model id. The request is `{ productJPEGBase64, itemTitle }`, with the photo at most 4,000,000 base64 characters and the title at most 300 characters (TRELLIS 2 takes no prompt, so only the title's length is logged). The Worker submits the photo as a data URI, polls the request's status every 1.5 s, reads the result's `model_glb` (or `model_mesh`, as fal's other image-to-3D models name it), downloads that https URL without the key, at most 40 MB, checks the GLB magic, and returns the bytes as `application/octet-stream`. The fal key goes only to `https://queue.fal.run`, so a status, result, or cancel URL that a reply names elsewhere is refused. 503 `{ error: "FAL_KEY is not set" }` before any call when `FAL_KEY` is missing, and 503 for a `TRY_IT_MODEL_ENDPOINT` that is not a fal model id. 400 for a bad body. The whole call is bounded at 120 s: running out answers 504 and cancels a request still queued or running, and every other failure answers 502 with a plain error. Caches nothing, since the app keeps each model on disk. Not run live yet: there was no `FAL_KEY` on 2026-11-22, when fal's queue answered a fake key for `fal-ai/trellis-2` with 401. Requires the app secret |
| `POST /picture-flows` | `api.anthropic.com/v1/messages` | Sonnet 5.5 (low effort) draws what flows through a popped-out picture, like air through an engine, and returns `{ flows: [{ color, style, points: [[x, y], ...] }] }` in frame pixels, with `color` one of blue, red, orange, cyan, green, yellow, or white, and `style` one of smoke, sparks, lava, droplets, air, current, or beads. Up to 6 flows of 12 points, clamped into the frame. Moving solid parts, like a bicycle chain, are not flows, so a picture of only those returns an empty list. The app never calls it for an artwork |
| `POST /theme-ideas` | `api.anthropic.com/v1/messages` | Sonnet 5.5 (low effort) writes three theme ideas for the theme studio from `{ request, currentIdeas?, basedOnIdea?, picture? }`: `request` is 1 to 500 characters (UTF-16 code units, as JavaScript counts them, so the app cuts it by that count), `currentIdeas` is up to 3 ideas in the reply's shape and at most 24,000 characters of JSON, `basedOnIdea` is 1 to 3 and needs `currentIdeas`, and `picture` is `{ jpegBase64, width, height }` with each side 1 to 4096, and a body over any limit gets a 400 before any model call. Returns `{ title, spokenLine, ideas: [{ version: 2, name, accent, contrast, cursor: { kind, shapes, picture }, motion: { turnsTowardMovement, trailStreaks, whileThinking, whileListening, springs? }, agents: { ring, colors, faces: { kind } }, sounds: { done, correct, needsYourOK } }] }` with exactly 3 ideas and every color uppercased, each a version 2 theme.json that the Worker marks with `version: 2`. A `shapes` cursor has 1 to 6 SVG paths in a 24 by 24 box, using only the commands `M L H V C S Q T Z` with every point from -2 to 26, so no arcs, and only space, tab, carriage return, line feed, form feed, and comma between tokens. A shape with no fill needs a stroke width above 0, since it would draw nothing. A `picture` cursor is the learner's picture with a `fit` of `whole` or `circle`, an outline color, and an optional tint of strength 0 to 0.6, and is allowed only when the request had a `picture`. `agents.ring` is `perAgent`, with 3 to 6 `colors` the tokens take in turn, or `accent`, which puts every token in the accent color and needs `colors` empty, and `agents.faces.kind` is `triangle` or `picture`, which is allowed only when the request had a `picture`. The accent and every per-agent color need a contrast ratio of at least 3 against `#101211`, and a per-agent color is never a saturated red or green at an HSL lightness of 0.75 or less, since red means failed and green means done, while a light pastel such as a light pink passes. One per-agent color that breaks a color rule is dropped when 3 or more good ones remain, instead of asking the model again. `motion.springs` is optional and may be null: the Pop and Glide springs (`response` and `dampingFraction`) and the Point speed (`response`) a theme gives the app, moved into safe limits (`themeSpringLimits`: Pop response 0.2 to 0.45 and damping 0.55 to 1, Glide response 0.3 to 0.55 and damping 0.75 to 1, Point response 0.3 to 0.6) instead of refused, and the prompt maps bouncy, playful, snappy, calm, and smooth to numbers. Each sound is a macOS system sound. `checkThemeIdeas` checks every rule, a reply that breaks one is asked for again once with the broken rule named, and a second failure answers 422 with `{ error }`. The request and the current ideas go into the prompt in `theme_request` and `current_ideas` tags with every `<` replaced by `‹`, and text in the picture is treated as data. No app secret is required |
| `POST /study-guide` | `api.anthropic.com/v1/messages`, then `api.notion.com/v1/pages` | Sonnet drafts a study guide from a YouTube video's transcript as schema-checked JSON (`{ title, summary, sections: [{ heading, points: [{ text, seconds }] }], keyTerms }`), and the Worker writes it as its own private Notion page, with a "Review these first" section built from the quiz results the learner missed or skipped and a timestamp link on every point. A request with `revision` creates the new page, then trashes the old one. A `revision` with no `instruction` is a refresh: the Worker keeps the previous guide word for word, checks it like a model reply, and rebuilds the page with the new quiz results, with no model call. Returns `{ pageHandle, pageURL, guide }`, where `pageHandle` is the page id signed with an HMAC keyed by the Notion token, and a revision whose handle the Worker did not sign gets a 400, so it can never trash another page. `videoURL` must be an https URL on youtube.com, a subdomain of it, or youtu.be, and a quiz result's `askedAtSeconds` and every transcript line's `seconds` must be from 0 to 99,999. A transcript over 10,000 lines or 500,000 characters, any text field over 2,000 characters, a `videoURL` whose timestamp link would pass Notion's 2,000 character limit, or a handle over 200 characters gets a 400 before any model or Notion call. The prompt wraps the title, transcript, quiz results, and previous guide in `video_title`, `video_transcript`, `quiz_results`, and `previous_study_guide` tags, with every `<` in their text replaced by `‹` so none can close its tag, and treats them as text to work from, never as instructions. 503 when `NOTION_API_KEY` is missing |
| `POST /study-guide-status` | `api.notion.com/v1/pages/{id}` | Whether a saved guide's page is still in Notion and out of the trash. Takes `{ pageHandle }` and returns `{ pageExists }`, with `false` for a trashed page or a 404. A handle the Worker did not sign gets a 400, another Notion failure a 502, and a missing `NOTION_API_KEY` a 503 |
| `POST /notion-page` | `api.notion.com/v1/pages` | Writes a task agent's findings as a private Notion page from `{ title, summary, sections: [{ heading, points }], sources: [{ title, url }] }`, with https sources only, and returns `{ pageURL }`. 503 when `NOTION_API_KEY` is missing |
| `GET /v1/3dtiles/...` | `tile.googleapis.com` | Google Photorealistic 3D Tiles for the landmark page. Only `root.json` and dataset files pass, and the Worker adds `GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY` |

Worker secrets: `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `ELEVENLABS_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY` (a Maps Platform key with the Map Tiles API enabled and billing linked), `NOTION_API_KEY` (a Notion personal access token, which acts as its owner and may create pages with no parent page, for study guides and task pages), `CLICKY_APP_SECRET` (a random string the app sends in `x-clicky-app-secret`, and passes to the harness in its environment, which `/study-guide`, `/study-guide-status`, `/notion-page`, `/v1/messages`, `/try-it-item`, `/room-wall`, `/room-floor`, `/room-furniture-composite`, and `/try-it-model` require before any Notion, model, fal, or depth service call, so it is required for the voice command brain and the task agents as well as the Notion routes, and an install with no secret keeps its voice commands on the old `/voice-command` route, with a constant-time compare in `worker/src/appSecret.ts`, answering 401 to a missing or wrong secret and to every request when the Worker has none), `FAL_KEY` (a fal.ai key, only for `/try-it-model`, which answers 503 without it), and `ASSEMBLYAI_API_KEY` only when Info.plist selects the `assemblyai` provider
Worker vars: `ELEVENLABS_VOICE_ID`, `DEPTH_SERVICE_URL` (the local depth service for `/room-wall` and `/room-floor`, `http://127.0.0.1:8801` in `wrangler.toml`), optional `TRY_IT_MODEL_ENDPOINT` (the fal model id `/try-it-model` runs, `fal-ai/trellis-2` when unset), and optional `LIVE_SCENE_MODEL` (`gpt` by default, `sonnet`, `opus`, or `fable` for Claude Fable 5.2) and `LIVE_SCENE_EFFORT` (overrides the scene model's effort, which is `medium` for GPT and `low` for Claude, where GPT takes low, medium, or high)
Benchmark-only Worker vars, set with `--var` on a local `wrangler dev` and never in `wrangler.toml`: `BENCH_MODEL` (a `claude-*`, `gpt-*`, or `gemini-*` model that replaces the model of every route that calls `requestAnthropicMessage` and of `/chat`, and does nothing when unset), `BENCH_EFFORT` (replaces the route's effort), and `BENCH_SERVICE_TIER` (a `gpt-*` model only, such as `ultrafast`). A `gemini-*` model also needs `GEMINI_API_KEY`, in `worker/.dev.vars`, and refuses a service tier. A `claude-*` `BENCH_MODEL` also runs the agent harness's `/v1/messages` on that model, and a `gpt-*` or `gemini-*` one answers it with a 501, since the task agent uses Anthropic's server-side web search. On `/live-scene`, a `gpt-*` `BENCH_MODEL` runs the app's GPT request with that model and `BENCH_SERVICE_TIER`, where no tier means standard, and any other runs the Claude request on that model, whatever `LIVE_SCENE_MODEL` says. `bench/README.md` has the bakeoff runbook.

### Key Architecture Decisions

**Menu Bar Panel Pattern**: The companion panel uses `NSStatusItem` for the menu bar icon and a custom borderless `NSPanel` for the floating control panel. This gives full control over appearance (dark, rounded corners, custom shadow) and avoids the standard macOS menu/popover chrome. The panel is non-activating so it doesn't steal focus. A global event monitor auto-dismisses it on outside clicks.

**Cursor Overlay**: A full-screen transparent `NSPanel` hosts the blue cursor companion. It's non-activating, joins all Spaces, and never steals focus. The cursor position, the ring, the loop, response text, and pointing animations all render in this overlay via SwiftUI through `NSHostingView`.

**Global Push-To-Talk Shortcut**: Background push-to-talk uses a listen-only `CGEvent` tap instead of an AppKit global monitor so modifier-based shortcuts like `ctrl + option` are detected more reliably while the app is running in the background.

**Shared URLSession for AssemblyAI**: A single long-lived `URLSession` is shared across all AssemblyAI streaming sessions (owned by the provider, not the session). Creating and invalidating a URLSession per session corrupts the OS connection pool and causes "Socket is not connected" errors after a few rapid reconnections.

**Interrupting a reply**: Escape or a quick ctrl+option tap stops the answer Clicky is thinking about or speaking, and a tap with no words asks nothing. With the settings dial open, Escape only cancels it. With the Theme Studio card open, Escape closes it and keeps the old theme. Next, an "Add theme?" card is answered with a no, and then, with the room art panel open, Escape closes it, stops the camera, and ends its spoken line, and it also stops a room art request still reading the page or capturing the picture. With the quiz summary Card open, Escape closes it. With an instant replay open, Escape ends it and leaves the pop-out up (see **Instant replay**). With a scene or pop-out on screen, Escape keeps its scene behavior, and a tap stops the speech but leaves the scene up. Outside a video quiz question, Escape does nothing while a scene or pop-out is building or touring. Saying "put it back" or "put that away" (`asksToPutTheSceneBack`) sends a pop-out or explored scene back, during its tour or after it, unless a quiz question is waiting on it. A push-to-talk press sets a cancelled narration's responding state back to idle, so the listening ring shows.

**Cursor ring and loop**: Clicky's voice states share one ring around the cursor (`CursorRingView`, `cursorCue`), and the cursor triangle or ball stays visible in every state.
While the learner talks, 24 ticks start 20 points from the cursor's center and grow outward with the microphone level, 5 to 16 points long and 2.5 points wide (`CursorRingGeometry.tickLengths`).
While Clicky thinks, three dots orbit the cursor once every 1.2 seconds.
Speaking hides the ring, since the speech Callout already brightens its words with the voice.
A status line such as "Popping it out" is a Callout above the cursor and does not hide the ring, so the ticks and dots keep showing, and the dots' orbit (24 points out) reaches about 3 points into the Callout's body above its tail, since the tail's tip sits 12 points above the cursor and the tail is 9 points tall, so a dot draws over the Callout each time one passes the top.
The ring and the loop use the theme's accent color (Inarizaki's is `#F4F4F5`).
Reduce Motion holds the ticks still and turns the dots' orbit into a slow opacity change.
The cues hand off instead of cutting (`CursorRingHandoff`): on release the ticks shrink into the dots over 0.3 seconds (the ticks at 0, 120, and 240 degrees become the dots, and each other tick slides to its nearest dot and fades, so the last frame is the dots' first frame), and when speech starts the dots fly from their orbit into the speech Callout's tail over 0.38 seconds, shrinking to half and fading there (`CursorRingView.speechTailOffset`, which defaults to the speech Callout's tail tip, `tetherCalloutTipRiseAboveTheCursor` above the cursor).
Every other change of cue, and every change with Reduce Motion on, crossfades over `DS.Move.fadeSeconds`.

**Cursor body language** (`CursorBodyLanguage.swift`, the Tether Point move and emotes, for every cursor kind: the triangle, a theme's shapes, a picture, the volleyball): every rule is a pure function of time.
The glyph has a 25 percent glow in the cursor color and a small offset contact shadow under every cursor kind, and the triangle is shaded light at the tip to deep at the base with a light edge along its top (`CursorGlyphShade`, `CursorTriangleGlyph`).
In a pointing flight the cursor stretches to `DS.Move.pointStretch` (1.15) along its direction of travel at the middle of the flight, keeping its area (`CursorPointFlight`, `CursorBodyLanguageView`), whichever way the cursor faces, and its streaks and glow follow the same speed share.
On landing it squashes to 0.9 along the flight for 80 ms and springs back with Snap.
A ring 24 points wide (`DS.Move.pointLandingRingDiameter`) closes in on the target with Pop and stays there while the cursor points at it, and fades out over `DS.Move.fadeSeconds` when the cursor leaves (`CursorLandingRingRules`, `CursorLandingRingView`).
With Reduce Motion the flight is the existing fade, there is no stretch or squash, and the ring fades in over 0.2 seconds and stays still.
`CompanionManager.playCursorEmote` publishes a `CursorEmoteRequest`, and the overlay plays it: a nod (two 3.5 point dips over 0.36 seconds) when a transcript with words arrives, a shake (`DS.Move.shake`, 7 points) when Clicky speaks a failure line (`speakLiveSceneLine(_:isAFailure:)`, whose caller says the line reports something that went wrong: the voice command's gave-up line, "couldn't build that one", the pop-out, video quiz (`VideoQuizSession.speakLine`'s second value), picture flows (`PictureFlowsOutcome.failed`), Theme Studio design failure lines, and every room art failure line (`RoomArtPreviewController.Environment.speak`'s `isAFailure`, true for each line but "Here it is at ..."), since a failure line can start with any words, and the flag defaults to false, so a new failure line needs its caller to pass it), when the voice fallback says it lost its voice, when a background agent fails, or when a first quiz answer is wrong, and a hop (10 points, with a green ring opening from 24 to 52 points over 0.5 seconds) for a right quiz answer or a challenge hit (`cursorEmote(forAnswerOutcome:)`).
A shake tints the cursor red and a hop tints it green while they move, and a nod does not tint.
With Reduce Motion no emote moves: each becomes a 0.5 second tint that rises and falls over `DS.Move.fadeSeconds` (red for a failure, green with a still ring for a hop, white for a nod), so a failure still reads through its color (`CursorEmoteRules`, `CursorTintedGlyph`).
The emotes pair with a short Callout (the red failure chip, the speech Callout), so meaning never rests on motion alone.

The loop shows which video or image Clicky is looking at (`CursorLoopController`, `CursorLoopState`, `CursorLoopView`).
It is one closed 3 point line in the accent color over a 5.5 point under-stroke (black at 55 percent), 4 points outside the media, with about 1 point of wobble that depends only on the media's rect, and a short overshoot where it closes (`CursorLoopOutline`).
A white spark with an accent outline leaves the cursor, draws the line in 1 second, and keeps lapping every 2.2 seconds, or every 4 seconds once a status line, such as a scene build's or a pop-out's, has shown under the loop for 3 seconds (`CursorLoopTimeline`).
Reduce Motion fades the whole line in with no spark.
The loop is drawn in the cursor overlay window of the screen that holds the media, under the cursor, and ignores the mouse.
A loop next to a screen edge is pulled 3 points inside it, and media that covers 95 percent of a screen gets no loop (`mediaFillsTheWholeScreen`).

When the loop draws: push-to-talk release starts a read of the largest visible video or image in Chrome's front tab, only when Chrome is the front app, beside the router call (`ChromeVideoTab.largestVisibleMediaInFrontTabOrNone`).
The loop draws only when the request then becomes a look at that media: the router calls `answer_with_screen`, `build_new_scene`, or `pop_out` (`AppVoiceTool.handsOffToALookAtTheMedia`), or the keyword path starts a scene or pop-out (`respondByTheKeywordPath`), and the rect is in hand within 300 ms of that hand-off.
A turn that only runs tools, a plain keyword-path answer, a quiz question, and a request made while a scene is on screen never draw one.
The Debug rehearsal presses and releases push-to-talk before each step, so a rehearsal step draws the loop as a spoken request would.

When the loop clears (`CursorLoopEndReason`): Clicky staying idle for 0.3 seconds while a loop shows (a cancel or a failure, since the voice state blips idle right after a request is submitted, and a request that has not handed off yet may sit idle for its whole router call, so it is left alone), a new push-to-talk press, a scroll (the push-to-talk event tap also listens to scroll wheel events, `scrollWheelPublisher`), the answer's speech starting, or a scene starting to show (`LiveSceneOverlay.onASceneStartedToShow`, so a pop-out rises from the line).
While the loop shows, Clicky samples the front tab's window id, tab id, URL, and the page's own geometry of the window and the media as the loop starts and once a second after (`FrontTabSample`, two Apple Events and no accessibility read, since the main actor also draws the spark), and clears the loop when any of them differs, with 2 points of tolerance on the geometry.
The first sample catches a tab switch between the release and the hand-off.
The front app becoming something other than Chrome clears it too (`NSWorkspace.didActivateApplicationNotification`).
A status line spoken under the loop, such as "Sure, let me build that for you", does not clear it.
The line fades out over 0.35 seconds, and the dots take over if Clicky is still thinking.

**Agent sparks on the loop**: a background agent that works on the video in front laps its own spark around the loop's line (`CursorLoopAgentSparks.swift`, `CursorLoopAgentSparkView.swift`, and the `agentLine` of `CursorLoopState`).
An agent works on the video in front when it started from the tab that is still in front (`agentWorksOnTheVideoInFront`): a 3D model, experiment, or task agent compares its front tab URL, and a study guide or update agent compares its video key (`studyGuideVideoKey`).
`BackgroundAgent` keeps no tab, so `CompanionManager` tells the loop each agent's `AgentSourceTab` when it learns it (`theAgentStartedFromTheFrontTab`): the guide at its start, a scene agent after its front tab read, and a task agent after its harness front tab read.
A spark joins the line and draws only while no scene is on screen, no quiz question is open, the room art panel is closed, and Show Clicky is on (`agentSparksMayDraw(clickyCursorIsEnabled:aSceneIsOnScreen:aQuizQuestionIsOpen:theRoomArtPanelIsOpen:)`, which `CompanionManager` hands the controller as a closure), as a request's loop draws only then, and with Show Clicky off the overlay fades a second after the answer.
The controller reads the gate when an agent starts, when a release reads the media, and each time the line is drawn (`agentLineToDraw`).
A line already showing hides at the next redraw of the overlay after the gate turns false, which the cursor's movement and the voice state trigger all the time.
A controller with no gate wired draws no sparks.
An agent held back is remembered and joins at the first release that allows it.
The sparks go around the media the loop already holds: the line already showing, a request's loop, or the rect the push-to-talk release read for the same turn (`CursorLoopState.agentStarted`).
A request's loop and the agent line are separate, so a spark's agent keeps its line after the request's loop ends.
With no request loop showing, the agents draw a quiet track of the loop's outline (the accent at 45 percent, 2 points) for their sparks to lap, and under a request's loop the sparks ride its line.
An agent with no media in hand and no line already showing for its video, such as a seeded agent or one whose turn the next press already cleared, draws nothing at first.
The loop remembers each running agent's source tab (`CursorLoopState.runningAgents`), so an agent whose spark left the line, by a scroll or a tab switch, rejoins when a later release reads that video's media again (`runningAgentsRejoinTheLine`).
A finished agent never rejoins, since only its last lap is left.
Each agent's spark is its token's color, filled, with a white edge, and starts at its own place on the line (`AgentSparkTimeline.startingLaps`).
A lap takes 4 seconds at no progress and 1.2 seconds at full progress, and the pace follows `BackgroundAgent.progress(at:)`, so the spark speeds up as the work nears its end (`AgentSparkPhase`, which the view advances each frame, since a spark's place depends on the paces before it).
When the agent finishes, its spark runs on to the end of the lap it is on in exactly 1.2 seconds, a spark at the start of a lap getting a full lap, and the controller removes the agent after the same 1.2 seconds (`AgentSparkTimeline.secondsAFinishingLapTakes`), so the spark arrives as the line fades over 0.35 seconds once it is the last agent.
A failed or cancelled agent, or one the store dropped, leaves the line at once, since a last lap would say it finished.
While the learner has paused every agent (`BackgroundAgent.pausedAt`), its spark holds still, and it carries on from the same place after the resume.
Only a change of what is in front clears the line: a scroll, a front tab or media change, another front app, or a scene starting to show (`CursorLoopEndReason.endsTheAgentSparks`).
A push-to-talk press, Clicky going idle, and the answer's speech leave it, since an agent keeps working through them.
The line is watched by the same once a second front tab poll as a request's loop, so a poll runs for as long as an agent works.
`CursorLoopController.agentStatusesChanged` hears each change of `BackgroundAgentStore.agents` and forgets an agent when it ends or the store no longer lists it.
With Reduce Motion on there is no spark: each agent is a still arc along the line from its start, as long as its progress, redrawn once a second, and the longest arc draws first.

**Team theme**: Every theme is a `.clickytheme` package, a folder holding `theme.json` and optional images, read by one parser (`ClickyTheme.parse`, under Theme Studio below).
The app bundles eleven: Clicky blue (the default) and ten Haikyu!! schools, kept in `themes/` of the repository as `NN-<id>.clickytheme`, where the number sets the order the menu bar panel and the settings dial show.
Xcode's synchronized folders copy every file to the top of the app's Resources, where eleven `theme.json` files would collide, so the packages live outside `leanring-buddy/` and the "Copy Bundled Themes" build phase (`tools/copy-bundled-themes.sh`) copies them to `Contents/Resources/Themes`.
`BundledThemes.loadedFromTheApp` reads them once with `CustomThemeStore.loadBundledThemes`, sorted by folder name, and a bundled `theme.json` names its own `id`, such as `school-karasuno`.
A Debug build with no bundled Themes folder, such as `tools/swift-check.sh`'s test run, reads `themes/` next to its source file instead.
No theme's name, colors, cursor, or roster is written in Swift, and the views read only the theme's fields.
The menu bar panel's theme cards and the settings dial's Team fan pick a bundled theme or a saved one (`CompanionThemeSettings.theme`, a `ClickyTheme`), saved under the `companionTheme` key in UserDefaults as `bundled:<id>` or `custom:<id>` (`ThemeSettingValue`).
A setting an older version wrote reads as the theme it named (`ThemeSettingValue.migrated`): `clickyBlue` is `bundled:clicky-blue`, `school:aobaJohsai` is `bundled:school-aoba-johsai`, `custom:<id>` stays, and `random` is Clicky blue with Shuffle on.
A launch writes nothing back, since an older Clicky that shares these defaults, such as the everyday build next to a Debug one, would read today's form as an unknown theme. Picking a theme or flipping Shuffle writes today's form, and each writes both the theme and the Shuffle switch, so a Shuffle that `random` turned on survives the pick.
An unknown value reads as Clicky blue.
`CompanionThemeSettings`, owned by `CompanionManager`, is the one source of truth.
The Shuffle switch at the top of the panel's section (`shufflesAgentThemes`, saved under `shufflesAgentThemes`) gives each new agent the look of a theme picked at random from every installed theme, bundled and saved (`themesAgentsMayShuffleThrough`), and keeps the cursor of the chosen theme.
A school's cursor is a volleyball drawn as `shapes` in its file: a light ring, the ball's fill, its seams, and its outline, in the 24 point box, so it stays easy to follow in flight.
Its fill, outline, and seam colors are data: the team's accent color with a black outline and seams in the team's contrast color, except Aoba Johsai, which has white seams, and Inarizaki, whose ball is black with white lines.
The ball's `motion` turns it toward movement and draws three streaks, which fade in and out with the flight's speed share.
While a cursor that turns toward movement follows the mouse, it faces the mouse's movement and shows the streaks once the mouse moves faster than about 180 points a second, fading them within a fraction of a second after it stops (`CursorFollowMotion`).
Reduce Motion keeps the following ball still, and the triangle (`cursor.kind` `triangle`, Clicky blue's) keeps its pointer angle.
A theme's `agents` say how every agent that starts under it looks, picked when it starts by `newAgentLook` and kept for its whole life (`BackgroundAgent.tokenColor` and `face`), so changing the theme only affects agents that start afterward.
`agents.ring` is `perKind` (each kind's own color, then Clicky's fixed palette, Clicky blue's), `perAgent` (the theme's 3 to 6 colors in turn), or `accent` (every agent in the accent, as a school in its jersey color).
`agents.faces` is `triangle`, `picture` (a saved theme's picture.jpg), or `roster`, a list of players with a `name`, an optional `fullName` and `number`, and an optional `portrait` file in the theme's images folder.
A roster hands out its players in order (`rosterPlayerForANewAgent`, `order` `firstUnused`): the first player the agent list does not show, then, once every player is listed, the first one whose agent has ended, and past that it starts over from the roster's start. Only faces of the same theme count, so another theme's player of the same name never holds one back.
An agent token with a roster face shows the player's portrait inside a progress ring in the theme's accent (`AgentTokenDiscView`, `agentTokenRingColorChoice`), and the agent list and the ending chip show the same portrait (`RosterFaceView`).
Inarizaki's accent is the light `#F4F4F5`, so its ring reads on the dark track.
A theme's color never stands for a state, so any ring color is allowed, red included: a finished agent's token has a green check badge, a failed one a red cross badge on a gray face in a red ring, and one waiting for the learner's OK a "!" in place of its face and a notch in its ring at 12 o'clock (`BackgroundAgentStore.agentIDsWaitingForApproval`, which `CompanionManager` keeps in step with the approval desk). A failed agent's row in the list shows the same disc as beside the notch, with its red ring and cross badge, and a finished meter turns green.
The agent list's header shows the shown theme's name after a square in its accent, and a theme whose agents wear its accent borders the list in it.
The bundled rosters' portraits are `leanring-buddy/HaikyuPortraits/<portrait>.jpg`, gitignored because they are anime art in a public repo, and fetched by `python3 -I tools/fetch-haikyu-portraits.py`, which reads each player's `fullName` and `portrait` from the theme files, so CI and a fresh clone have none, and the views draw the theme's accent with the player's initials instead.
The Xcode target uses file system synchronized groups, so the fetched files bundle with no project change, at the top of Resources, which is a bundled roster's images folder (`ThemeOrigin.bundled(imagesFolderURL:)`).
Double-clicking a `.clickytheme` package in Finder opens Clicky, which `Info.plist` declares as the owner of the exported type `com.crodris.clicky-watch-along.theme` (a package, extension `clickytheme`).
`CompanionManager.installTheThemePackage(at:)` first reads and checks it with every rule an install applies (`CustomThemeStore.previewPackage`, which writes nothing), and a package that does not parse is refused with a log line naming the rule and the chip "Couldn't add that theme", with no card.
A package that passes asks "Add theme?" first (`ThemeInstallConfirmation`, `ThemeInstallCardPanelController`): a Tether Card near the top of the screen under the mouse with the theme's cursor on a dark disc, `Add "Moonlit" to Clicky?`, one line on how it moves (`themeInstallCardSummary`, the Theme Studio's tags without the sounds), and Cancel and Add theme buttons.
The card has no origin to Glide out of, since a double-click in Finder gives the app no position, so it grows in place with the Glide spring, or fades in with Reduce Motion on, and it fades out when answered.
Escape answers it with a no, after the agent list and the Theme Studio card in the Escape order, and several files opened together are asked about one at a time, in order.
Nothing is installed until the learner adds it, a file already showing or waiting is not asked about again, and the buttons ignore clicks for 0.4 seconds after the card appears and while it fades, so a second click of a fast double-click cannot answer the next card.
The install compares the package it reads with what the card showed (`CustomThemeStore.install(packageAt:expectedPreview:)`) and refuses one that changed since, so the learner installs what they agreed to.
Then `installTheConfirmedThemePackage` runs the install, which reads the package again, since it may have changed while the card showed: it copies `theme.json`, `picture.jpg` when the theme uses it, and the roster's portrait files into the Themes folder under a new id (`CustomThemeStore.install(packageAt:)`, which copies nothing else in the package), reading each only as a regular file with no symbolic link on its path and under its size cap (`plainFileData`: 256 KB for `theme.json`, 8 MB for the picture, 1 MB per portrait) and writing the bytes itself, so a package someone sent cannot make it read or write outside the package and the Themes folder, puts it on, plays its done sound, and shows "Theme added" with its name, or refuses a package that no longer parses with the same log line and chip.
A roster portrait the package lacks shows initials.
An install opens the package folder once (`OpenedPackageFolder`) and reads every file from that open folder, walking each file's path one name at a time with `openat`, opening each folder from the one before it with `O_NOFOLLOW` and the file from the last folder, then checking and reading the open file.
So the package folder is resolved once per read, every name below it is looked up from a folder already open with `O_NOFOLLOW`, and a hostile network volume that swaps the package folder, or a folder inside it, for a link cannot send the read outside the package.
An empty name, "." or ".." is refused even though the theme's rules refuse them first.

**Sharing themes as files**: dragging a theme card out of the menu bar panel makes a `.clickytheme` file where it lands, such as the desktop (`ThemeFileDrag`).
The card's `onDrag` hands over an `NSItemProvider` file promise for `<name>.clickytheme`, whose handler writes the package only when a drop asks for it, into `Clicky theme exports` under the temporary directory, which each drag empties first, and the system copies it to the drop's place.
The file name is the theme's name, trimmed, with a slash, a colon, a control character, a bidirectional control such as a right-to-left override, or a leading dot turned into a hyphen (`CustomThemeStore.exportedPackageFileName`).
A drag session takes the mouse, so the card never hears the mouse leave, and it drops its hover when the drag starts.
`CustomThemeStore.exportPackage(of:toPackageAt:)` writes a folder it creates, never one already there: `theme.json`, `picture.jpg` when the theme uses it, and the roster portraits a saved theme ships in its own folder, read with `plainFileData`.
A bundled theme never exports its roster portraits, since the schools' portraits are local wiki art and not Clicky's to give away, so its file names every player and the faces show initials, and a saved theme's portrait missing from its folder is left out the same way.
A Theme Studio idea is never exported, and a saved theme whose picture is gone is refused.
The exported file installs back through the "Add theme?" card like any other.
Finder previews a `.clickytheme` file with the `ClickyThemeQuickLook` extension, embedded in the app's `Contents/PlugIns` (a data-based Quick Look preview, `com.apple.quicklook.preview`, sandboxed, with the bundle ID `<app ID>.theme-preview`).
It reads the package with `CustomThemeStore.previewPackage`, the same read and `ClickyTheme.parse` rules an install applies, so a file Clicky would refuse gets Quick Look's plain fallback, and it returns `ThemeFilePreviewView` rendered at 2x as a PNG: the cursor still on a dark disc, the name, the "Add theme?" card's motion line (`themeInstallCardSummary`), the accent and contrast swatches, and the agents' colors and faces.
The preview is one still image, so it has no motion and needs no Reduce Motion form.
The extension compiles the theme's own source files from `leanring-buddy/` through the project's membership exception set for its target, not copies: `ClickyTheme.swift`, `CursorFollowMotion.swift`, `CustomThemeStore.swift`, `DesignSystem.swift`, `JSONValue.swift`, `SVGPathData.swift`, `TetherMaterials.swift`, `ThemeFilePreviewView.swift`, and `ThemeTags.swift`.
A change that makes one of them use an app-only type breaks the extension's build, so keep them free of the app's other files: `CustomThemeStore.standard()` is in CompanionThemeSettings.swift for that reason, and `JSONValue` and the theme tags have files of their own.
`tools/swift-check.sh` type-checks that set on its own, reading the file list from the project's exception set, with flags that mirror the Swift settings in `tools/swift-check/Package.swift` (change the Xcode targets, `Package.swift`, and those flags together), so a shared file that starts using an app-only type fails there and not only in CI's Xcode build.

**Room art**: "Show me how this would look in my room", "on my wall", or "could I see this shirt on me" (`asksToSeeArtInMyRoom`, a keyword branch ahead of the live scene's "show me" and of the router, like the Theme Studio's, or the router's `show_art_in_my_room` hand-off tool with the brain flag on) tries the item on Chrome's front tab in a live camera panel: wall art hangs on the learner's wall and furniture stands on their floor, each at its real size, and a top is drawn over the shirt they wear.
The phrase asks only when a room phrase ("in my room", "on my wall", "on the wall behind me", ...) comes right after a placing verb and the item ("show this", "put it", "can I see this", "hang that print") or after the item and a look ("how would this look", "what would that poster look like"), where up to two words from a closed list (good, nice, great, okay, ok, hanging, framed, up, there) may sit between the look and the room phrase, as in "would this look good in my room" and "how would this look hanging in my living room", and the item is "this", "it", or "that", or "this", "that", or "the" and one noun, so "how does the router in my house work", "what would look good in my room", and "I put a poster in my room" go to the router.
"On me" is one of the room phrases ("could I see this shirt on me", "how would this look on me"), and "try" with the item and "on" ending the request asks too ("can I try this on", "try on that shirt", with an optional "please" or "now" after it), so "what's on me" and "let me try this on the next video" go to the router.
Before the camera opens, Clicky checks the app secret ("Clicky needs its app secret for this. See the README's Worker setup."), that the front tab is an https page ("I can only read store pages served over https."), and that the page text could be read and is not empty ("I couldn't read that page."), since every Worker route the panel calls would refuse the request.
The page reader (`POST /try-it-item`) says what kind of item the page shows, `wallArt`, `furniture`, `clothing`, or `other`, with that kind's own data, and the app parses the reply at the boundary into `RoomArtPageReading` (`RoomArtWallArtReading` for wall art, which `RoomArtwork` hangs, `RoomArtFurnitureReading`, `RoomArtClothingReading`).
The request carries the learner's words (`learnerRequest`, cut to 500 UTF-16 units and left out when blank), so the reader knows which item they mean when the page shows several.
What the reading asks for is one pure rule (`roomArtReadingPlan`): wall art hangs on the wall, furniture whose page states a size stands on the floor, and a top (a T-shirt, shirt, sweater, hoodie, or jacket) whose photo shows it laid flat is tried on.
Wall art or furniture whose page states no size says "I couldn't find the size on that page.", so nothing is placed at a size the model guessed.
A dress or another garment says "I can try on shirts, sweaters, and jackets so far." (`onlyTopsSoFar`), a photo of the top on a model says "I can only try on a shirt from a flat photo of it, and this photo shows it on a model." (`shirtPhotoOnAModel`, since the model's body would come along with the cutout), a photo on a hanger or any other way says "I can only try on a shirt from a flat photo of it." (`shirtPhotoNotFlat`), and an item of kind `other`, such as a lamp, says "That doesn't look like wall art, furniture, or a shirt." (`notSomethingToTry`).
It reads the largest picture in the front tab and prefers the image file the page names over a screen capture, since a capture also holds a cookie banner or any other element over the photo and that hung on the wall with the art (`RoomArtPictureSource`): when the picture's `imageFileURL` is https (any visible image, cropped or not), it downloads the file with the animated page image's safe download (`PageImageAnimation.downloadedImageData`: https only for the first request and every redirect, no cookies or credentials, a 200 reply, at most 8 MB, 5 seconds in all, and never from localhost, a `.local`, `.internal`, `.lan`, or `.home.arpa` name, or a private, loopback, or link-local address, redirects included, `hostIsLocalOrPrivate`), refuses a side over 4096 before decoding, a long side under 160 pixels (a lazy-load placeholder), and any format but JPEG, PNG, WebP, GIF, or HEIC, scales it so its long side is at most 1280 (never up), and draws it over white as a JPEG, and it captures the screen the way the Theme Studio does only when there is no URL or the file cannot be used, with the log saying `Room art: used the page's image file` or `...so the picture is a screen capture`, unless the request was cancelled. It also reads the tab's URL, title (cut to 500 UTF-16 units), and text (cut to 30,000; the read first opens collapsed Measurements, Dimensions, Product details, and Specifications sections and `<details>` elements, waiting 600 ms when it opened any, since IKEA hides the height there, and the text adds only size-like key and value pairs from the page's JSON-LD and Next.js or Nuxt data, never whole data objects, which can hold tokens or emails, `roomArtPageTextScript`), cancels a scene still building, as the Theme Studio does (`cancelTheSceneStillBuilding`, which resumes a video Clicky paused for it), so the finished scene never plays over the panel, clears a scene or pop-out on screen, whose window would cover the panel, opens the panel (`RoomArtPreview.swift`), and starts the camera (`RoomCamera.swift`, 1280x720).
Escape or a push-to-talk press while the picture is downloaded or captured stops the request with nothing opened or said (Escape cancels the download, and a second room art request cancels the first request's download, and a cancelled request captures nothing and logs no fallback line), and a panel closed while macOS asks for camera access stops the camera once it starts.
A press that cancelled the request leaves the voice state to the newer request, so the cancelled request never sets it back to idle.
A ready 3D model or experiment does not rise while the panel is open (`backgroundSceneMayRiseNow`'s `theRoomArtPanelIsOpen`), and waits for "show the experiment" instead.
The page is read (`POST /try-it-item`) as soon as the panel opens, while the camera starts, with "Reading the page…" in the status line, and the video stays covered until the camera's first frame with a person mask (`theFirstFrameIsIn`), since the layer still holds the last session's frame.
The reading's kind decides what is measured, so a shirt never waits on a Depth Pro run (about 14 s), and furniture measures its wall on the floor's keyframe, which the depth service answers from the floor's cached depth in about 0.4 s.
Wall art is flattened out of its photo, and furniture is cut out of its photo with Vision's foreground mask, off the main actor (`furnitureCutout`, trimmed to its own pixels so its lowest pixel stands on the floor), which fails with "I couldn't cut the furniture out of its photo." (`furnitureNotCutOut`).
The flat cutout is furniture's placeholder: it shows as soon as the floor is measured, and its better looks replace it with a 0.5 s crossfade (see **Furniture's looks** below).
Once the item is ready and the camera has its first frame with a person mask (`waitingForTheCamera` until then), it picks the spot: the barest wall spot away from the person for wall art (`bareWallSpot`, in the upper middle of the frame), or the open floor with the least texture away from the person for furniture (`bareFloorSpot`, a window 30 percent of the frame wide and 18 percent high, `RoomArtBareFloorWindow`, its center from 62 to 86 percent of the way down, with a spot at the side edge costing up to twice its texture, since the plainest floor is often a far corner where the frame would cut the piece off), keeps that frame as the keyframe, and calls `POST /room-wall` or `POST /room-floor`, with "Measuring your wall…" or "Measuring your floor…".
While the surface is measured, Clicky's loop outlines the spot (`RoomArtLoop.swift`).
It is the cursor loop's own line and spark (`CursorLoopOutline` and `CursorLoopTimeline`, drawn through the shared `CursorLoopDrawing`) in the theme's accent, around the window `bareWallSpot` or `bareFloorSpot` scored (`RoomArtBareWallWindow` is 24 percent of the frame wide and 30 percent high), shifted inside the frame for a click near an edge.
It starts when the keyframe is taken, for the first measurement and for a click's, and the spark starts on the line, since the panel has no cursor to fly from.
Each frame the processor maps the window's four corners through the tracker's warp, the same warp that moves the art's corners, so the line follows the room as the camera turns (`RoomArtLoopGeometry`), and the panel bends the outline onto the mapped corners (`RoomArtLoopOutline`, which cuts each curve into 12 pieces and maps each point through the perspective transform from the window to its current corners).
The loop leaves with a 0.35 second fade when the item shows, the floor is not found, or the measurement fails, and at once when the panel closes (`RoomArtLoopState`, `RoomArtPhase` decides each change).
A failed move fades it where the surface last was, since the tracker goes back to the old keyframe.
With Reduce Motion on, the whole line fades in with no spark.
The art's four corners come from the wall plane and its size in meters, and the art is flattened out of the product photo from the corners Claude gave.
Furniture's four corners come from the floor plane (`FloorPlane.keyframeCorners`): the front face of its box (`RoomFurniturePlacement`) standing on the anchor and facing the camera, at the page's height, and as wide as the cutout's own shape at that height (`RoomFurniture.drawnWidthMeters`), since a product photo is often a three quarter view that showing at the page's width would squash.
Each frame, `RoomTracker.swift` aligns the keyframe to the new frame with ECC (enhanced correlation coefficient) on 320x180 grayscale, solving only a turn of the laptop (rotation and shift, 3 numbers), masked to leave out the person in both frames, warm-started from the last frame, and always against the same keyframe, so the art does not drift.
A full 8-number homography and corner trackers both failed on a plain wall with the learner's body covering most of the texture, and the 3-number model held at a correlation of 0.78 to 1.0.
After 3 lost frames, Vision's translational registration restarts ECC.
Person segmentation (`VNGeneratePersonSegmentationRequest`) runs on its own queue and keeps the learner in front of the item.
Core Image draws each frame (the item warped to its corners, the room's light and color around the spot, about 0.7 camera pixels of blur, a contact shadow, and the person on top) into a `CAMetalLayer`, at 25 to 30 fps on a recorded pan.
The art's shadow sits just below it on the wall, and the furniture's is a soft dark ellipse across its base (`furnitureContactShadow`), whose lower half shows on the floor in front of it.
A click on the video moves the item there with a new keyframe and measurement (the video shows the crosshair for wall art and furniture, and the arrow for a shirt, which a click does not move), and a click while one runs is ignored.
A click's failed measurement puts the item back where it stood, tracked against its old keyframe (`putBackTheItemFromBeforeTheMove`), keeps the camera running, and shows the failure in the status line without speaking. Only the first measurement's failure ends the panel, except a floor that was not found.
A first floor measurement that finds no floor (a `/room-floor` 422, or a floor the piece cannot stand on) keeps the camera running and says "I couldn't find your floor. Tilt the screen down so the floor shows, then click the floor." once (`waitingForAFloorClick`, with the same advice in the status line), and a click on the video measures the floor at the click, where a second miss only shows in the status line.
When the tracker loses the room long enough to hide the item, the status line says "Lost the wall. Turn back, or click the wall to place it again." or "Lost the floor. Turn back, or click the floor to place it again." until it finds the room again.
A flat lay top needs no depth: the processor turns on the shirt try-on (`startTheShirtTryOn`) while the garment is cut out of its photo off the main actor (`ShirtGarment.make`), and each frame `ShirtPoseTracker` finds the torso, `ShirtFraming.assess` judges it, and the framing's kind goes through `ShirtFramingDebouncer`, which settles a kind only once it has held for 0.5 seconds, since `assess` has no hysteresis.
Each settled kind reaches the controller, which shows its coaching in the status line ("I can't see you yet. Step in front of the camera." or "Stand back so I can see your shoulders and chest.") and says the first one once while the shirt is still on its way, not as a failure, and after that only the status line follows the learner.
The loop traces the torso where the shirt will land (`RoomArtLoopGeometry.torsoLoop`, the line around the torso's box bent onto `ShirtTryOnRenderer.bodyTorsoCorners`) from the start of the shirt until it first shows.
Once the garment is ready and the framing has settled on ready, `ShirtTryOnRenderer.composite` draws the shirt on every frame with a torso, in place of the item, with the person mask the segmentation already made, and Clicky says "Here it is on you." once.
A garment that cannot be cut out says "I couldn't lift the shirt out of its photo." (`garmentNotCutOut`).
`RoomArtPhase` is the state machine: `closed`, `readingThePage`, then for wall art and furniture `waitingForTheCamera`, `measuring`, `showing` with an optional `moveFailure`, `moving`, and for furniture `waitingForAFloorClick`, and for a top `preparingTheShirt`, `waitingToSeeTheLearner`, and `showingTheShirt`, and `failed`.
Every transition is a pure function, and each measurement carries a `RoomArtMeasurementID`, so a reply for an earlier measurement or a closed panel changes nothing, and the page reading, the cutout, and the garment check the opening number, so a reply after a close changes nothing either.
Clicky says "Here it is at 16 by 20 inches." once the art shows, or "Here it is, 31.5 inches wide and 29.5 tall." once the furniture does, with the size built from the page's centimeters in inches rounded to the nearest half inch (`roomArtSizeInInches`, `roomFurnitureSizeInWords`), never from the page's free-text label, which only the log shows.
That line and every failure line wait, as the Theme Studio's lines do, while push-to-talk is held, audio plays, or Clicky thinks about a request that is not the room art's own opening, and a line is dropped when the learner pressed push-to-talk after asking (`roomArtLineStep`), so the reply 14 seconds later never cuts off a newer answer.
Every close of the panel, Escape, the Close button, and a new request, runs the controller's `endTheSpokenLine`, which `stopTheRoomArtSpeech` answers the way the Theme Studio's close does: it cancels a line still waiting, stops a line already playing by its `SpokenLineToken` with `stopPlayback(ifTheCurrentLineIs:)`, and sets `.responding` back to idle only when that stop reached the room art's own line (`voiceStateAfterAPanelStoppedItsLine`), so an unrelated answer keeps playing.
The failures are: no app secret or a 401 from any route (the app secret line), a page that is not https, page text that could not be read or a failed `/try-it-item` (pageNotRead), a page that states no size ("I couldn't find the size on that page."), a garment that is not a top or not laid flat, an item that is not wall art, furniture, or clothing, a cutout Vision could not make, no camera or no camera access, a `/room-wall` or `/room-floor` 502 or 503 ("I couldn't measure your room. Is the depth service running?"), a 422 or a wall that cannot place the art ("Try pointing the camera at a bare wall."), a floor that was not found (the tilt and click line above), and a request to any route that got no answer, such as a Worker that is not running or a timeout (a `URLError`, "I couldn't reach Clicky's Worker.").
Every failure line is spoken as a failure, so the cursor shakes as Clicky says it (`speakLiveSceneLine`'s `isAFailure`), and "Here it is ..." and the shirt's coaching are not.
A failure keeps the panel with its line until the learner closes it.

**Furniture's looks**: a flat cutout of the product photo looked like a sticker (the photo's viewpoint is wrong for the camera, nothing grounds it, and the light does not match), so it is only the placeholder, and three better looks follow, best first.
1. The store's own 3D model, rendered for real. While the cutout is made and the floor measured, `RoomFurnitureModelLoading.findTheStoreModel` runs one page script in the front tab (`roomFurnitureModelDiscoveryScript` in `RoomFurnitureDiscovery.swift`), which lists model files from https URLs ending in .glb, .gltf, or .usdz in the HTML and in the resources the page loaded, `model-viewer` `src` and `ios-src`, `a[rel=ar]` links, JSON-LD `3DModel` nodes, and a Shopify store's `/products/<handle>.js` media, plus extensionless files the product data names as a model.
   Only a file on the page's own registrable domain (`registrableDomain(ofHost:)`, so web-api.ikea.com for www.ikea.com) or one the page itself loaded is downloaded (`candidatesThePageMayOffer`), with the page image's safe download at a 40 MB cap (`PageImageAnimation.downloadedImageData`'s `largestByteCount`), at most 4 files, 15 s each.
   A GLB is tried first, since `GLBLoader.swift` reads it with every read bounds-checked and every count capped, then a USDZ, which SceneKit's own parser reads with consistency checks on, then an extensionless file, which counts only when its bytes are GLB or a ZIP holding a .usdc or .usda (`RoomFurnitureModelFileKind.sniff`). A Draco GLB and a .gltf are skipped, since there is no Draco decoder and a .gltf needs other files.
   `GLBLoader` reads an uncompressed glTF 2.0 binary: meshes with positions, normals (made from the triangles when missing), texture coordinates, and indices, node transforms, and metallic-roughness materials with embedded PNG or JPEG textures. It refuses anything else, and caps nodes (100,000), vertices (3,000,000), indices, materials (256), primitives (4,096), images (32), each image's side (4096), and the pixels its images decode and derive (64 million, with each derived roughness, metalness, or alpha-flattened image made once per image), so a hostile file cannot exhaust memory. A loaded USDZ is held to the same node and vertex caps (`checkTheCounts`).
2. A generated model (`RoomFurnitureGenerationClient.generateAModel`, `POST /try-it-model`), when the page offers none and the Worker has a fal key.
3. The painted composite (`paintTheItemIntoTheKeyframe`, `POST /room-furniture-composite`), with the mask drawn from the projected corners of the item's 3D box (`furnitureCompositeMaskPNG`). Its layer is the region the client pasted (`RoomFurniturePaintedComposite`), kept only when the painted item is no shorter than 85 percent of the box's projected front face and no taller than 115 percent of its whole projected outline, counting rows where at least 6 pixels, or 2 percent of the pasted region, changed strongly (a share alone left out thin legs), and never a row below the box's lowest corner, where the pasted region holds only the painted shadow and the floor the service drew again (counting them measured a Blu Dot chair painted at 103 percent of its box as 121 percent). The log names the painted rows, the box outline's, and the front face's. One run drew a LACK side table 23 percent taller than its outline, and the outline alone overstated chairs: a painted Target chair as tall as the exact render of the store's model measured 79 percent of it.
A model is rendered first, in under a second, and the composite is then asked for and replaces the render when it passes the height check, since on the same frame and item it looked more real (a real contact shadow and the room's own light). A Worker that answers 503 for the composite is not asked again for that panel. A model that does not render goes straight to the composite.
Each look is made for one keyframe (`RoomArtKeyframe.keyframeNumber`), and the frame processor drops a look made for a keyframe a click has replaced (`showTheFurnitureLayers`). A click renders the model again for its new keyframe and asks for a new composite.
The render (`RoomFurnitureRenderer.swift`) runs once per placement on its own queue with an offscreen `SCNRenderer`, and returns full-frame layers in keyframe pixels that the frame processor moves with the tracker's warp, as an affine map from the tracked frame corners (`RoomFurnitureComposition`), since mapping the render through the cutout's quad would apply perspective twice.
- The camera is Depth Pro's pinhole: a custom projection from the focal length and principal point (`roomFurnitureProjection`), with the camera space turned from y down and z forward to SceneKit's y up and z toward the viewer (`roomFurnitureCameraToSceneKit`).
- The model's units and up axis are never trusted (`RoomFurnitureModelFit`): the axis whose share of the model best fits the page's shape is up (glTF's y unless a z up file fits clearly better), the model is scaled uniformly to the page's height with its pivot at its bottom middle, and it turns 90 degrees about up when its width and depth come out swapped by more than 15 percent. Its front faces the camera on a measured floor, as the cutout does, and its front bottom edge stands on the anchor.
- The light is the blurred keyframe as a neutral image-based environment and a broad key light from above and the frame's brighter side, with HDR off. A white card the size of the item's front, rendered in the same light, sets half the wall's tint (`shareOfTheWallsTint`, since a painted wall's own color would tint a warm gray sofa purple), and the render's brightest part (its 95th percentile) is held to 0.92 of the wall's lit white, sampled from the brighter half of a band of wall above the box, never the floor.
- The shadow is a top-down silhouette of the model, blurred wide and laid on the floor plane under the footprint, plus the model's lowest 3.5 cm blurred tight and darker, as model-viewer does, since an offscreen renderer's shadow maps are unreliable. It is drawn over the floor before the model.
- The model renders at twice the keyframe's size and is scaled down with Lanczos for clean edges, then gets 0.7 camera pixels of blur, and each frame adds the camera's own grain, measured in the keyframe's flattest areas, moved every frame.
- The learner stays in front of the furniture, unless its base is lower in the frame than the person mask's lowest row (`roomFurnitureDrawsOverThePerson`, `lowestPersonRowShare`), when the piece stands nearer the camera than their feet and draws over them.
The piece does not stand at the spot the floor was measured at, since the bare floor search often picks a dark, plain corner where the frame cuts the piece off. Once the floor is measured, any other floor point is that pixel's ray on the same plane (`FloorPlane.moved(toAnchorPixel:)`), so `roomFurnitureSpotWhereTheWholePieceFits` picks, with no new measurement, the spot whose whole box (footprint and full height, from the page's size) fits inside the frame with 4 percent to spare, nearest the lower middle of the frame and on bare floor (the keyframe's texture map, `RoomArtKeyframe.floorTexture`), and never more than 10 cm farther back than the measured spot, since the plane goes on forever while the room ends at a wall. When no spot shows the whole piece, it stands where most of it shows, and Clicky says "Step back so I can show the whole piece." once, after "Here it is". A click keeps the spot the learner clicked.
The floor is checked against the wall on the same keyframe (`measureTheFurnituresFloor`): the wall is measured at the keyframe's bare wall spot (`RoomArtKeyframe.wallSpotAnchor`), and a floor whose normal is more than 20 degrees from the wall's up is a desk or a bed and not the floor.
With no floor (a laptop camera often shows only the wall behind the learner), a floor is inferred from the wall (`FloorPlane.inferred`): level with the wall's up, 1.05 m below the camera, with the piece square to the wall and its back on it, used only when at least a third of its box is in the frame (`shareOfTheBoxInsideTheFrame`). Otherwise the panel says to tilt the screen down and click the floor, as before.
The log says `Room furniture: the page names N model files, ...`, `found the store's own model at <host>`, `rendered in N ms, ... white gains ...`, `showing the storeModel from <host>`, and `showing the painted composite` or why a look was skipped.

The depth service (`tools/depth-service/`) is a local Python server with Apple's Depth Pro, a stand-in for a hosted GPU: one Depth Pro run at a time, RANSAC on a 4,000 point subset, and an SVD without full matrices (the default built a 14 GB matrix).
It refuses a request with an `Origin` header (403) and a body that is not `application/json` (415), like `VoiceToolDebugServer`, so a web page in a browser on this Mac cannot spend Depth Pro runs, and it answers 422 for a plane with a NaN or infinite number instead of writing NaN.
It keeps the last two depth maps by a SHA-256 of the JPEG and the focal hint, so furniture's wall measurement on its floor's keyframe costs no second Depth Pro run, and each reply says `depthWasCached`.
A Debug build launched with `--room-camera-file <path>` loops that video instead of the camera, so verification needs no camera.
Two generation services let the furniture try-on work for any furniture page with a photo and a size, not only for the few pages that publish a 3D model, and `RoomFurnitureGenerationClient` (`RoomFurnitureGeneration.swift`) is the app's side of both.
`paintTheItemIntoTheKeyframe` sends the keyframe, the product cut out on white, and a mask to `POST /room-furniture-composite`, and returns the keyframe with the drawn piece pasted in, at the keyframe's size.
`furnitureCompositeMaskPNG` draws that mask: the convex hull of the item's projected box corners, grown by a shadow margin with round corners, white on black in 8-bit gray.
The edit re-renders the whole frame, so pixels away from the piece come back slightly changed (a window's view, the light on a wall).
The client pastes the reply only inside the mask grown by 24 pixels and feathered over 4 (`furnitureCompositePasted`), since on the live check a chair's arm and a table's legs came out up to about 20 pixels past the mask, and everything past that stays byte for byte as filmed.
