The edit also re-exposes the frame (on the live check a wall came back 12 to 17 levels brighter), which showed as a lighter patch around the piece.
So before the paste, the reply's tone is matched to the keyframe with a gain and an offset per channel, fitted on a ring of room 96 pixels wide just outside the paste (`toneFitsMatchingTheReplyToTheKeyframe`).
A fit with a gain outside 0.7 to 1.4, an offset past 40 levels, or fewer than 500 ring pixels leaves the reply as it came, since the ring then held something other than the same room.
`generateAModel` sends the product photo to `POST /try-it-model` and returns the GLB, and keeps each GLB in `~/Library/Caches/<bundle id>/TryItModels/<SHA-256 of the photo and the title>.glb`, so the same product is never paid for twice.
A cached file that is not a GLB is made again, and a cache that cannot be written only costs a new model next time.
Both calls throw `RoomFurnitureGenerationError`: `serviceIsNotSetUp` for a 503 (no `FAL_KEY`), so the panel can say the feature is not set up instead of that it failed, `workerReturned` with the status for any other reply that is not 200, `replyCouldNotBeRead`, and `inputCouldNotBeRead` for a keyframe or mask that is not an image, before any request.

**Theme Studio**: "Hey Clicky, change my theme" opens a card next to the cursor with three theme ideas, and the learner picks, refines, and saves one by voice or click (`docs/plans/theme-studio.md`).
Each idea is a `ClickyTheme` (`ClickyTheme.swift`): a name, an accent and a contrast color, a cursor, its motion, its agents (a ring of `perAgent` colors or the `accent`, and `triangle` or `picture` faces), and three sounds.
The cursor (`CustomCursorDrawing`) is `triangle` (Clicky's own, which only a theme file uses), `shapes`, 1 to 6 SVG paths drawn in the 24 point box a school's volleyball uses (`SVGPathData`, `SVGPathShape`), or `picture`, the picture the learner pointed at, with its long side at 32 points and its short side at least 12 points (a banner shaped picture is cropped to its center), as the whole image with 4 point rounded corners or a round crop of its center, a 1.5 point outline, and an optional tint of at most 0.6 strength drawn over it.
`ClickyTheme.parse(themeJSON:id:origin:pictureIsAvailable:)` is the one boundary.
It reads a `theme.json` of version 2 (`version: 2`, with `agents`), the shape the Worker's ideas, a saved theme, and a bundled one share (`themeJSON` writes it), and version 1, the Theme Studio's files before theme files, with `agentColors` (a `perAgent` ring) and `usesPicture` (picture faces), and refuses any other version.
It applies the rules of the Worker's `checkThemeIdeas` again, since a saved file can be edited by hand: a name of 1 to 24 characters, `#RRGGBB` colors, an accent and per-agent colors with a WCAG contrast of at least 3 against the overlay surface `#101211`, no per-agent color that looks red or green (hue 340 to 20 or 95 to 165 with HSL saturation over 0.35 and HSL lightness of 0.75 or less, since red means failed and green means done, while a light pastel such as a light pink passes), no `colors` list for an `accent` or `perKind` ring, path commands `M L H V C S Q T Z` only, separated by space, tab, carriage return, line feed, form feed, or comma, with every written coordinate from -2 to 26 and at most 600 characters, a stroke width up to 3 and a fill or a stroke, and a stroke width above 0 when there is no fill, 0 to 3 trail streaks, a thinking motion of none, spin, pulse, or wobble, a listening motion of none or pulse, optional `motion.springs` moved into the same limits as the Worker's (see "Theme motion" below), sounds from the 14 macOS system sounds, a roster of 1 to 60 players with different names whose portraits are paths below the theme's folder (`isAPortraitPathInsideTheTheme`, so "../x.jpg" is refused), and a picture cursor or picture faces only when a picture exists.
The parsed theme keeps where it came from (`ThemeOrigin`: `bundled`, `saved`, or `idea`), which decides its setting's prefix and its images folder, and `usesPicture` is true when the cursor or the faces are the picture.
In a version 1 file, a picture cursor gives the agents picture faces whether or not the idea said so.
Inside the app the type is trusted, so no view checks a color or a path again.
`CustomThemeStore` saves each theme to `~/Library/Application Support/Clicky/Themes/<id>/theme.json`, with `picture.jpg` beside it when the theme uses the picture, and skips a folder that does not parse with a log line.
A save refuses an id that is not a theme id (`isAValidThemeID`, so "../x" throws before any folder is made), creates the theme's folder with no intermediate folders, so an existing folder throws before any write, and the cleanup after a failed write removes only a folder that save created.
The id is the name as a lowercase slug and 4 hex digits, and it is the folder's name.
A rename (`rename(themeID:to:)`) rewrites `theme.json` with the new name, trimmed and 1 to 24 characters (`ClickyTheme.validName(from:)`), in version 2, so the id and the folder stay. It puts back the file's modification date after the write, since that date is the theme's saved date, and a rename is not a save, so the dial's Team fan keeps its order.
A delete (`delete(themeID:)`) removes the theme's folder.
Both start with the `isAValidThemeID` check, so an empty id or "../x" throws before any file call, and both refuse an id with no folder (`noThemeHasThatID`).
A saved theme's setting is `custom:<id>`, and an id no saved theme has reads as Clicky blue.
The settings dial's Team fan lists the bundled themes, then the three most recently saved custom themes, newest first (`SettingsDialFans`).
`CustomThemeStore.loadSavedThemes()` returns each theme with its saved date (`SavedCustomTheme`), the modification date of its `theme.json`, which the fan sorts by.
The menu bar panel draws every theme as a card (`ThemeCard` in `ThemeCardView.swift`, see **Menu bar panel**), bundled and saved alike: the home shows up to five, and its All themes page shows the bundled themes in their order, then the saved ones by name.
A saved theme's card shows a pencil and a trash can on hover or keyboard focus, each with the pointing hand and a hover state, and offers Rename and Delete on a right click and as VoiceOver actions.
The pencil turns the name into a text field that takes focus: Return saves, Escape or a click away cancels, and a refused name keeps the field open with the phrase "1 to 24 characters". Escape there does nothing else: `closeLiveSceneOnEscape` returns while a text field in Clicky's own window is being edited, so it does not also close the studio card or a scene.
The trash can asks once: the first click shows a red "Delete" button over the bottom of the card for 3 seconds, and a second click deletes.
A card keeps its size in every state, so a problem is a short red phrase over the bottom of the card.
`CompanionThemeSettings` holds the saved `theme`, the studio's `previewTheme` and `previewPicture`, and `shownTheme`, which every view that draws the theme reads (the overlay, the agent list, the pop-out's quiz label, and the sound calls).
A preview shows everywhere, and only the saved theme is written to UserDefaults. `saveAndApply` saves the idea, makes it the theme, and ends the preview.
`renameSavedTheme(themeID:to:)` and `deleteSavedTheme(themeID:)` call the store and then reload `savedCustomThemes`.
Renaming the theme in use keeps it in use under the new name, and deleting it sets the theme to Clicky blue.
A delete also drops the theme's picture from `CustomThemePictureCache`.
An agent that is running keeps the color it was given, as agents keep theirs for life, and a picture face it wore falls back to the triangle.
What a theme's file sets, for a bundled theme and a saved one alike:
- The cursor is `CustomCursorView` for every theme, the triangle included, with the glow the triangle has. `whileThinking` runs only while Clicky thinks (spin is one turn every 1.2 seconds, pulse scales 1 to 1.12 and back every 0.9 seconds, wobble turns plus and minus 12 degrees every 0.8 seconds), `whileListening` pulse scales with the microphone from 1 to at most 1.2, and Reduce Motion turns every motion and the streaks off (`CustomCursorMotionRules`, pure functions of the voice state and the clock, with a clock only while a thinking motion runs).
  A cursor with `turnsTowardMovement` faces the mouse's movement as a school's volleyball does, and one without it stays upright (`CursorFacing`), while its trail streaks, `CursorFlightStreaks` drawn `trailStreaks` times, turn to trail behind the movement. The triangle keeps its pointer angle whatever its motion says.
- The ring, loop, bubble, chips, and dial read `cursorColor`, so they follow the accent.
- Under a `perAgent` ring each new agent takes the first of the theme's agent colors that no other running agent or waiting result has, then cycles through them (`AgentPaletteColor.custom`, `agentPaletteColorToAssign(forCustomThemeAgentColorHexes:colorsInUse:)`), under an `accent` ring the accent, and keeps it for its whole life.
  A saved theme with picture faces gives the agent the picture as its round face where a school shows a portrait (`AgentFace.themePicture`, `ThemePictureFaceView`) on the token, in the agent list row, and on the ending chip. An idea's agents keep the triangle, since an idea has no picture.jpg yet.
  Agents read the saved theme and never the studio's preview, so an agent started while the learner only tries an idea does not keep it.
- The Pop, Glide, and Point moves play the shown theme's springs (`ClickyTheme.moveSprings`, see "Theme motion" below).
- The three sounds go through the shown theme (`ClickyTheme.sounds`): done (an agent finishing), correct (a right quiz answer or a challenge hit), and needs your OK. The bundled themes keep Clicky's "Tink", "Glass", and "Pop".
The studio is the state machine `ThemeStudioState` (`ThemeStudio.swift`): `closed`, `designing(previousIdeas:)` while a Worker call runs, and `showingIdeas` with 1 to 3 ideas and one highlighted.
Its transitions are pure functions, each tested: asking for ideas, ideas arrived (only while designing, so a reply for a closed card is dropped), the design failed (back to the previous ideas, or closed when there were none), pick, save, and close.
`asksToDesignATheme` is a keyword branch ahead of the router, like "let me try it": the word "theme" with a request word within the four words before it, a design word (change, make, design, create, customize, customise, redo, switch) or an asking word (give, want, need, get, try, or "like" after "I'd" or "would", so "this sounds like a pirate theme" and "I like your theme" ask nothing), and an owner word: my, your, or Clicky's directly before "theme", as in "change my theme to something cozy", or a form that also fits a theme for something else: my, your, or Clicky's one word before "theme", "a" one word before it, as in "create a cozy theme" or "I want a new theme", or, with a design word, "a" directly before it, as in "create a theme". Those forms count only when "theme" ends the request or is followed by please, like, that, with, based, or, when the words point at the picture, from, so "I need a theme for my essay", "I need a good theme for my essay", "I want a new theme song", and "a theme from this poem" are questions, and so is "design a theme for night", by the same rule. "The" never counts as an owner, a possessive "'s" comes off a word, so "my theme's colors" counts, and "theme" followed by "of" never counts, so "how does the author change the theme here", "what's the new theme in chapter two", and "I need a theme of loss for my essay" are questions for the router. "New theme" or "a new theme" alone, with only hey, okay, Clicky, or please around it, is a request too. "From the picture" is detected when the words also say this picture, this image, this photo, or match this.
It does not fire while a quiz question is open, since the router reads those, and with the brain flag on the router picks `design_theme` itself.
While the studio is open no keyword branch runs ahead of the router at all, "let me try it" and "animate this" included, since the card holds every spoken word (`keywordBranchAheadOfTheRouter`).
A failed `/voice-command` request while the studio is open says "Sorry, I couldn't do that one." and does not fall back to the keyword path, which would answer "save it" as a question about the screen (`routerFailureFallback`).
`ThemeStudioController` runs it: it opens the card at once in the designing state with shimmering placeholders and nothing spoken, calls `POST /theme-ideas` through an injected function, puts the highlighted idea on the real cursor as the preview, speaks the reply's `spokenLine`, and on a failure says "Sorry, I couldn't design that one." and goes back to the previous ideas or closes.
A new design cancels one still running, and the old ideas stay dimmed on the card meanwhile.
Starting a design cancels a scene still building the way "let me try it" does, so the router is not left off and a video Clicky paused for the scene plays again.
The studio's spoken lines wait until no audio is playing, push-to-talk is not held, and Clicky is not thinking about a request, all checked together in one loop (`themeStudioLineMayStart`), so a line never plays over the learner or over the request that followed it.
The studio's own design holds the thinking state while it reads a picture, so that state does not hold a line back while a design runs, and the "no picture" line still plays before the ideas' line.
That excuse ends at the first push-to-talk press after the design started, since a thinking state after a press is the learner's own request: `CompanionManager` counts presses, records the count when each design starts, and passes both counts to `themeStudioLineMayStart`.
A request from the picture draws the loop around the largest picture in Chrome's front tab and captures it the way the image pop-out does, and keeps the thinking state up until the ideas arrive. With no picture to capture, Clicky says "I don't see a picture in Chrome's front tab, so I'll go from your words." and designs from the words.
The card keeps the picture, so a refinement and a save keep it.
With Show Clicky off, transient cursor mode fades the overlay a second after the last interaction, so the preview then shows on the card's tiles only.
While the studio is open and no quiz question is open, the router takes every command, even while a scene it started is still building (`voiceCommandRouterTakesTheTranscript`, `themeStudioIsOpen`).
The quiz phrases such as "stop the quiz" and the keyword branches such as "animate this" step aside for it (`videoQuizPhrasesAreMatchedAheadOfTheRouter`, `keywordBranchAheadOfTheRouter`).
Only "put it back" keeps its phrase match ahead of the router.
With a quiz question open, nothing changes: the answer goes to the judge and "stop the quiz" keeps its phrase match.
The quiz phrases ("can we stop", "quiz me on this video") are the router's too, unless a quiz question is open (`videoQuizPhrasesAreMatchedAheadOfTheRouter`), since the router still offers `end_quiz` while a quiz runs.
The router is offered `design_theme` (`request`, optional `basedOnIdea` from 1 to 3, optional `fromPicture`: true captures the picture again, false drops the card's picture, and a missing input keeps it) whenever no quiz question is open, `pick_theme_idea` (`number`) and `save_theme` only while ideas show, and `close_theme_studio` only while the card is open.
`design_theme` hands off, since the card and the spoken line say the result, and it shows no chip. The others show "Trying idea 2", "Theme saved", and "Kept your old theme".
The router's scene state carries `themeStudio: { ideas: ["Ember", "Moonlit", "Forest"], highlighted: 2, designing: false }`, and the debug tool server serves the four tools from the router's own context, so `design_theme` is callable there with its `request`.
Saving writes the theme and its picture, sets the theme, closes the card, stops the line Clicky was saying about the ideas, plays the theme's done sound once, and shows the chip.
A click on Save shows the same "Theme saved" chip, or "Couldn't save the theme" when the write fails.
Escape closes the studio first, after the dial and the agent list and ahead of any scene behavior, and keeps the old theme. A push-to-talk press does not close it.
Escape, the Close button, `close_theme_studio`, and a save all stop the line Clicky is saying about the ideas, or one still waiting to be said, and only that line: an answer playing when the card closes keeps playing.
The studio keeps the playback token of its own line (`SpokenLineToken`, which `ElevenLabsTTSClient.speakText` returns once the line's audio starts) and stops playback only through `stopPlayback(ifTheCurrentLineIs:)`, which does nothing once another line has replaced it or it has ended, and the card's close resets the responding state only when that stop reached the line.
A line still loading is ended by cancelling the studio's speech task, since a token exists only once audio plays.
The card (`ThemeStudioPanel.swift`) is a Tether Card (`tetherCard()`) in a borderless nonactivating panel that never becomes key, one level below the cursor overlay windows so the previewed cursor draws over it (the carrier's moves draw above them, on their own panel), placed at the first corner 96 points around the mouse that does not cover the pointer, and placed again by the same rule each time it resizes (`keepThePanelOnScreenAsItResizes`, which the agent list uses too), so a card that grows stays on the screen, reusing the agent list's panel class, hover-and-pointer view, and placement functions.
It shows the title with "Trying 1 of 3", a "From your picture" row with the picture small beside it when the ideas came from a picture, three tiles (the idea's cursor at 1.6 times its size, its name, its accent and agent colors as swatches, and a small agent token in its first agent color with the real disc's dark edge and shadow, and the picture as its face when the theme uses it), the highlighted idea's tags in words, the hint "Say one, two, or three · or what to change · say save it", and Save and Close buttons.
The highlighted tile's cursor runs its real thinking motion, and its streaks pulse the way they do when the cursor moves, so "spins while thinking" and "2 trail streaks" are seen and not only read, and Reduce Motion holds them still (`CustomCursorView` does that itself).
The highlighted tile has a speaker button that plays the idea's done, correct, and needs-your-OK sounds in a row, 0.7 seconds apart (`ThemeStudioController.playTheHighlightedIdeasSounds`, `themeSoundPreviewPlan`), and a pick, a new design, a save, and a close end a preview still playing.
A failed design that leaves the old ideas on the card says so in a red Callout with a cross on the card (`showsTheDesignFailedNotice`, cleared by the next design, pick, or close), and then the chip "Couldn't design that one" is left out, since it would say the same thing twice.
A first design that fails closes the card as before, with the chip.
The card Glides out of the cursor glyph on the carrier with the tether line, and closes back into it at 0.7x, with the card drawn from its last open state (`lastOpenState`) while it closes.
Ideas land 90 ms apart with a Pop each time they arrive (`ideasArrivalCount` is the tiles' identity), so a refinement lands again over the dimmed ideas.
Save stamps the saved tile with a check, flies that idea's cursor from the tile into the real cursor on the carrier (`CarrierFlightPlan.send`), and then closes the card into the cursor (`ThemeStudioCardClosing.saved`); every other close is `dismissed` and closes into the cursor at once.
With Reduce Motion on, the card crossfades in place, no cursor flies, and the stamp only fades.
The window is the Card grown by the agent list's transparent margin (`AgentListWindowMargins`), where the Card's shadow draws.
The highlighted tile is outlined in its idea's accent, and Save is filled with it, with dark or white letters by contrast.
A tile that is not highlighted sits at 0.92 of its size and springs up with its own idea's Pop when picked, and the highlighted idea's tags add "bouncy motion", "calm motion", "snappy motion", or "custom motion" when its springs differ from Clicky's own (`themeMotionFeel`). A tile click picks an idea, and every clickable element shows the pointing hand and a hover state, except a tile or Save while the ideas are dimmed.

**Instant replay**: after Clicky narrates a frame pop-out, "show me again" or the Replay button plays the narration's pointing stops again, silently, with no model call (`InstantReplay.swift`, `InstantReplayPanel.swift`).
Each sentence whose part the cursor pointed at records a stop (`ReplayStop`): Clicky's own part label, the sentence, and the camera pose the page heads for once the camera settles (`cameraPoseForTheReplay`, the page's `cameraPose()`: yaw, pitch, and distance).
A sentence about a part that already has a stop joins that stop (`replayStops(_:adding:)`), so two numbers never sit on one label.
The narration offers its stops when it ends, a cut-off one included, only while the same scene is on screen (`LiveSceneOverlay.scenesPlayedCount`), and a narration's stops replace the earlier ones, so a replay plays the latest explanation.
A scene clear forgets them (`InstantReplayController.forget`).
A replay stop calls the page's `focusStop(label, pose)`, which calls `focusPart` and eases the recorded yaw (the shortest way round), pitch, and distance with the page's own ease over 1.5 seconds, a drag, a pinch, or a voice turn or zoom taking the camera over from the turn, so the camera turns back even after the learner dragged, and jumps there with Reduce Motion on.
The page then waits for the camera, the cursor flies to the label with the sentence in its bubble (held by the replay's own ID), and a play-through holds it for 0.9 seconds plus the larger of 1.5 seconds and 0.3 seconds a word (`replayStopDwellSeconds`), then moves on. After the last stop's dwell the replay ends there, the cursor flies back to the mouse, and the focus clears as at a narration's end.
Each stop's number sits on its label's top left corner, and a dotted path in the accent links the parts of the stops reached so far (`showReplayStops`, `hideReplayStops`).
A click on a number posts `stopTapped:<index>`, parsed at the boundary as `LiveScenePageEvent.replayStopTapped`, and during a replay a click on a stop's label steps there instead of starting a new, paid explanation.
`endFocus()` stops the camera on the current frame: it clears the pan (`focusMove`) and the turn (`poseMove`), holds the target, and sets the drag and zoom goals to the current values, and any later camera ease must be stopped there too.
While a part is focused, the pan from the subject's center toward the part stops where the subject's projected box would reach further outside the paused video than it does at the center (`focusTargetThatKeepsTheSubjectOverTheMedia`, with its math tested in `worker/src/framePopoutReplay.test.ts`), so the lifted model stays over the video and off the control.
The control is a nonactivating panel that never becomes key, with first-mouse buttons (the agent list's pattern), on the Card, 14 points under the paused video or inside its bottom edge when the screen has no room (`instantReplayControlOrigin`): Replay and the stop count before a replay, and Back, Replay, Next, "2 of 3", and an end button during one.
With Reduce Motion on, a replay shows every stop's number and the whole path at once, never moves on by itself, and the control lists every stop's sentence with the current one in bold.
One generation number cancels every pending step (`InstantReplayController`): a step, End, Escape, a new Replay, push-to-talk, and a scene clear bump it, so no wait of the old travel moves the camera or the cursor afterward.
End and Escape stop the camera (`endFocus`) and the cursor where they are (`buddyFreezeCount`, which `BlueCursorView` observes, mid-flight included), and the cursor flies back to the mouse once the mouse moves 100 points (`releaseTheBuddyTheReplayFroze`, which also schedules the transient hide). Only a cursor pointing with the replay's own hold is ever frozen (`cursorPointsForTheReplay`), so a cursor pointing for an answer keeps its own fly-back.
A narration starting ends an open replay and hides the control (`narrationStarted`), and only the newest narration offers its stops when it ends, while its scene is on screen (`narrationEnded`), so a narration a tap cancelled never brings back its stops, and one that recorded none shows the control again for the stops before it. Any request that reaches `respondToTranscript` ends an open replay first, and each click on the control first stops an answer Clicky is still giving, so a replay never moves the camera or the cursor under another answer. Stopping the quiz also ends an open replay, since the quiz summary Card shows then. Every line Clicky starts to say ends an open replay too, since a replay is silent and a line means another answer from any surface. A theme design starting ends it too. `speakTextEndingAnOpenReplay` is the one caller of `ElevenLabsTTSClient.speakText`, and `playLineEndingAnOpenReplay` the one caller of `play`, for a line fetched ahead. It calls `endForAnotherAnswer`, which stops the camera and the cursor only while a replay travel is running. After a press, a voice command's own turn or zoom keeps easing. Every show and hide of the control bumps a version number, and a show that waits on the page for the media's box draws nothing once a later hide came (`controlShowIsStillWanted`).
Escape ends a replay after the menu bar panel, the dial, the agent list, the studio, an "Add theme?" card, the room art panel, a recap's disc gather, and the quiz summary Card, and ahead of the scene, so a second Escape sends the pop-out back as before.
A push-to-talk press stops the travel and the camera and keeps the replay open at its stop, so "next" and "back" still work, and the press sends the cursor back to the mouse as always.
`asksToReplayTheExplanation` ("show me again" or "replay that", as the whole utterance) runs ahead of the router (`replayPhraseRunsAheadOfTheRouter`, the `replayTheExplanation` route of `transcriptRoute`) only while stops are recorded for the scene on screen, no quiz question is open, and the Theme Studio card is closed. "Do that again" and "play it again" go to the router, since they also mean repeat the last command or play the video.
The router is offered `replay_explanation` while stops are recorded and `replay_step` (`step`: `next`, `back`, or `end`) only while a replay is open, so "back" and "stop" step the replay then and never seek the video or close the scene, and neither is offered while a quiz question is open. Both hand off, so nothing is spoken over the silent replay and a call after one in the same reply gets a `Not run` result, and their chips say "Showing it again", "Next stop", "Back a stop", and "Ended the replay".
The router's scene state carries `replay: { stops, open, atStop }` while stops are recorded.

**Transient Cursor Mode**: When "Show Clicky" is off, pressing the hotkey fades in the cursor overlay for the duration of the interaction (recording → response → TTS → optional pointing), then fades it out automatically after 1 second of inactivity.

**Live Scene**: A transcript that contains "inside", "look like", "show me", "3D", or "render" calls `POST /live-scene`, and every label stays visible.
`POST /live-scene` asks GPT-6.1 Sol Ultrafast (`gpt-6.1-sol`, service tier `ultrafast`, medium reasoning effort) for a self-contained three.js page and a quiz, and GPT-6.2 Sol Ultrafast for a simulation, which beat 6.1 Sol 14-2 in the blind judge on 2026-11-19 and takes about 17 seconds longer while the video keeps playing. GPT-6.2 Astra beat 6.1 Sol on 3D models too, at 2.5 times the cost, and 3D models stay on 6.1 Sol for the price. On a Starship frame, medium drew rounder, tiled ships than low for about $0.15 more and 2 to 4 more seconds, and high cost 4 times as much with no better ship.
The page builds the whole subject from the outside, and adds the parts inside, fading the outer shell, only when the learner asks to see inside.
A 3D model labels at most 10 parts, placed so none overlap or crowd an edge, and its view crops no part of the subject at the end of the tour or after a half turn (`labelCountAndPlacementRule`, `modelFramingRule`, which only a 3D model's prompt carries, through `liveSceneInstructionLines`).
On 2026-11-22 these rules won the blind judge 6 to 2 on 3D models and lost 1 to 4 on experiments, so a simulation's prompt keeps neither (`bench/README.md`, "Framing and label rules").
The page imports three.js only from `clicky-asset://three/three.module.js`, which the overlay serves from the app bundle.
The overlay serves the generated page from `clicky-asset://live/page.html` with a Content-Security-Policy header, a content rule blocks http, https, ws, and wss, and navigation is limited to `about` and `clicky-asset`.
When OpenAI is out of credit, rate limited, refused, or down (an HTTP 401, 403, 404, 429, or 5xx, or a fetch that throws), the Worker builds the scene on Sonnet 5.5 instead (`openAICannotAnswerNow`, shared with `requestAnthropicMessage`), which loses to GPT-6.1 Sol in the blind judge, 2 to 4, but built every page in the 2026-11-19 bakeoff.
The Worker log says `[/live-scene] gpt-6.1-sol could not answer (HTTP 429), so the scene falls back to claude-sonnet-5-5`, and the usage line names the model that answered.
On a timeout, an error, or a 422, Clicky says "Sorry, I couldn't build that one." and resumes a video it paused for the scene.
A page that fails to load, or whose web content process ends, takes the same recovery as a page that throws: a quiz falls back to the voice-only question, and a scene says that line.
A page can also hang with no error and never post `tourFinished`, so the overlay ends a tour still open 30 seconds after its page started (`LiveSceneTourEnd`): it runs the page's `endTour` and reports the end once, and the narration, the quiz question, or the landmark line goes on as after any tour.
The bundled tours end within about 15 seconds and a generated one within about 10, and the log says `Live scene: the page did not end its tour within 30 seconds, so Clicky ended it`.
Only a failure of the load Clicky started counts (`pageLoadFailureEndsTheScene`), where that load is the `WKNavigation` that `load(_:)` returned, so a navigation the page starts itself, or one the policy refuses with WebKit error 102, leaves the scene on screen.
A transcript that says "quiz" or "test me" plays the same scene with one label shown as "?".
The overlay plays a generated scene on the screen under the mouse cursor.
A frame pop-out or landmark page plays on the screen showing its video or image instead, so it lines up even when the mouse is on another display.
The page rises out of the video or image it was asked about: Clicky reads the on-screen rect of the largest visible video or image in Chrome's front tab before the request, with a 2 second Apple Event timeout, since the tab can be any busy site, skipping images that show nothing (not loaded, hidden, or fully transparent), taking the player's box instead of the box of a video the page has not started, which can sit above the player that shows its thumbnail with under half of it in the viewport (a close ancestor of about the video's size that is almost fully visible, in `largestVisibleMediaJavaScript`), and places the rect from the web area in Chrome's accessibility tree, with the page-geometry math as the fallback when no web area matches, and `LiveSceneRise.swift` adds the cursor screenshot and that rect to the page for the bundled `scene-rise.js`, which shows the screenshot clipped to the rect and grows the page out of it once the scene is ready.
In a quiz, Clicky asks what that part is and what it does when the camera tour ends, and a correct name that misses what it does gets one follow-up.
The learner answers by voice.
`POST /judge-answer` asks Sonnet to grade that answer as correct, named only, wrong, or a request for help, and returns one spoken sentence.
If the judge call fails, the app falls back to the word match in `LiveSceneAnswerJudge`.
A finished scene stays above every window until Escape clears it.
A frame pop-out does not vanish when it clears.
`window.liveScene.returnToSource()` in `frame-popout.html` fades the labels, eases the camera back to its starting pose, and flattens the subject back into the frame exactly over the video rect in 0.8 seconds, then posts `returnFinished` through `liveSceneEvent`.
`LiveSceneOverlay` tracks this as `LiveSceneOverlayPhase` (`hidden`, `showing(isFramePopout:)`, `returningToSource`).
`clear()` on a frame pop-out starts the return, and the window hides on `returnFinished` or after a 1.5 second fallback.
A second `clear()` during the return, a new scene, or any non-pop-out page hides at once, and `clear()` on a hidden overlay does nothing.
`waitForReturnToFinish()` returns when the window has hidden, and every path that resumes a video Clicky paused awaits it first, so the subject lands in the still frame before the video moves.
That includes "stop the quiz", which clears the question's pop-out before `VideoQuizSession.stop()` and then plays the video with `resumeVideoAfterQuestion()`, a plain question or "stop the quiz" that cancels a scene build, a pop-out that fails to open, and "Sorry, I couldn't build that one."
"Stop the quiz" reads whether a scene was building before it resets the phase for an open question, so a scene asked for on another tab is cancelled too.
Every clear in `CompanionManager` goes through `clearTheLiveSceneAndReleaseTheBuddy()`, which also sends the blue cursor back to the mouse when a narration holds it on a part, since a narration cancelled mid-sentence may never release it. It also forgets which page is on screen and ends an experiment's lesson, so after Escape the voice router's scene state carries no prediction question.
Only `frame-popout.html` has `returnToSource` and `answerMoment`, so generated pages and `landmark-tiles.html` keep the old instant clear and plain reveal.
During a video quiz question, Escape skips the question, clears its pop-out, and plays the video on.
When the tour ends the overlay catches the mouse, a drag orbits the camera around the last stop, and a trackpad pinch zooms it.
The overlay never becomes key, so the pinch comes from the push-to-talk event tap, not AppKit.
The overlay is a nonactivating panel, so the page under it keeps the keyboard.
Before any public release, return a plain error instead of the upstream's own error text from `/live-scene`, `/tts`, `/transcribe-token`, `GET /v1/3dtiles/`, and the Anthropic error path of `requestAnthropicMessage` (the `/v1/messages` and `/chat` passthroughs keep Anthropic's errors, since their callers read them), add a runtime block for WebRTC in dynamically created frames, add authentication and rate limits to the Worker (the `GET /v1/3dtiles/` route spends the Maps key for any caller, `POST /voice-command` is an open Haiku proxy like `/chat`, and `POST /live-scene`, `POST /video-quiz-plan`, `POST /picture-flows`, and `POST /theme-ideas` spend the OpenAI and Anthropic budget for any caller), return a plain error from `/room-wall` instead of the depth service's own error text, which it forwards on a 422 and inside a 502's error, send video captions to /live-scene as a separate untrusted field instead of merging them into the learner's request, and pass part labels read from a generated page as sanitized untrusted data instead of putting them in the system prompt.
A simulation page's control labels and units, its challenge prompt, its hypothesis, and the page tool descriptions and `inputSchema`s the router sends to `/voice-command` (a description is cut to 1000 characters, a tool whose schema encodes to over 2000 bytes is dropped, and a repeated tool name is dropped, in `voiceToolsOfferedToTheModel`) are page text of the same kind, so name them in that pass too.

**Simulation ("let me try it")**: `liveSceneKind(for:)` returns `.simulation` for "let me try it", "let me play with it", "can I try it", "simulate this", and the bare "let me try" or "can I try" when it is the whole utterance (`asksToTryASimulation`).
A try phrase must end the utterance, with up to two of "out", "now", "please", "myself", "too", or "in 3D" after it, each with an optional comma, so "let me try that again" and "can I try this on my phone" build nothing, and neither do "Let me try to ..." or "how do I simulate this".
A simulation wins over "show me" and "in 3D" but not over a quiz, pop-out, or animate request.
With no scene on screen and no quiz question open, `handleTranscript` starts the experiment agent (`startTheExperimentInTheBackground`) straight away, ahead of the voice command router, so a demo never depends on a model choosing the tool, and Clicky says "Sure, I'll build that while you watch.", or "Sure, I'll build that once you say go ahead." while every agent is paused (`experimentStartedSpokenLine`).
With a scene on screen the router reads it, since "can I try 60 degrees" means a slider.
`LiveSceneKind.workerRequestKind` is the one value that reaches `POST /live-scene` as `kind`, and a simulation is built by a background agent (`BackgroundAgentKind.experiment`, below), so the video keeps playing while it builds and pauses when the experiment rises out of it.
A simulation page is a science experiment: the prompt asks for the equipment the topic needs, a lab bench for a tabletop experiment and a grassy field for one that covers many meters, the standard simple model for the topic (projectile motion, torque balance, the pendulum period, Newton's law of cooling, buoyancy, Snell's law, Ohm's law, a rate that doubles for every 10 degrees Celsius, or electron-pair repulsion for a molecule's shape), the geometry that model's textbook formula assumes, and, for a rule-of-thumb model such as electron-pair repulsion, one free constant tuned so settled results match the textbook values.
Its rules are general, with a launched ball as the main example, so a new topic needs no paragraph of its own.
Over a screen that is not a science lesson, the prompt asks for the science behind what it shows instead of a copy of the app.
The page draws its own sky or lab room so the paused video never shows behind it, shows each instrument's reading and each material's state, sets each control's new setup without showing the result, animates each run as it happens (whatever moves, moves, a reading sweeps to its new value, and a traced path draws as it goes), and frames the equipment large with a camera that moves only for the tour, the voice tools, a drag, and the challenge, from a view where no part hides what a readout or a run depends on.
The challenge tolerance is about 3 percent of the target, or, for a readout with only a few separate values such as a molecule's angles, less than half the gap to the nearest other value.
It keeps the whole `window.liveScene` contract and adds `controls` (up to 3 of `{ name, label, min, max, step, value, unit }`, the variables), `setControl(name, value)` (clamps, applies, puts the run back at its start, false for an unknown name), `run()` (plays once from the current controls and returns a Promise that resolves when the run ends), `reset()`, `readouts` in `getState()` (`{ label, value, unit }`, the measurements, empty before the first run, rounded to 2 decimals), `setLabelsVisible(visible)` (part labels start hidden so they never cover the setup), `setPreviewVisible(visible)` (a setup with a path draws it as a dashed line that `setControl` redraws from the same physics the run uses, ending at a ring where the run will land, hidden until the guess), faded lines of the last 3 runs' paths, and `challenge` (`{ prompt, readoutLabel, target, tolerance }`) with `setChallengeVisible(visible)`, which marks the target and frames it.
The model never draws controls, and the prompt asks it to name each animation callback for what it animates and never `frame`, since GPT-6.2 Sol declared `frame` twice in about a third of simulation pages on 2026-11-19, which stops the script. `liveSceneReply.ts` rejects a simulation page that lacks any of `controls`, `setControl`, `run`, and `readouts`, or that draws an `<input>`, `<button>`, `<select>`, or `<textarea>`, as markup or with `createElement` or `createElementNS`, so the retry-once path handles it.
The other primitives are optional, and a page without one loses only that step.
The quiz fields carry the lesson: `question` is the hypothesis, `answerLabel` the control it is about, `functionWords` empty, and `explanation` the why.
A simulation is not graded by the judge, so its phase ends at `.allLabelsShowing`, and after the tour Clicky speaks the hypothesis plus "What's your guess?" (`SimulationLesson.guessInvitation`).
The lesson runs in four stages that `scene-tools.js` owns (`lessonStage`): `guess`, where Run and the dashed path stay locked until the router's `record_guess` tool hears an answer (`clickyTools.simulation.guessHeard()`) or the learner clicks the locked button, which posts `guessSkipped`; `explore`; `challenge`, which the app starts with `clickyTools.simulation.startChallenge()` once the guess is settled, hiding the dashed path so the learner aims from earlier trials, and which `startChallenge` declines, ending the lesson with no challenge, when no readout carries the challenge's `readoutLabel`, since that target could never be hit; and `done` after a hit.
`SimulationLesson.swift` holds Clicky's side: the guess, the run report parsed at the boundary (`SimulationRunReport`, with the last 5 trials and the challenge outcome), and every spoken line.
After each explore run Clicky says the readouts, then asks Claude, through the streaming `/chat` path with `simulationPredictionCheckSystemPrompt`, whether the trials answer the hypothesis.
While every trial so far used one setting (`SimulationRunReport.trialsTryMoreThanOneSetting`), Clicky skips that call (`simulationGuessCheckStep`), since one setting cannot answer a guess about a change.
It says `simulationTryAnotherSettingLine` on runs 1 and 2, and from run 3 it moves on with the page's explanation.
The reply starts with `[SETTLED]` (Clicky says whether the guess was right and why, in at most 35 words) or `[OPEN]` (what to try next), and after 3 runs the lesson moves on anyway with the page's explanation.
A failed check says the explanation.
Each challenge try gets `simulationChallengeSpokenLine`, and a hit plays "Glass".
`scene-tools.js` draws one panel on the document root once the tour ends, when the app shows the question or a tool call arrives, never by polling the page's `__tourFinished` (a slider per control, a Run button labeled "Say your guess" while locked, the hypothesis while Clicky waits for the guess (`clickyTools.simulation.showQuestion`), the readouts, the challenge line, and a results table of the last 5 trials), and a press on it never turns the model.
The panel is a Card styled by `scene-style.css` (see **Scene stylesheet**), and it rises into place when it first shows.
Its stage line names the lesson's stage: "Guess first:" with the hypothesis, "Try it:" while exploring, and "Challenge:" with the page's prompt and the tries so far, followed by the last try's outcome from the challenge report, a hit with its distance from the target in green or a miss with how far off and whether it was over or under in amber.
When a run lands, its readouts count up from the run before's over 0.6 seconds, and Reduce Motion shows them at once.
The table's one measurement column shows the challenge's readout, else the first readout that has differed between trials, else the first readout, so a fixed control sample beside the test one never fills it.
The panel is opaque, and its readouts show "…" while a run plays, since they are still the run before's.
The panel covers the bottom of the view, so `keepSceneAbovePanel` moves the camera's view up with `camera.setViewOffset`.
The lift is half the height the panel reaches with a full trials table (`panelHeightWithAFullTable`), or half a taller panel's height, so the scene stays still as rows arrive.
The view offset keeps the scene and the labels the page projects in the space above the panel, and `keepSceneAbovePanel` applies it again after a resize.
The Run button, the `run_simulation` tool, and `set_control` with `run: true` share `startRun()`, which posts `runFinished:<report JSON>` (`{ readouts, pastRuns, challenge }`) through `liveSceneEvent` when the run's Promise resolves, and ignores the end of a run a newer run replaced.
A run that rejects, throws, or has not ended after 20 seconds only unlocks Run, adds no trial, and posts nothing, since the readouts on the page are still the run before's.
`LiveScenePageEvent.simulationRunFinished` parses it at the boundary, cutting each label and unit to a short length and keeping at most 3 finite readouts, and `CompanionManager.narrateTheSimulationRun` speaks only while Clicky's own record says a simulation is on screen (`LiveScenePageOnScreen.simulation`, decided by `simulationRunMayBeNarrated`), and takes a challenge outcome only after Clicky started the challenge (`simulationRunNarrationStep`), starting with `simulationReadoutSpokenLine` (no model call, for example "That run gave a landing distance of 40.84 meters, and a peak height of 10.2 meters.").

**Voice Commands**: A spoken request, usually a follow-up command while a scene is on screen or a video plays in Chrome's front tab, on any site, goes to `POST /voice-command` instead of the keyword path (`voiceCommandRouterTakesTheTranscript`).
A quiz answer still goes to the judge, a scene still building keeps the keyword path, and "put it back", "stop the quiz", and "animate this" keep their own phrase matches ahead of the router.
The Theme Studio card changes this while no quiz question is open, as its section says.
**One brain flag**: with `brainTakesEveryRequest` on (UserDefaults, default off, read only by `BrainTakesEveryRequest.isOn`), `voiceCommandRouterTakesTheTranscript` takes every first utterance, even with nothing on screen and no video in front, so Haiku picks the action and the "animate this" and "let me try it" keyword branches are skipped (`keywordBranchAheadOfTheRouter`).
A quiz answer (the judge), a scene still building, "stop the quiz", and "put it back" keep their own code even with the flag on, and with the flag off nothing changes.
The Theme Studio card's exception applies the same way with the flag on or off.
Turn it on with `defaults write com.crodris.clicky-watch-along brainTakesEveryRequest -bool YES`, or launch a Debug build with `--brain-takes-every-request`.
**Harness flag**: with `brainRunsInTheHarness` on (UserDefaults, default on, read only by `BrainRunsInTheHarness.isOn`, where only an explicit NO turns it off), `runVoiceCommand` sends each command to the agent harness instead of `POST /voice-command` (`runVoiceCommandInTheHarness`).
It decides where the router runs, and `brainTakesEveryRequest` decides when the router takes a transcript.
The harness runs the same loop as the old path, and with the flag off nothing changes.
**The app secret decides the backend** (`VoiceCommandBackend`): the harness brain calls the Worker's `/v1/messages`, which requires the app secret, and the old path's `/voice-command` does not, so an install with no `clickyAppSecret` at launch runs voice commands on the old path and logs once `Brain: no app secret, so voice commands use the Worker router`.
A brain turn the Worker's own secret check refuses ends in `task_failed` with the reason `app secret refused` (`errAppSecretRefused` in `harness/brainloop.go`, told from Anthropic's own 401 by the Worker's body, `{"error":"Missing or wrong x-clicky-app-secret"}`, where the error is a string, and by its status).
The app then sends that same command once on the old path when none of its tools has run, and uses the old path for the rest of the launch (`nextStepAfterTheHarnessFailedACommand`), and every other failure takes the router failure fallback as before.
`verify-clicky.sh doctor` reports a missing or refused secret, and `app-launch --no-app-secret` and `--wrong-app-secret` pass the Debug-only launch argument `--app-secret-mode none` or `wrong` to drive both paths.
Every reader of the secret goes through `ClickyAppSecret.current`, so that argument makes the Worker requests, `VoiceCommandBackend`, and the harness's environment act as if the secret were missing or one the Worker refuses, and a Release build ignores it.
The options never write or delete the defaults, since the everyday Clicky shares that domain.
The old path, its Swift approval checks, and this fallback stay until the plan's last PR deletes them.
`harness/brain.go` holds the brain's prompt, which is the Worker's with the lines that named `recent_actions` rewritten to point at the earlier commands in the conversation, the `earlier_commands` tag, and the `interrupted_before` tag, and the ports of `toolsForAnthropic` and `voiceCommandNextStep`.
The Worker's own prompt in `worker/src/voiceCommand.ts` is unchanged, since the old path still uses it.
It runs Haiku 5.5 with thinking off and low effort, with a cache breakpoint on the system prompt, one on the last tool, and one on the last block of the previous command.
**The brain's session** (`harness/brainsession.go`): the harness holds one session in memory for the life of its process, and a harness relaunch or an app launch starts it empty.
A command's user message holds the scene state for that moment and the learner's words, each in its own tag (`scene_state`, `interrupted_before`, `learner_words`) with no `recent_actions`, and the session appends the user message, the model's turns, and the tool results, each tool result cut to 2,000 characters.
A result's text items share that limit, and an item with no characters left becomes the `...[cut]` marker, since the API refuses an empty text block and one refusal would fail every later command.
Each `<` in a tool result is written as `‹` before the session keeps it, with its line breaks kept, so a caption cannot hold a `learner_words` tag of its own.
A cancelled or failed command rolls back to where it started, and the action lines of the calls that finished, and a `-> cut off, may have run` line for a call a press cut off while the app ran it (`voiceToolCutOffActionLine`), go into the next command's user message in an `interrupted_before` tag.
A call that finished in the app right before the press counts too: the app writes its result ahead of `cancel_task` (`HarnessVoiceCommandLines.callFinished`), and the harness reads a result already queued when the cancel arrives (`waitForTheVoiceToolResult`), so its action line still reaches the rollback.
A hand-off stays in the session with a `Handed off:` result, and each call after it in the same reply gets a `Not run: the request was handed off` result, so every `tool_use` has a `tool_result`.
A failed call (or a call to a tool the command was not offered) stops the calls after it the same way, each getting `Not run: an earlier call in this reply failed`, and the command asks the model again.
Before a command, when no request went out in the last 5 minutes or the session holds more than 24 commands, every command but the newest 6 folds into one text block at the start (an `earlier_commands` tag, per command the learner's words and its action lines, with the scene states and tool results dropped).
The block lists at most the newest 40 folded commands (`mostFoldedCommandsKept`) and opens with one line that says how many older commands it dropped, so it stays small however long the process runs, since each command after a cache miss pays full price for it and a block that grew without limit would pass the context window.
Folding only on a cold cache or at the cap keeps a fold from throwing away a warm cache, and no model call writes it.
A new command waits for the command it cancelled to roll back, so it never reads a session about to change.
The session also holds the record of applied changes that `undo_last_change` reads (see "Undo on the harness path" below).
A cancelled command rolls back its messages but not that record, since the calls that finished did change the screen.
Every piece the session writes into the prompt that a page, a caption, or a tool's error text can reach sits in its own tag, with each `<` in it written as `‹`, each backslash doubled, and each line break character (CR, LF, vertical tab, form feed, U+0085, U+2028, U+2029) as `\n` (`escapedLine`, `taggedLine`), so it cannot close its tag or forge a line. The scene state is the one exception: it is JSON, so `taggedJSON` keeps it valid JSON, writing each `<` as `‹` and U+0085, U+2028, and U+2029 as `\u` escapes, with backslashes left as they are. Each piece is escaped once, where it is written into the prompt, so it reads the same however many commands it passes through, and a literal `\n` in page text differs from an escaped line break.
The prompt says only the `learner_words` tag holds what the learner said.
Tool results replayed from earlier commands are still data the model reads, so a yes the learner never said is refused by the harness's own check (`approvalVoiceAnswerTheLearnerSaid` in `harness/voiceapproval.go`).
No brain tool returns an image, so the session keeps none.
`harness/brainloop.go` sends one request per turn with no stream, no retry (`option.WithMaxRetries(0)`), and an 8 second timeout, for up to 3 Haiku turns, and refuses a tool the command did not offer before the app sees it.
**The turn budget rule**: when the third Haiku turn still needs the model (a lookup or a failure), the command gets one more turn on Sonnet 5.5 (`brainPlanningModel`, low effort, thinking off, the same prompt, tools, and session history, and its own cache) before the gave-up line.
Only that turn runs on Sonnet, and it has a 30 second timeout (`brainPlanningTurnTimeout`) instead of 8.
Before its request the harness writes a `log` line, `Brain: planning on claude-sonnet-5-5`, with the command's `taskID`, and the `Brain: turn 4 on claude-sonnet-5-5` token line follows.
The app reads the planning line (`HarnessTaskRouter` turns it into a `.planningTurnStarted` event, matched on the `Brain: planning on ` prefix) and waits 35 seconds for the next event (`secondsToWaitForAPlanningTurn`) instead of 10, so its wait stays above the harness's own 30.
A Sonnet turn that finishes the command ends it like any turn, and one that still needs the model, times out, or fails ends it with `gaveUp`, not `task_failed`, since the command's earlier calls already changed the screen and the keyword path would repeat them.
A hand-off, cancel, or rollback works as on any turn.
Each call reaches the app as a `tool_call` with its `turnIndex`, and `runOneVoiceToolCall` runs it with the same chips, `runVoiceTool`, and action lines as the old path, then sends its outcome back.
**Approvals on the harness path**: the app sends `approvalIDWaitingAtPress` with each `voice_command`, the request the card showed at the press, and `approvalIDSeenAtPress`, that same request when a card had also shown its whole summary for a second then, or null (`ApprovalAtPress`, `approvalAtThePressStillWaiting`), and no `approvalWaiting` in the scene state.
The harness decides the rest, next to the approval gate it shares with the task agents (`harness/voiceapproval.go`): it offers `answer_approval` only while the gate holds the waiting request, it writes `approvalWaiting` into the scene state itself from the gate's capability word and never reads or sends the summary, and it handles the `answer_approval` call itself, so the call never reaches the app.
Any answer counts only in the command's first reply (turn 0). A no counts at once, read or not (instant No). A wrong no costs the agent a retry, while a wrong yes can send data out. A no for an unread request still needs a word of no in the transcript. The router takes every command while a request waits, so a misread command could otherwise decline a request the learner never looked at. A yes counts only for the request seen at the press (`approvalOffer.yesMayCount`), and a yes for an unread request fails with a result that tells the model only a no counts yet. A yes also needs the learner's own transcript to be a short answer (`approvalVoiceAnswerTheLearnerSaid`, ported from `VoiceApprovals.swift` and pinned to it by `harness/testdata/approval-voice-answers.json`, which a Go test and a Swift test both read), and the harness settles the request through the gate, which takes the first answer it receives and drops the second, whether a voice answer or a click's `approval_result`.
The harness then sends `approval_settled` (`approvalID`, `approved`, `scope`) for either, and logs `Brain: approval <id> given (once)` or `declined` for a voice answer.
The desk drops the card and shows the chip only from `approval_settled` (`HarnessApprovalDesk.settle`), and a click marks the card as answering (`answeringApprovalIDs`) until it arrives, so the card never shows a decline the gate did not take.
The `answer_approval` call shows no chip of its own, so a voice yes on the old path shows the chip once.
A settlement that the desk's own 120 second timeout caused shows no chip when it is a decline, since "Declined it" would appear with no context, and the log line stays.
An answered request whose `approval_settled` has not come within 15 seconds (`defaultAnswerSettleGraceSeconds`) loses its card with a log line naming only its ID and shows no outcome, so a hung harness never leaves "Sending your answer..." on the card.
The harness keeps the earlier commands in its session, so the app sends no action lines with a command on this path (`recentVoiceToolActionLines` feeds only the old path).
**Undo on the harness path** (`harness/brainundo.go`): a model that undoes a change by itself has to work out the reverse of an angle, a factor, or a number of seconds, and it often gets it wrong, so the harness does that part.
The session keeps a record of the calls that changed the screen (`appliedChanges`, newest first, at most 20), each with its tool and the exact input the app ran, without the confirmation.
A call counts when its outcome is `changed` or `handedOff`, and a lookup or a failed call is not recorded.
A hand-off is recorded because no inverse reverses a pop-out, but it must stand between "undo that" and an older change, and so must a voice answer to an approval, which is `changed` with no inverse.
The record takes the finished calls of a cancelled command too, since they did change the screen, so "undo that" after a cut-off command reverses the call that finished.
Each change also keeps the scene it was made to (`sceneIdentity`, read by `sceneIdentityOf` from the command's `scene_state`), because Escape and a tab switch change the screen outside the harness, and an inverse run on another scene would turn a new pop-out back by an old one's rotation.
The page part is the state's `page` plus `sceneNumber`, a field the app adds that counts up each time a scene starts to show (`LiveSceneOverlay.scenesPlayedCount`, `sceneNumberForTheSceneState`) and is absent with no scene on screen, since two pop-outs of the same video have the same page name and labels.
The video part is the video's `title` and `durationSeconds`.
A page tool (camera, cutaway, labels, simulation) depends on the page part, a video tool (seek, pause, play, speed) on the video part, and every other tool, such as a hand-off or an answered approval, on neither (`changeTargets`), so it never goes stale and keeps standing between "undo that" and an older change.
Each command drops the stale part of the record as it begins (`changesThatStillApply`): the newest stale change and every change older than it, for good, since the newest thing the learner did is gone from the screen and an older change must not become what "undo that" reaches.
So `undo_last_change` is never offered for a change made to another scene or video, and a stale change never comes back when the learner returns to the scene.
A seeded change has no scene and takes the scene of the first command that reads it.
`undo_last_change` is offered after the app's tools, only while the newest change has an inverse and the command offers the tool that makes it (`withTheUndoOffer`), and its description never names the change, so the tool is the same text in every command that offers it.
The tool is added and removed between commands as the newest change gains or loses an inverse, and it sits at the end of the tool list where the cache breakpoint is, so each flip rewrites the cached prefix from the tool list onward, which includes the system prompt and the history breakpoints, since tools come first in the cache order (the measured cache read share fell from 0.9443 to 0.9303, see `bench/README.md`).
Its description tells the model to call it for "undo" and "undo that", and to make an older action's reverse itself, such as "undo the first turn", or "the other way".
The harness handles the call, as it does `answer_approval`, but it still runs the work in the app: it sends the app the inverse as an ordinary `tool_call` with the model's call ID plus `-undo`, so the app shows its usual chip and writes its usual action line, and the outcome of that call is the undo's outcome.
The model's confirmation is spoken as for any call, and the model reads the app's result under its own call ID, behind a line that says what ran.
The turn's `Brain: turn` log line names the model's call, `undo_last_change`, and never the inverse's input.
The system prompt does not name the tool, because a prompt that names a tool the command did not offer led Haiku to call it anyway (see `bench/README.md`), so the tool's description holds the guidance.
An undo that changed the screen removes the change it reversed and records nothing of its own, so a change is never undone twice and the change before it is next.
A failed undo leaves the change in the record, and a failed undo goes back to the model like any failed call, which also stops the later calls of its reply (each gets a `Not run` result), as the rule for a failed call says.
A later call in the same command that changes the screen first can leave the newest change without an inverse, and an undo after it fails with a result that tells the model to make the reverse itself, since the offer was decided when the command began (`inverseOfTheNewestChange`).
A call a press cancels before the app answers may already have run, since page JavaScript keeps running and `play_video` spends about 1.6 seconds on its play check (`noteACallWhoseEffectIsUnknown`).
A cut-off page call that is not an undo is recorded as an ordinary change with its inverse (`aCutOffCallHasRun`), since the app runs a page tool's JavaScript in its own web view, which answers in a few milliseconds, so "undo that" reverses it.
That holds only for a call the app had started: the app's `cancel_task` names the call it had started and not answered in `runningToolUseID` (`HarnessVoiceCommandLines.commandWasCancelled`), and the harness keeps the inverse only when that names the cut-off call (`toolUseIDTheAppWasRunningAtTheCancel`).
The app drops a call that reaches it after the press, or that waits in its queue behind the press, without running it, and its cancel names no running call then, so "undo that" never reverses a move that did not happen.
That holds only once the page's tour has finished: while the command's scene state says `tourFinished` is false (`pageTourIsStillGoing`, `pageMayStillRefuseChanges`), a pop-out may still be building and a landmark refuses every move until its reveal, so the cut-off call gets no inverse.
A page that does not report `tourFinished`, as a generated page may not, counts as finished.
Without that inverse, Haiku worked out the reverse of a cut-off turn itself and got it wrong in 11 of 15 runs of `session-undo-after-cut-off` on 2026-11-21, and with it the case passed 15 of 15.
Any other cut-off call, a video call or an undo, gets a change with no inverse in its place (`effectIsUnknown`), and "undo that" cannot reach past it.
Every cut-off call also gets an action line that ends `-> cut off, may have run`, which reaches the next command's `interrupted_before` tag, so the model, which the system prompt tells to reverse an earlier action itself when asked to undo, does not reach past the cut-off call either.
A line the app's result already wrote (a result queued before the cancel) gets no second line.
For an undo cancelled before the app answered, that change also keeps the change the undo reversed from being undone a second time, so no undo is offered until another change is made.
A call the cancel kept from being sent never ran and leaves no change, and neither does a lookup, which changes nothing.
An undo whose result the app wrote before its `cancel_task` removes the change it reversed and sends its line to the next command in `interrupted_before`.
The inverse table (`inverseBuilders`) holds only tools whose inverse follows from their own input: `rotate_model` (both angles negated), `zoom` (the reciprocal of the factor after the page's 0.3 to 3 clamp, and none for a factor under one third, whose reciprocal one call cannot make), `seek_relative` (negated), `set_labels` and `set_inside_view` (the flag flipped), and `pause_video` and `play_video` (each the other).
The flags and the pause pair are two-valued, so a call that changed something started from the opposite value.
Every other tool is left out, because its inverse needs state its input does not hold (`explode_view`, `set_speed`, `seek_to_time`, `seek_to_moment`, whose result names the line it landed on and never the time it left, `zoom_to_part`, `reset_view`, `set_control`) or it has no opposite (`run_simulation`, `close_scene`).
The inverse is exact until the page clamps the move, and then it moves back too far: this is a known limit, and the harness adds no clamp logic.
The page clamps the camera's absolute pose (distance 0.4 to 1.8 times its rest distance, and on a frame pop-out pitch within plus or minus 1.1 radians with yaw unlimited, or pitch within plus or minus 0.45 radians and yaw within plus or minus 0.75 radians on a card that shows depth), so zooming in by 2 twice stops at the 0.4 floor and an undo by 0.5 lands at 0.8.
A seek past the end of the video is clamped the same way.
The old path and the Worker's prompt (`worker/src/voiceCommand.ts`) do not have the tool.
A bench `seed_session` fills the record from its messages, counting every call that ran since a seed has no outcome, and from its `interruptedBefore` lines (`appliedChangesOfASeed`), and `reset_session` clears it.
A seed reads an interrupted line that ends `-> cut off, may have run` as a new change, with its inverse for a page call and with none for any other call, and never as an undo, as the real harness records it.
A seed skips a failed call and a call whose result is `Not run`, reads a seeded `undo_last_change` as the removal of the newest change recorded before it, and reads an interrupted line that is the inverse of that newest change as the undo's own line, since an undo's action line is the inverse call's line and a seed cannot tell it from a new call that happens to be that inverse.
`voice_command_finished` carries the spoken line whole, which the app cuts to 300 characters by grapheme cluster (`voiceCommandLineToSpeak`) and speaks after the serve lands, or `gaveUp`, which says `voiceCommandGaveUpLine`.
**The early line**: a turn with calls first sends `voice_line_if_every_call_changes` with the line it speaks if every call returns `changed` (`spokenLineIfEveryCallChanges` in `harness/brain.go`, the one function `nextVoiceStep` also speaks), ahead of its first `tool_call`.
The line carries the turn's tool names (`toolNames`), and the app starts fetching its audio at once (`ElevenLabsTTSClient.startFetching`), so the ElevenLabs round trip overlaps the calls instead of following them, unless a call hands off or looks something up (`earlyLineIsWorthFetching`, from `AppVoiceTool.mayHandOff` and the offered read-only tools), since that line never plays and its fetch would only bill ElevenLabs.
On `voice_command_finished` it plays the fetched audio only when the cut finished line is exactly the cut early line (`voiceCommandLineAudio`), and otherwise fetches the finished line as before.
A later turn's early line replaces an earlier one, and every exit that does not play it cancels the fetch: a press, a hand-off, a failure, a gave-up line, or other words.
A `task_failed`, a harness that cannot start, or 10 seconds with no harness event while the app waits on one (`secondsToWaitForTheNextHarnessVoiceCommandEvent`, 35 seconds after the planning line) runs the same fallback as a failed router (`runTheRouterFailureFallback`).
Once that wait has run out, a tool call still queued behind it never runs, since the fallback answers the request.
A `voice_command` line the harness refuses, such as one with no tools, gets a `task_failed` for its `taskID` at once, so the fallback starts with no wait, as the old path's 400 did.
With the Debug "Log tool call JSON" switch on, the app logs each tool call the harness sends (`Voice command JSON tool call from the harness`).
A press cancels the command's Swift task, whose cancel handler sends `cancel_task`, naming in `runningToolUseID` a call it had started and not answered, and the harness logs `Brain: command cancelled during turn N`.
A hand-off cancels that task itself before its outcome exists, so it sets a mark first (`HarnessVoiceCommandLines.handOffHasStarted`), and the cancel handler then sends the `handedOff` result instead of `cancel_task`.
A call that finished before the press has its outcome recorded the same way (`callFinished`, called by `runOneVoiceToolCall` once the call ran and the press had not cancelled it), and the cancel handler then sends that result before `cancel_task`.
A call still running at the press sends only `cancel_task`, and a hand-off's mark wins over both.
Every hand-off branch of `runAppVoiceTool` returns `AppVoiceToolStep.handOff` with its new path, and only `voiceToolOutcome(of:handOffMark:)` starts that path, after it sets the mark, so no branch can hand off without it.
Once a `handedOff` result is sent, the app ends the command as finished with no spoken line, without waiting for `voice_command_finished`, since the harness always finishes silently after one, and a hand-off that leaves the task running, such as `close_scene`, never reaches the fallback when the harness exits or stalls.
The harness marks a command complete under its mutex when the `handedOff` result arrives, so a `cancel_task` after it changes nothing, and a new command cancels one still running.
With the flag on, the harness process starts with the app (`AgentHarness.prewarm`).
Turn it off with `defaults write com.crodris.clicky-watch-along brainRunsInTheHarness -bool NO`, and a Debug build launched with `--brain-runs-in-the-harness`, which `verify-clicky.sh app-launch --harness-brain` passes, turns it on whatever the saved setting says.
A Debug build launched with `--worker-port <1024-65535>` calls the local Worker on that port instead of 8787 (`debugWorkerPort`), which `verify-clicky.sh app-launch --worker-port` passes, so a verify run can use its own Worker while another checkout's holds 8787.
A Debug rehearsal step that starts with `^` presses push-to-talk through `handleShortcutTransition(.pressed)` 0.3 seconds after its `voice_command` line goes out, and releases it 0.2 seconds later.
**Voice command timing**: each routed voice command, on the harness brain or the Worker router, logs one line when it ends (`VoiceCommandTiming`, `VoiceCommandTimingRecorder`): `Voice command timing: release_to_transcript_ms=… release_to_screen_state_ms=… transcript_to_request_ms=… request_to_first_call_ms=… first_call_to_first_line_ms=… release_to_first_line_ms=… line_audio=… speculation=…`, where `line_audio` is `fetched_early` when the finished line played the audio fetched for the early line, `fetched_now` when it was fetched after the calls, and `-` when no finished line was spoken or the command ran on the Worker router, and `speculation` is `kept` for a command started on the partial transcript that the final one kept (its `transcript_to_request_ms` is then negative, since it went out before the transcript), `discarded` for the final transcript's own command after it discarded one, and `none` otherwise, with `-` for a mark the command never reached, such as the first line of a hand-off, which speaks elsewhere, or the release of a quiz scene command, which is not timed from its press: its press went to the quiz answer judge first, so its release mark stays `-` and its transcript mark comes after the `/judge-answer` call. The marks are the push-to-talk release (`lastPushToTalkRelease`, which a transcript counts only for its own press, and which the release of a press ignored during the microphone's tail never stamps, `PushToTalkPressAcceptance`), the transcript reaching `sendTranscriptToClaudeWithScreenshot`, everything the command reads from the screen being in hand, the command going to the harness or the Worker, the first tool call reaching the app, and the first spoken line's audio starting (`speakLiveSceneLine` returning true). A rehearsal step waits 0.7 seconds after its simulated release before it sends the transcript, standing in for the microphone's 0.5 second tail and the provider's final result, so a rehearsal's release marks are comparable between runs but are not a measure of real transcription time.
**Screen read at release**: everything a voice command reads from the screen before its model call needs no words, so push-to-talk's release starts it (`startReadingTheScreenForTheVoiceCommand`, `VoiceCommandScreenRead`), and it overlaps the microphone's 0.5 second tail and the transcript's finalization instead of following the transcript. The release of a press that was ignored during the tail starts nothing and stamps no release time (`PushToTalkPressAcceptance`), and a release starts no read while a quiz question is open or a scene is building (`screenReadStartsAtRelease`). The read is the front video state (`frontVideoStateForVoiceCommands`, which takes the release's front tab, read by Apple Events to Chrome on the main actor) and then the page's tools and state and the study guide check (`readThePageForTheVoiceCommand`, a `Task` that `runVoiceCommand` awaits when it has not finished, inside a cancellation handler that cancels it, so a press does not leave a cancelled command waiting on a hung study guide check, and a command a press cancelled during the read returns before it sends anything to the harness or the Worker). The study guide check takes its URL from the release's front tab read (`FrontVideoURLSource.releaseRead`, nil included, so a release with no tab never reads again) and then may wait on the Worker, and it reads `frontTabURLOfTheFrontWindow` itself only for a command with no release read (`FrontVideoURLSource.readNow`, the `runVoiceCommand` fallback). `CompanionManager` holds the one read (`voiceCommandScreenRead`): a press clears it and cancels its page read, a release starts it, and the transcript of the same press takes it at the top of `sendTranscriptToClaudeWithScreenshot`, once, before any early return (`screenReadForTheTranscript`), so a transcript never uses a read from an older press or one started before its own press was released, and a read that a transcript takes but no voice command receives is cancelled. A press with no words, such as a quick tap that interrupts a reply, gets no transcript, so its read runs and stays until the next press cancels it. The read records what identified the page on screen when it started (`VoiceCommandPageIdentity`: whether the overlay shows a scene, `LiveSceneOverlay.scenesPlayedCount`, and the kind of `liveScenePageOnScreen`), and the transcript drops the read when the identity has changed, so Escape, a pop-out's return, or a new scene during the tail never hands the router the tools of a page that is gone, and the screen is read fresh. A transcript with no usable read, such as a quiz scene command or a release that came while a quiz question or a scene build was open, reads the screen as it did before (`frontVideoStateSourceForTheTranscript`). The conditions that decide whether the router takes the transcript, a quiz question open and a scene building, are still checked when the transcript arrives, and then the read's front video is dropped (nil, with no study guide), so a read nobody uses is only a wasted read. The Apple Events block the main actor for about 300 ms at release. The microphone's audio tap runs on the audio thread, so no audio is lost, and the tail's stop is a main queue timer that fires late only when the main actor is blocked past the tail. That was not measured on a real press: a rehearsal runs no microphone session, so its 730 ms `release_to_transcript_ms` is its fixed 0.7 second sleep, and the fall in `transcript_to_request_ms` from a median of 326 to 8 on 2026-11-20 is the only rehearsal result. On a real release the cursor loop's read of the front tab's media (`CursorLoopController`) and this read both block the main actor, one after the other.
Both begin with Chrome's front tab, and the study guide check needs its URL, so the release makes one `FrontTabReadAtRelease` and hands it to both: the first to ask reads the tab (the loop's task, when Chrome is the front app) and the others reuse it, and a release no one asks about reads nothing.
The voice read's tab is the loop's snapshot, taken before the loop's media read, so a tab switch in the next few hundred milliseconds is not seen by that command (as before, only the snapshot time moved).
The tab read is one AppleScript with several property reads and takes 125 to 235 ms on the main actor, so reading it once instead of three times cut `release_to_screen_state_ms` from a median of 539 ms to 258 ms over steps 2 to 8 of an eight step rehearsal on 2026-11-21 (683 to 372 for the first step).
The loop's own read stays at about 190 ms, and the loop and the voice command still read their own page scripts, since they read different things: the largest media's geometry, and the front video's state.
The log says `Release read: read the front tab in N ms` and then `Release read: reused the front tab, which took N ms to read the first time`.
**Starting on the partial transcript** (`VoiceCommandSpeculation.swift`): on a real press the final transcript arrives about 0.55 to 0.6 seconds after the release (0.7 in a rehearsal), while the screen read is in hand after about 0.2, so once the release's page read is in, the voice command starts on the words heard so far (`BuddyDictationManager.partialTranscript`).
Apple Speech's partial words lag the audio, so at that moment the partial is often only the first word ("Zoom", "Turn") or nothing, and each newer partial before the final transcript (`BuddyDictationManager.partialTranscriptUpdates`) discards the waiting command and starts it again on the newer words (`screenReadToStartFrom(whenThePartialWordsBecome:)`), so the last partial, the one most likely to match the final transcript, is the one that waits.
