It starts only when `speculativeStartStep` allows it: speculation is on, voice commands run on the harness brain, no approval request waits at the press, read or not (`ApprovalAtPress` is nil in the command's `VoiceCommandInputs`, since the harness settles `answer_approval` from the transcript's own words and a no counts for a request whose card was not read), the partial has words, and `transcriptRoute` sends the partial to the router. Words that are missing or that do not go to the router yet wait for newer ones (`waitForOtherWords`).
`transcriptRoute` is the one place a transcript's route is decided, for the partial and for the final transcript: the quiz phrases, "put it back", the keyword branches, the router (`voiceCommandRouterTakesTheTranscript`), or the keyword path, in that order, and it reads the front video only past the keyword branches.
Its `TranscriptRouteContext` carries `backgroundAgentIsRunning` and `backgroundAgentsArePaused`, so "hold on" and "go ahead", which are router tools (`pause_agents`, `resume_agents`) and not keyword branches, go to the router while an agent runs or a pause holds, for the partial and the final transcript alike, and to the keyword path when no agent runs.
A command held across a change in the agents, a pause, a resume, or an agent that started or finished while the learner spoke, is discarded, since the tools offered and the scene state's `backgroundAgents` differ (`VoiceCommandInputs`), and the final transcript builds its own command.
"Go ahead" is also a yes to a waiting approval request, so a request at the press keeps it from starting a command on a partial transcript like any other word, and `resume_agents` is not offered while one waits.
The harness never pauses a voice command, held or not (`pause_task` for a command changes nothing), so a pause never holds, cancels, or reorders a held command.
Room art's `showArtInMyRoom` is one of those keyword branches.
With the brain flag off, a partial transcript that asks for room art starts no command, and with it on the router takes it and the harness holds the command until the final transcript.
`show_art_in_my_room` is a hand-off tool (`AppVoiceTool.mayHandOff`), so its confirmation line is never fetched early.
The command goes out with `waitsForTheFinalTranscript`, and the harness runs its first turn but holds it at a gate before its first call and its commit (`finalTranscriptGate` in `harness/brainloop.go`), so nothing the learner can see or hear happens on a partial transcript.
Its events, the early line included, wait in their stream, so the early line's audio starts fetching only at the confirm.
`CompanionManager` holds one `VoiceCommandSpeculationState`: `waitingForTheScreenRead`, then `waitingForWords` or `awaitingTheFinalTranscript` (each holding the release's `ReleaseScreenReadInHand`), and a new press ends any of them (`end()`), discarding a waiting command.
The press's final transcript takes it (`takeForTheFinalTranscript(ofPress:)`) and `speculationStep` keeps it only when the final transcript goes to the router, the backend is still the harness, the held command still runs (`HarnessTaskRouter.isTracking`, false once its first turn's `task_failed` arrived or its harness exited, so the final transcript starts a fresh command that also launches a new harness), the transcript still has its release read, the words match (`transcriptWordsForComparison`: lowercase, split on whitespace, punctuation dropped only at the edges of a word, so "1:30" and "130" or "-45" and "45" differ), and the tools, the scene state, and the approval at the press built again from the same page read equal the ones sent (`VoiceCommandInputs`).
A kept command gets `confirm_voice_command` and runs through `runVoiceCommandInTheHarness` like any other, and its event deadline starts only then, since its events waited in the stream.
A discarded command gets `cancel_task` with no running call, the harness rolls it back with nothing in `interrupted_before` and no change recorded, and the final transcript runs the normal way, which costs one cancel line and no wait.
A Debug build launched with `--no-voice-command-speculation` turns it off (`VoiceCommandSpeculation.isOn`), and the log says `Voice command: started on the partial transcript "<words>" N ms after the release`, `Voice command: the final transcript kept the command started on the partial transcript`, or `Voice command: discarded the command started on the partial transcript, because <reason>` (`newer words were heard` for a restart), or `Voice command: waits for newer partial words N ms after the release, because <reason>`, and the harness logs `Brain: turn 1 waited N ms for the final transcript`.
A rehearsal step that starts with `%` stands in for the microphone's partial transcript: `%partial >> final` hears `partial` at the release and `final` as the final transcript, and `%words` hears the same words both times.
A press whose dictation ends with no final transcript (it came back empty, or Apple Speech heard no speech) discards its waiting command at once (`endForAnEmptyFinalTranscript(ofPress:)`, through the dictation session's `endWithNoTranscript`), the same way a final transcript with other words does, with the log reason `the final transcript came back empty`.
A page with no video and a Chrome that refused or timed out both leave the command with the 7 tools of the no-video set, a 4,206 token cache write, so each read that finds no video logs why.
A failed front tab read logs `Release read: could not read the front tab: <error>`, or `Front video: could not read the front tab: <error>` on a read with no release, where a Chrome with no window is `chromeHasNoWindow`.
A video state script that fails logs `Front video: could not read the video state of the front tab (<host>): <error>`, such as a timeout.
A reply with no video state logs `Front video: no video state for the front tab (<host>), because <reason>` (`ReasonTheReplyHoldsNoVideoState`): the page has no video it can reach, which includes a video in a frame from another site, the page script returned nothing, which is what Chrome returns when the script throws, the reply is not JSON, or the state failed the check of what the model may read.
The `Front video:` lines come from every caller of `frontVideoStateForVoiceCommands` and of the voice video tools, so a voice play's check can log up to 4 of them in 1.6 seconds.
Each command logs `Voice command: offering N tools, a video in front` or `no video in front` at its start.
On 2026-11-21 one of three Debug launches gave its first command the no-video set while a video played.
Five launches after that, each with a video command first (two with Chrome the front app, three with Finder), all gave it 16 tools and a video, so the cause is not known.
One guess, not tested, is a script that outlasts its 2 second Apple Event timeout while Chrome is busy.
A router failure with the flag on starts the experiment for "let me try it" when no scene is on screen (`routerFailureFallback`), and sends anything else down the keyword path.
The tools use the WebMCP and MCP shape (`{ name, description, inputSchema }`), but the page is a `WKWebView`, not Chrome, so they are plain functions the app calls with `callAsyncJavaScript`, not `navigator.modelContext`.
Page tools live in the bundled `LiveSceneAssets/scene-tools.js`, which the overlay adds to every page before its scripts run as `window.clickyTools` (`list`, `call`, `state`).
They are `rotate_model`, `zoom`, `zoom_to_part`, `reset_view`, `explode_view`, and `set_inside_view`, each listed only while the page has the primitive it needs, and every call ends the camera tour first, except on a landmark page before its reveal, which keeps locating and framing the place until then, and refuses a voice turn, zoom, or reset with "still loading", since the tour would put the camera back when it ends. A frame pop-out refuses them the same way while it builds or lands back in the video, so Clicky never confirms a change that did not happen.
`frame-popout.html` and `landmark-tiles.html` add `rotateBy`, `endTour`, `getState`, and `resetView` to `window.liveScene`, and their camera eases to a voice zoom or turn after the tour, or jumps there with Reduce Motion on. A voice turn on a pop-out of a picture that already shows depth stays inside the same limits a drag has, so the card never shows its edge.
Rule 7 of the `/live-scene` prompt asks generated pages for the same primitives plus `THREE`, `camera`, `renderer`, and `parts` (`{ label, object, isOuterShell }`), which `explode_view` moves apart and `set_inside_view` cuts open with a clipping plane through the model's center that follows the camera.
Page tools also include `set_control` (name and value, and an optional `run` boolean, listed with the page's controls and their ranges in its description) and `run_simulation`, each listed only while the page has `controls`, `setControl`, and `run`, and `set_labels` (visible), listed only while the page has `setLabelsVisible`, with `labelsAreShown` in the state.
`set_control` with `run: true` sets the control and then runs through the same `startRun()` as `run_simulation`, so a request such as "try 60 degrees" is one call instead of a chain whose last call the brain sometimes dropped while its spoken line said it ran (4 of 40 runs of `planning-guess-set-run`).
The guess lock applies: before a guess is heard the whole call is refused before the control moves, with a result that says it did not set or run, since a failed call must change nothing for the harness's undo record.
`run_simulation` stays for "run it again", and its chip reads "Running it" while the chip of `set_control` with `run: true` reads "Setting angle to 60 and running it".
`pageVoiceToolNames` lets only those names through.
App tools (`AppVoiceTool`) are `answer_with_screen`, `build_new_scene`, `pop_out`, and `animate_picture` (the "animate this" path: it animates the pop-out on screen, or pops the picture out and animates it), which hand off to `respondToTranscript` (the old path) and are offered whenever no quiz question is open, with or without a scene or video, `show_art_in_my_room`, a hand-off to the room art panel offered whenever no quiz question is open, `build_simulation`, which starts the experiment agent like `build_model_in_background` starts a model and is left out while an experiment is on screen (Clicky's own record says `.simulation` and the overlay shows a scene, `simulationIsOnScreen`, never the page's `lessonStage`, which a page can fake), so it cannot start a second paid build, plus `record_guess`, offered only while a simulation waits for a guess, `answer_approval`, offered only while a task agent waits for the learner's OK (see "Skills, MCP servers, and approvals"), and `close_scene`, `explain_part`, `pause_video`, `play_video`, `seek_relative`, `seek_to_time`, `set_speed`, `find_moment`, `seek_to_moment`, `end_quiz`, `make_study_guide`, `revise_study_guide`, `open_study_guide`, `build_model_in_background`, `show_ready_model`, `start_background_task`, `hear_task_answer`, and `pause_agents` and `resume_agents` (see **Pausing every agent**), each offered only when it can work. The Theme Studio's `design_theme`, `pick_theme_idea`, `save_theme`, and `close_theme_studio` are described under **Theme Studio**, and `replay_explanation` and `replay_step` under **Instant replay**.
The study guide tools are offered while a YouTube video is in front and no quiz question is open. A video gets one guide: the app saves each guide in UserDefaults under the video's YouTube id (`SavedStudyGuides`, `studyGuideVideoKey`), so it outlives a relaunch, and once a video has one, `make_study_guide` is left out and only revise and open are offered, so asking again opens the saved guide. Before reusing a saved guide, the app asks `POST /study-guide-status` whether its page is still in Notion, once per guide per launch, alongside the page reads of the voice command, and again on every `open_study_guide`. A trashed or deleted page makes the app forget the saved guide (`savedStudyGuideStillInNotion`), so the video can get a new one, and so does a 400, which the Worker gives every handle once the Notion token changes (`studyGuidePageExists`). When the Worker cannot answer, the saved guide stands. It also stands while a guide agent runs for the video, since a revise or refresh trashes the old page before its reply arrives, and when a newer guide was saved during the check (`savedStudyGuideIsForgotten`). A new guide for a video that already has one, such as after a tab switch between the request and the write, fails with "This video already has a study guide". The app opens a saved page only when it is an https page on Notion (`pageURLIsOnNotion`), and refuses a Worker reply that names any other.
`make_study_guide` and `revise_study_guide` start a background agent whose task lives in `backgroundAgentTasks`, keyed by the agent's id (never `currentResponseTask`, so a push-to-talk press does not cancel it). Several agents run at once, and each video has one guide agent at a time (`aStudyGuideAgentIsRunning(forVideoKey:)`). The task posts the captions and the video's quiz results to `POST /study-guide`. Both tools return at once so the router speaks its confirmation while the video keeps playing.
Each guide is a background agent (`BackgroundAgentStore`, owned by `CompanionManager`), so the learner sees the work.
While it runs, its agent token floats beside the notch (see **Agent tokens** below), with an amber ring around an amber triangle under Clicky blue when no other agent has amber. The ring's fill is `BackgroundAgent.progress(at:)`, which moves in a straight line through each step toward 85% of that step's share over the step's expected seconds (`BackgroundAgentStep`, 3 for the captions and 12 for writing) and waits there if the step runs long, so it never shows a step done before it is. It is timed from `currentStepStartedAt`, so the token and the agent list's meter show the same progress at the same moment (`AgentProgressView`, one linear animation per step, no loop). The agent list's row shows the same disc as the token, so its ring fills with the same progress. The app log says `Study guide: read the captions in N s`.
Clicky no longer speaks when a guide ends, because the learner is watching a video.
A finished guide's token fills its ring, keeps its color, and shows a green check mark at its base, "Tink" plays once, a chip above the cursor says "Study guide ready" in the agent's amber, with an amber triangle for its icon, so it reads as that token's line, and the page opens in a Chrome tab behind the one the learner is on (`ChromeVideoTab.openInABackgroundTab`, https only, the active tab index is put back).
With no Chrome window to open it in, the chip says "· say open my study guide" instead, and `open_study_guide` still opens the page and brings it forward. Clicky keeps the id of the tab it opened for each guide, so `open_study_guide` switches to that tab and brings its window forward (`appleScriptToBringChromeTabForward`, which compares Chrome's tab ids as text), and opens the page again only when the learner closed the tab. A revision's new page loads in the tab of the page it replaces (`appleScriptToLoadPageInChromeTab`), and the chip says "· updated in its tab".
A failed guide's token turns gray in a red ring with no sound, and the chip says "Couldn't write the study guide" or "Notion isn't connected yet", for 4 seconds like the finished chip.
`build_model_in_background` is the second background agent kind, `BackgroundAgentKind.threeDModel`, with a violet ring (`DS.Colors.backgroundAgentThreeDModelViolet`).
It is offered while a video is in front and no quiz question is open, and its `whatToBuild` input holds the learner's words.
Its steps in the agent list are "Grab the frame", a screenshot of the playing video, and "Build the 3D model", the same `POST /live-scene` request a scene build makes. The video is not paused, no status line shows, and nothing is spoken while it runs.
The tool descriptions split the two builds: asking to build, make, or create a 3D model goes to the background agent, and asking to show, see, or render something in 3D now goes to `build_new_scene`, which keeps the landmark check, so a famous place still shows its Google 3D tiles.
When the model is ready, "Tink" plays and the chip says "3D model ready". It rises out of the video at once (`backgroundSceneMayRiseNow`) only when Clicky is idle and done speaking, no scene or pop-out is building or showing, no room art panel is open, no quiz question is open, and the tab it was built from is in front.
Then Clicky pauses the video, takes a new screenshot for the rise so the still frame matches, plays the page with `scene-rise.js` on the screen showing the video, and narrates it like a scene build. The build's screenshot is of that screen too (`screenAndCaptureShowing`), since the mouse may be on another display by then. Clearing it resumes the video.
Otherwise the page waits in `readyBackgroundScene`, the chip adds "· say show the model", and `show_ready_model` shows it the same way. The router takes every command while a model or experiment waits, even with no video in front, and a scene that finishes building replaces one still waiting, so the build that finishes last is the one shown, even when it was asked for first. A push-to-talk press during the rise's screenshot puts the model back to wait, and plays the video on unless a newer request started, since a scene request holds the pause. A plain question asked during that screenshot leaves the video paused.
A failed build's token turns gray in a red ring, and the chip says "Couldn't build the 3D model".
`build_simulation` and the direct "let me try it" path start the third background agent kind, `BackgroundAgentKind.experiment`, with a lime ring (`DS.Colors.backgroundAgentExperimentLime`).
It builds the page of the Simulation section above with the same code as the 3D model agent (`buildTheBackgroundScene`), so the video keeps playing, no status line shows, and nothing is spoken while it runs. Its steps are "Grab the frame" and "Build the experiment" (about 40 seconds), and the log says `Experiment: built "<what>" in N s`.
The agent holds the finished page in a `ReadyBackgroundScene`, which also records the agent's kind and, for an experiment, the `SimulationLesson` made from the page's quiz fields.
When it is ready, "Tink" plays and the chip says "Experiment ready", and it rises out of the video under the same conditions as a model.
An experiment built with no video in Chrome's front tab keeps no source tab (`sourceTabURL` nil) and may rise once the screen is free, since "let me try it" also works on a screen that is not a video (`aReadyBackgroundSceneMayRiseOverTheFrontTab`). A model still needs its source tab in front.
Saying "let me try it" while an experiment waits shows it, and while one builds says "Your experiment is still building." and starts nothing (`experimentRequestStep`), and `build_simulation` fails the same way, since one build at a time keeps a paid build from replacing another.
A "let me try it" while a scene builds in the foreground cancels that build first.
When the screen is busy the chip adds "· say show the experiment", and `show_ready_model` shows it.
An experiment plays with `pageOnScreen: .simulation` and sets `simulationLesson` from the ready scene, and Clicky does not narrate it, since the lesson speaks its own guess invitation after the tour.
The router's `build_simulation` confirmation is a few plain words that it has started, and a failed build's chip says "Couldn't build the experiment".
`start_background_task` is the general agent kind, `BackgroundAgentKind.task`, with a teal ring (`DS.Colors.backgroundAgentTaskTeal`), for any task that needs more than a quick answer from the screen, such as "find out how this catch compares to the first booster catch". Its `goal` input holds the learner's words with what "this" refers to, and the tool is offered outside a quiz question. It also takes an optional `skill` input, a string enum of the skill names, listed only while the catalog has skills (see "Skills, MCP servers, and approvals").
The harness runs the loop (`harness/loop.go`): one streamed `POST /v1/messages` turn at a time with an append-only history, up to `mostTaskAgentTurns` (14), on Sonnet 5.5 at medium effort with `tool_choice` auto, web search and web fetch (6 uses each, fetched pages cut to 8,000 tokens), and the agent's strict tools with a cache breakpoint on the last one. `runTheTaskAgent` starts the task on the harness (`AgentHarness.startTask`) and runs each `tool_call` it gets, one at a time in order, then sends the `tool_result`, until `task_finished` (`finishTheTaskAgent`, with the page URL the app wrote) or `task_failed` (the failure chip). Cancelling the agent's Swift task sends `cancel_task`, which stops that task's request in the harness. The app runs the agent's app tools (`TaskAgentTool`): `report_step`, which sets the list's steps (`BackgroundAgentStore.reportStep`, `BackgroundAgent.reportedSteps`); `read_video_captions`; `look_at_video`, a capture of only the video's area from Chrome's windows (`captureScreenRectAsJPEG`), so a web page that tells the agent to look around the screen gets nothing else; and `write_notion_page`, through `POST /notion-page`, at most once, keeping the page only when the reply names an https page on Notion (`taskAgentNotionPageURL`), since "Open page" hands it to NSWorkspace, and sending a page with an empty title, a summary under 40 characters, no section, or a section with an empty heading or point back to the agent to rewrite (`taskAgentPageInputProblem`), since one take wrote a page whose whole body was "x". The harness refuses the page before it reaches the app when a source link is outside what the task may cite (`citableSources` in `harness/taskagent.go`): the links of its web search results, the page in front when the task started, and the `sourceURL` of a `read_video_captions` result, which the harness reads from that field and never from the captions text. A link matches when its scheme and host in lowercase, its path with no trailing slash, and its query are the same, ignoring the fragment. The page writes the link as the set spelled it, not as the agent did, since a fragment or extra slashes could carry data to the page's script: the harness sends the app an input rebuilt from what it parsed, with each `sources[i].url` replaced by the set's spelling. A fetched page adds nothing, since the fetch result reports the link the agent asked for even after a redirect, so a page reached through a link inside a fetched page can be read but not cited, and neither can a link from the clipboard, a screenshot, a caption, or an MCP result. The refusal names each refused link and says which sources count, so a page-steered agent cannot carry what it read out in a source link. The harness log says how many sources were refused, never which ones, or says that the sources could not be read. A page the task was not offered gets "is not a tool" with no source check. The general tools are `take_screenshot` (the screen under the mouse, returned as an image and saved as `screenshot-N.jpg` in a folder for that task under the temporary directory, whose path comes back with it so a later tool can pass the file, and which is deleted when the task ends), `read_clipboard`, `write_clipboard`, and `open_url` (https only, `taskAgentOpenableURL`), run by `runTaskAgentGeneralTool`. A tool name the app does not know gets an error result. `finish` never reaches the app. `nextStepAfter` (`harness/taskagent.go`) decides what follows a turn: a paused turn goes back as it is, finish ends the task once the other calls of its turn have run, since the agent often writes its page and finishes in one turn, unless that page was refused, which sends the refusal back with a finish that did not count (`finishWaitsForTheRefusedPage`), and an agent that stops without finish leaves its last words as the answer. A refusal, a reply cut off at the token limit, or an error from the Worker fails the task with a reason. The harness logs one token line per turn, which the app prints, since the Worker no longer sees a turn's usage.
A task's progress runs as one 45 second step, since nobody knows its steps in advance. When it ends, "Tink" plays, a page it wrote opens in a background tab, and the teal chip says "Task done · say what did you find". The answer is spoken only when the learner asks (`hear_task_answer`, `readyTaskAnswer`), so it never talks over the video. Until it is heard, the router takes every command, even with no video in front, so "what did you find" works from the page the agent opened (`taskAnswerWaitsToBeHeard`). A failed task's chip says "Couldn't finish the task".
A web search answer took about 11 seconds in two turns, and a comparison with a Notion page about 25 seconds in four turns.
**Agent harness protocol**: one JSON object per line, each with a `type`, and a task's messages carry its `taskID`, the agent's UUID string in `BackgroundAgentStore`. The app sends `start_task` (`goal`, `frontTabTitle`, `frontTabURL`, the last two null with no tab, `skill`, the name of a skill file or null, and `startsPaused`, true only for a task started while every agent is paused), `pause_task` and `resume_task` (`taskID`, see **Pausing every agent**), `tool_result` (`toolUseID`, `content` as a string or an array of content blocks such as the image block `look_at_video` returns, `isError`, and `sourceURL`, the video's link, which the app sets for `read_video_captions` alone), `approval_result` (`approvalID`, `approved`, `scope`: `once` or `always`, `once` when missing), and `cancel_task` (with `runningToolUseID` for a voice command, the call the app had started and not answered at the press, and left out when none was running). A voice command reuses the task messages with its own UUID in `taskID`: the app sends `voice_command` (`transcript`, `sceneState`, and `tools` as `{ name, description, inputSchema, readOnly }`, and `approvalIDWaitingAtPress` and `approvalIDSeenAtPress`, each a string or null, and the harness reads no `recentActions`, and `waitsForTheFinalTranscript`, true only for a command started on the partial transcript, which the harness holds before its first call and its commit until `confirm_voice_command` (`taskID`) arrives, and which the harness refuses with `task_failed` when either approval field is set, since a partial "yes" or "no" could answer that request) and adds `outcome` (`changed`, `lookedUp`, `handedOff`, or `failed`) to its `tool_result`, and the harness adds `turnIndex`, from 0, to its `tool_call`, whose `input` leaves out the `confirmation` the harness keeps (a model call to `undo_last_change` reaches the app as the inverse of the newest change, an ordinary `tool_call` whose `toolUseID` is the model's call ID plus `-undo`), sends `voice_line_if_every_call_changes` (`spokenLine`, and `toolNames`, the turn's calls in order) before the first `tool_call` of a turn whose calls carry a confirmation, ends it with `voice_command_finished` (`spokenLine`, `gaveUp`) or `task_failed`, and logs one `Brain: turn N on <model> stopped for <reason>, tokens in N, cache read N, cache write N, out N, N ms, calls <names>` line per turn, where `<names>` are the names of every tool the turn called, in order, joined with commas, or `none`. The app never sees a call after a hand-off or a call to a tool the command was not offered, so this line is the only record of them, and it holds names only, never a call's input. A command runs in its own goroutine next to the tasks and never waits on the MCP servers, and its session is described under the harness flag above. Three bench-only messages, `reset_session` (no fields, empties the session) and `seed_session` (`messages`, the Anthropic user, assistant, and tool result messages of earlier commands, and an optional `interruptedBefore` list of action lines, refused with a stderr line when a message is empty or a `tool_use` has no `tool_result` in the next message), and `seed_approval_request` (`approvalID`, `capability`, which puts a waiting request in the approval gate with no task, so a voice answer has one to settle), work only when `CLICKY_BENCH=1` is in the harness's environment. `bench/harnessProcess.ts` sets it for the brain evals, and `agentHarnessEnvironment` never passes it, so a harness without it drops these lines with a note on stderr. The harness sends `ready` once, `tool_call` (`toolUseID`, `name`, `input`), `approval_request` (`taskID`, `approvalID`, a random `approval-` and 32 hex digits, so a new harness process never repeats an ID the app may still hold, `capability`: `sends`, `deletes`, `spends`, or `reads_private`, and `summary` in plain words), `approval_settled` (`approvalID`, `approved`, `scope`, the answer the gate took for a request, a click's or a voice answer's, sent once per request), `task_finished` (`spokenSummary`), `task_failed` (`reason`), and `log` (`message`, and the command's `taskID` on the one line the app acts on, `Brain: planning on <model>`). The harness reads `CLICKY_WORKER_BASE_URL`, `CLICKY_APP_SECRET`, `CLICKY_SKILLS_DIRS` (the skills folders, joined with `:`, the learner's first), and `CLICKY_MCP_CONFIG` (the path of `mcp.json`) from its environment, which the app sets to just those plus `PATH`, `HOME`, `USER`, `LOGNAME`, `TMPDIR`, `LANG`, and `SHELL`, so none of the developer's Anthropic credentials reach the Worker (`agentHarnessEnvironment`), and writes only protocol lines to stdout, with debug text on stderr. Several tasks run at once, each in its own goroutine, and the calls of one turn go out together and come back as one user message. A cancel sends nothing more for that task, and the harness exits when stdin closes. The app parses each line at the boundary into `HarnessMessage` and drops and logs a line it cannot read. `HarnessTaskRouter` hands each message to its task's event stream, and an `AgentHarness` whose process exited fails every task it had and launches a fresh process for the next task. The bundled binary is a universal build with no cgo, so it resolves DNS with Go's own resolver and does not read macOS proxy settings.
**Agent tokens**: each running agent, and each finished agent whose result waits, is a 22 point disc floating in the menu bar row just right of the notch, and no words float beside it. The ending chip speaks for it, above the cursor with its tail on the triangle.
A disc is an opaque dark ring track (`DS.Colors.agentTokenTrack`) filled in the color the agent took from its theme, around a face: a roster player's portrait (initials in the theme's accent without the portrait files, `RosterFaceView`), a saved theme's picture, or a triangle in the agent's color. It has a 1.5 point dark edge and a soft shadow, so it reads on a light menu bar. A finished agent gets a green check badge, a failed one a red cross badge on a gray face in a red ring, and one waiting for the learner's OK an amber "!" in place of its face, a notch in its ring, and a soft pulse (`agentTokenBadge` still names the "!" badge, which no surface draws now, and the disc draws only a finished or failed agent's badge, `agentTokenBadgeOnTheDisc`).
Under Clicky blue every agent has its own color, so three task agents are never three identical tokens. `BackgroundAgentStore` picks the color when an agent starts and the agent keeps it for its whole life (`BackgroundAgent.tokenColor`, `agentPaletteColorToAssign`, tested in `AgentTokenTests.swift`). The agent takes its kind's color (study guide amber, 3D model violet, experiment lime, task teal) when no other running agent or waiting result has it. Otherwise it takes the first free color from the fixed palette `AgentPaletteColor`: teal `#2dd4bf`, amber `#FFB020`, violet `#a78bfa`, lime `#84cc16`, sky `#38bdf8`, pink `#f472b6`, and past six in use it cycles through the palette in order. The palette has no red, which means failed, and no green, which is the done check. An agent in its 4 second ending has given its color up, and a team player's color is its team's accent, which is never a palette color, so it takes none away from the others. The agent shows its color in its token ring and face triangle, in its ending chip at the cursor (`VoiceToolCallChipSender.backgroundAgent(color:face:)`), and in its row in the agent list. Another theme replaces the fixed palette with its own agent colors or its accent, and may give the agent its picture or a roster player as a face (see **Theme Studio**).
Each disc is its own borderless nonactivating panel at level `.mainMenu + 3` (`AgentTokenDockController`, `AgentTokenDiscView`), 28 points wide and as tall as the menu bar, so a click anywhere between two discs lands on one, and clicks elsewhere reach the menu bar. A panel never becomes key, so Chrome keeps the keyboard.
Position rules are pure functions in `AgentTokenLayout.swift`, tested in `AgentTokenTests.swift`. `agentsWithTokens` ranks the agents: running first, then waiting results newest first, then tokens in their 4 second ending. `agentTokenLayout` takes the tokens each display showed last time, the ranked agents, and how many discs fit, which is at most 4. When the agents fit in the first `min(3, positions)`, all show. Otherwise the last position is a "+N" disc and the tokens fill the ones before it. A token that shows keeps its position until it leaves, and the ones after it close the gap. Rank only decides who fills an open position, so a higher ranked newcomer waits in "+N". Only a smaller free space, or the "+N" disc taking the last position, makes the last token give way.
A finished agent whose result waits (a 3D model or experiment not yet shown, a task answer not yet heard) keeps its token until the result reaches the learner or 10 minutes pass (`BackgroundAgent.resultIsWaitingToBeDelivered`, `BackgroundAgentStore.setResultIsWaiting`, which `CompanionManager` calls when a scene rises, when "hear the answer" speaks, and when a newer result replaces an older one). For a task, only hearing the answer delivers it, so opening its page keeps the token. A result delivered at once, such as a study guide page, and a failed agent keep their token for the 4 second chip, then it leaves.
Free space runs from the notch's right edge, from `NSScreen.auxiliaryTopLeftArea` and `auxiliaryTopRightArea`, or from 8 points past the front app's last menu title when its menus continue past the notch, to the first status icon. The dock reads the menu titles from the front app's accessibility `AXMenuBar` each time the front app changes, off the main thread, and finds the first status icon from the on-screen windows at `CGWindowLevelForKey(.statusWindow)`, filtering by layer because Control Center owns them. While any token exists it refreshes once a second, so discs return when space reopens.
With no notch, discs start at the display's horizontal center or 8 points past the front app's last menu title, whichever is further right. Every display whose menu bar shows (`menuBarIsShowing`, from `visibleFrame`) shows its own discs. In a full-screen space macOS draws no app window in the strip beside the notch at any level, measured on a 16 inch MacBook Pro, so no disc shows there and endings use the cursor chip. Where not one disc fits, nothing floats.
A new token fades in at its position, and one that leaves fades out. With Reduce Motion on, it appears and goes with no animation, except that a token a tool started fades in (see **The serve** below). Hovering a disc grows it a little on the Snap move (not at all with Reduce Motion), shows the pointing hand, and after 0.3 seconds shows its peek card (below). Clicking a disc opens the agent list as a detached panel under it with that agent's row expanded, and "+N" opens it with no row expanded. A second click on the same disc closes it, and a click on the same agent's disc on another display moves the list there (`AgentTokenDiscID` names a disc by its display and its agent).
**Disc motion, peek, menu, and Bring back** (Tether PR 7, `AgentTokenViews.swift`, `AgentTokenPeek.swift`, `AgentTokenMenu.swift`, `AgentTokenBringBack.swift`): two loops are allowed on a disc, and both run on Core Animation (`AgentTokenLoopView`), so the render server plays them and a SwiftUI loop never pegs the GPU on a high-refresh display. A running agent's bright dot orbits its ring clockwise every 3 seconds, so a slow agent still looks alive, and a disc waiting for the learner's OK pulses a ring out once every 1.6 seconds (`AgentTokenLoop`). With Reduce Motion on, the dot only fades between 25 and 100 percent at the top of the ring, and the pulse is a still halo, so the "!" face and the notch carry the state. A disc's badge pops in on the Pop move, and its ring settles on the Snap move.
The peek is a Card (`.tetherCard()`) 8 points below the menu bar under a hovered disc, centered on it and kept 8 points inside the display (`agentTokenPeekCardFrame`). It glides 8 points into place on the Glide move, and with Reduce Motion it only fades. It never takes the mouse, so hovering it cannot end the hover that showed it, and a click, a right click, or the disc leaving hides it. It shows `agentTokenPeekContent`: the agent's kind and subject, its current step with the elapsed time (updated each second) and a meter of its steps, "Ready" lines that say what to say, a failed agent's reason cut to 80 characters, and for a waiting approval only its capability ("Needs your OK to send something") and "Click to review, or say yes or no", never the request's summary, since a page or a server may have written it. The store keeps each waiting agent's oldest request capability in `approvalCapabilitiesByAgentID` for it. The "+N" disc's card says how many agents it stands for.
A right click on a disc pops a menu of what the agent list's row offers (`agentDiscMenuItems`): Review request while an approval waits, Show model or Show experiment and Hear answer for a waiting result (held while a quiz question is open, like the list's buttons), Open page, and after a separator Cancel agent for a running one. The "+N" disc, and an agent with nothing to offer, show "Open agent list". The items run the closures `CompanionManager` also gives the list (`AgentDiscMenuActions`).
Bring back plays when an agent ends and its ending chip shows (`CompanionManager.bringBackTheEndingChip`, `AgentTokenDockController.bringBack`): a 10 point white spark with a ring in the agent's color runs from its disc up to the screen's top edge and along it to an elbow 100 points past the cursor (the other side when the cursor is within 120 points of the screen's right edge), then drops to the cursor glyph and ends there, on the move's 0.6 seconds with the first 40 percent along the edge (`agentTokenBringBackPath`, `CarrierFlightPlan.bringBack`). The run along the edge stays off the page, and only the drop to the cursor passes over it, since the route does not read where the video is. A tether in the agent's color traces the route behind the spark (`tetherFollowsThePath`), fading 250 ms after the landing. The chip shows as the spark lands, so it pops in where the spark ends, on the Callout's Pop. With Reduce Motion on, a still ring fades in at the cursor over 0.2 seconds and holds for 0.3 (`bringBackWithReduceMotion`), and the chip follows. An agent with no disc on the mouse's display, such as one that went to "+N", shows its chip at once, and a push-to-talk press lands the spark (`finishTheServeAtOnce`, called before the chip's Recall), so the chip it carries shows and shrinks into the cursor like any chip already up. A new serve lands only an earlier serve, never a spark in the air. The approval-waiting chip has no Bring back.
**Menu bar icon** (`MenuBarStatusIcon.swift`): the status item is 26 points wide in every state. It shows the shown theme's cursor (`themeSettings.shownTheme.cursor`) as a template silhouette, so a preview shows on it too: the triangle, a drawn cursor with its closed outlines kept and its open lines, such as a volleyball's seams, cut out, and the triangle for a picture cursor. The states are idle (also while Clicky speaks), listening (the cursor with two sound waves), and thinking (a small cursor with a dot that orbits once every 1.2 seconds at ten frames a second, a timer that redraws only a 26 by 18 image, held at the top with Reduce Motion). A state change crossfades in 150 ms. While an approval waits, an amber dot with a dark rim sits on the icon's top right over any state. macOS draws only a status item's image and never a subview of its button, so the badge state is a colored image, not a template, drawn in white or black from the button's appearance (`effectiveAppearance`, observed), and fades in, with no pop. The app logs `Menu bar icon: frame ..., on screen ..., visible ...` a second after launch, since a status item can sit behind the notch when the menu bar is full, and the Debug tool server's `debug_click_menu_bar_icon` clicks the button as the learner does (`verify-clicky.sh menu-icon click`), which is how a check opens the panel when no script can find the icon's window in the window list.
**Tether tokens and the carrier**: the Tether redesign (`docs/plans/tether-build.md`) draws every surface from a small set of tokens, and later Tether PRs adopt them surface by surface instead of writing their own sizes, colors, springs, and durations.
- Type: `DS.TypeScale` has four sizes in six styles: `headline` 17/700 rounded, `voice` 15/700 rounded (anything Clicky says on a Callout), `title` 13/600, `body` 13/400, `caption` 11/500, and `data` 11 monospaced. Use it as `Text("Popping it out").font(DS.TypeScale.voice.font)`, and set a Data string with `Text(verbatim:)` so a URL does not turn into a link. Swift reads `DS.Type` as the metatype of `DS`, so the scale has this name.
- Theme motion: a theme's `motion.springs` (`ThemeMoveSprings`, optional, so every older file parses) restyles three moves, Pop and Glide as springs and Point as a speed, since the pointing flight is a distance-paced smoothstep and not a spring: a flight lasts `response / 0.5` times its usual 0.6 to 1.4 s. Clicky's own are Pop 0.32 and 0.68, Glide 0.42 and 0.9, and Point 0.5, and every bundled theme keeps them by writing no `springs`. The limits are Pop response 0.2 to 0.45 and damping 0.55 to 1, Glide response 0.3 to 0.55 and damping 0.75 to 1, and Point response 0.3 to 0.6, so the slowest spring settles in 0.81 s (a spring settles in about `0.989 * response / damping` seconds) and the bounciest Pop overshoots 12.6 percent and the bounciest Glide 2.8 percent of the distance. `ClickyTheme.parse` and the Worker's `checkThemeIdeas` move a number outside its limit to the limit and refuse a value that is not a number, and `worker/src/testdata/theme-spring-limits.json` is read by a test on each side, so the two cannot clamp differently. `DS.Move.themeSprings` holds the shown theme's springs (`CompanionManager` keeps it in step through `CompanionThemeSettings.observeShownMoveSprings`, which logs `Theme motion: ...` on each change, so the studio's preview moves the whole app, and the Xcode test host runs it too, so a test that wants Clicky's own springs reads `curve(using: .standard)`), `DS.Move.curve` reads it, `curve(using:)` and `animation(reduceMotionIsOn:springs:)` take springs explicitly, and the Theme Studio's tiles use their own idea's Pop (a tile springs up when picked, so a bouncy idea visibly overshoots). Reduce Motion ignores the springs, since a move's Reduce Motion form is a fade.
- Moves: `DS.Move` names the ten moves, `send`, `bringBack`, `point`, `rise`, `returnToSource`, `pop`, `glide`, `snap`, `recall`, and `shake`. Each has a `curve` (`MoveCurve`: a spring, a timed bezier, a time paced by distance, or a decaying shake) and a `reduceMotionForm` (`ReducedMotionForm`: a 0.15 second crossfade, a 0.2 second fade at the target that leaves a still ring for the flights, or nothing for the shake). Animate a SwiftUI change with `withAnimation(DS.Move.pop.animation(reduceMotionIsOn: reduceMotion)) { ... }`, which is nil for the shake and gives the Reduce Motion fade when it is on, and drop the scale or travel yourself when Reduce Motion is on. A Glide close plays `DS.Move.glide.curve.timeScaled(by: DS.Move.glideCloseTimeFactor)`. The shapes of the moves are constants next to them (`popStartScale`, `glideStartScale`, `snapPressScale`, `recallEndScale`, `pointStretch`, `fadeSeconds`, `tetherLineWidth`, `tetherFadeSeconds`), and `MoveBezier` holds the beziers (`smoothstep`, `quadraticEaseIn`, `cubicEaseInOut`) that CSS, SwiftUI, and Core Animation all take. The doc comment on `DS.Move` maps each of today's timings to the move it becomes.
- Materials (`TetherMaterials.swift`): `.tetherCard()` draws a view on the Card (a `#121419` to `#171a21` surface, a 1 point white edge at 8%, an inner top highlight, a two-layer shadow, radius 14, and no padding of its own). `TetherCallout(accent:rim:icon:content:)` is the Callout: a solid `#14171e` pill with an accent rim (1.5 points for `.speech`, 1 point for `.chip`) and halo, a 26 point accent icon disc with near-black ink on every accent (`DS.Colors.inkOnAccent`), a tail at its bottom left toward the cursor, and its words in white `voice` type. Both read only the accent the caller passes from the theme (`shownTheme.cursorColor`) or a status line's fixed hue, never a team name. `TetherCalloutShape.tailTip(inCalloutOfSize:)` is the point a Pop or a Recall scales about.
- Callouts (`TetherCalloutViews.swift`): speech, status lines, tool chips, agent endings, and errors are all a `TetherCallout`, drawn above the cursor with its tail's tip 12 points above the triangle (`tetherCalloutPanelOrigin` for the chip and status panels, `speechCalloutCenter` for the overlay). A Callout near the right edge slides left and near the top slides down. The accent is the theme's `cursorColor` for speech and a voice tool chip (the chip reads it through `VoiceToolCallChip.themeAccentProvider`, which `CompanionManager.start` sets to the shown theme), the agent's own color for an agent ending, a status line's fixed hue (`LiveSceneStatusLine.calloutAccent`, the 400 step of its color, since the hue says what kind of work runs), and red with a cross for a failure (`VoiceToolCallChipSender.failure`, `DS.Colors.destructiveText`). Speech has a 1.5 point rim, and chips and status lines are 1.5 and 1 (`TetherCalloutRim`). Every Callout appears with a Pop about its tail's tip (`.tetherCallout` transition), leaves with a 6 point rise and a fade in 0.7 of the Pop's time, and shrinks into the cursor in 180 ms when a push-to-talk press interrupts it (a Recall: `LiveSceneStatusLabel.hide()` with no set, and `VoiceToolCallChip.recall()`, which leaves up a chip that waits for the learner's answer, so an approval request's chip stays). The status label and the chip stay in the view tree while they leave, with the leave as a pose (`TetherCalloutPresence`), so the exit is chosen before it starts. The speech Callouts enter and leave the tree with a transition (`.tetherCalloutPopAndFadeRise`). The pointing bubble's leave is chosen by `.tetherCalloutPop(andLeave:)`: a push-to-talk press while the cursor points for a narration notes an interruption (`PointingBubbleExitRecord`), and the overlay takes that note once as it hides the bubble, so the bubble shrinks into the cursor. SwiftUI removes a view with the transition it had on the last render, so the overlay sets the Recall one turn before the bubble leaves, and the cursor's flight back waits for that turn too. Only a held pointing leaves the note, a flight still on its way to the part stops at once, and when that turn comes `pointingBubbleRecallStep` decides: fly back to the mouse, leave the buddy to a new pointing this screen flies to, or give way to a new pointing on another display. With Reduce Motion on, every Callout only fades in place over 0.15 s. A failure chip shakes once as it appears (`TetherCalloutShake`, `DS.Move.shake`), and with Reduce Motion the red and the cross carry it. A chip follows the cursor at 30 Hz while it shows, from its measured width, and sits above a status line that is up, so neither covers the other. A failure Callout (red, with a cross) shows for a failed agent, a build or router failure, a failed save or design, a failed animation, a theme that could not be added, and a quiz summary question Clicky could not go back to. The speech Callout wraps by measured width at 300 points, shows at most 3 lines, and a longer sentence scrolls a line at a time while the last complete line stays above the one being spoken (`SpeechCalloutLayout`). A last line of one word takes the word above it when the two fit. The status lines keep their seven animated icons, drawn in ink on the accent disc, and share one letter motion: each letter rises 6 points, 18 ms after the one before, then the line sits still (`StatusLineLetterRise`). Chips and status lines do not stack: a new chip replaces the old one in place, where the spec has the old one pushed up.
- The carrier (`TetherCarrier.swift`): SwiftUI cannot animate across windows, so a move that crosses them plays on `TetherCarrier.shared`, one click-through, nonactivating panel per display at `.screenSaver + 2` that covers its display and shows only while a move plays there. A move is a `CarrierFlightPlan` of poses (pivot, scale, opacity) sampled at 120 a second from the move's curve, built by `CarrierFlightPlan.send(along:carriedSize:)`, `.glideFromOrigin(_:into:)` (the Card grows out of the point of its frame nearest the origin), or `.glideClose(from:into:)` (the Card going back into its origin, ending hidden, which is not `DS.Move.returnToSource`, the pop-out's flatten into its video). Play one with `TetherCarrier.shared.play(.glideFromOrigin(discCenter, into: listFrame), carrying: someView, onDisplay: displayID, tetherColor: NSColor(theme.cursorColor), onLanded: { showTheRealList() })`, which returns a `CarrierFlight` whose `landAtOnce()` ends the move where it is, as a push-to-talk press does. The carrier draws the view in the plan's resting frame and moves it with Core Animation keyframes, and calls `CATransaction.flush()` right after adding them, since a main thread that blocked later in the same turn otherwise held the commit back and ate 400 ms of a half second flight in the phase-zero prototype. It draws a solid 1 point tether line in `tetherColor` from the origin to the moving pivot on the same clock, which fades 250 ms after the landing. A plan with `tetherFollowsThePath` draws the line along the route the pivot took, from the origin up to the pivot (a Bring back needs it), and `CarrierFlight.endTheTetherNow()` takes the line off at once, which the dock calls when a disc that waited under a landed token slides, so the line never ends where the disc used to be. On landing it calls `onLanded`, keeps the carried view over the real surface for 0.05 seconds (only the main thread removes it), then calls `onGone`. A move stays on one display, since a window draws only on its own display, and several moves may play at once. With Reduce Motion on, do not use the carrier: apply the move's `reduceMotionForm` to the real window. The plans, the layer translation that places a pose (`carrierLayerTranslation`), and the tether's keyframes (`carrierTetherLines`) are tested in `TetherCarrierTests.swift`, and the tokens in `TetherTokensTests.swift`.

**Pausing every agent** (Tether PR 16, `AgentTokenPause.swift`, `harness/pause.go`): "hold on" pauses every background agent, and "go ahead" lets them go on.
The router offers `pause_agents` while an agent runs and no pause holds, and `resume_agents` while one holds and no request the learner can answer by voice waits, and it takes every command while an agent runs or a pause holds, even with nothing on screen and no video in front (`voiceCommandRouterTakesTheTranscript`).
The scene state's `backgroundAgents` says how many run and whether they are paused, such as `"2 running"` or `"2 paused, and a new agent waits for go ahead too"` (`backgroundAgentsStateText`), so a confirmation for an agent started during a pause says when it starts.
"Go ahead" is also a yes to a waiting approval request, so while one waits the learner answers it first and resumes after, and a resume never doubles as an approval (`agentsPausedApprovalWaiting` in the evals).
A bare "hold on" means the agents, never the video, and "hold on, what is that" is the question it starts, which `bench/fixtures/brain-evals.json` pins in the `agentsRunning`, `agentsRunningNoVideo`, `agentsPaused`, and `agentsPausedApprovalWaiting` contexts.
The pause is `BackgroundAgentStore.agentsArePaused`, set by `pauseEveryAgent` and `resumeEveryAgent`, and `CompanionManager.setEveryAgentIsPaused` runs both tools.
A pause really pauses: no model turn and no tool call starts, and no result is delivered, until the resume.
- A task agent's harness task gets `pause_task`, and its `pauseGate` holds its next turn, each tool call of a turn that finished during the pause, and each new approval request.
  A request or a call already in flight finishes, and its result waits with the task.
  The harness logs `Task agent: paused by the learner`, `Task agent: holding turn N until the learner resumes`, and `Task agent: resumed by the learner`.
- A study guide, 3D model, or experiment agent awaits `waitWhileAgentsArePaused` before its Worker call and before its ending, a finish or a failure, so no chip, sound, page, or rising scene comes during a pause.
  What it reads on the Mac, the frame, the front tab, and the captions, it reads at once, so an agent started during a pause still builds from what the learner asked about.
  A task agent's tool call that reached the app just before its `pause_task` waits too.
  An approval request that reached the app just before it shows at once, like one already waiting, since "go ahead" answers it first.
  No unit test covers where these holds sit in `CompanionManager`, since that code has no seam for one, so the app log's `Agents: holding <step> until the learner resumes` lines are their evidence.
  A Worker reply that arrives during it waits, and a study guide's page is saved only after the resume.
- An approval already waiting stays waiting, and the learner can still answer it.
  The approval desk's 120 second timeout keeps running, so a pause longer than that declines it, as it would with no pause.
  The call it allows waits for the resume.
- An agent started during a pause starts paused (`pausedAt` set at its start, and `start_task` with `startsPaused`, since a later `pause_task` could arrive after the harness's first request).
- Push-to-talk, the voice brain, and voice commands never pause.
Each agent's progress stops at `pausedAt`, its ring and the list's meter stand still, and a resume moves `currentStepStartedAt` later by the pause's length, so the progress picks up where it stopped.
Its elapsed time stops where the pause began (`elapsedTimeStoppedAt`, which a step that finishes during the pause does not move), and the pause's length goes into `secondsSpentPaused` at the resume or the agent's end, so the time goes on with no jump.
The panel home's agents row says "N paused · say go ahead", or "N paused · answer the request first" while a request waits for the learner's OK (`panelAgentsSummary`).
Cancelling every running agent from the list ends the pause, since nothing is left to hold.
The discs fold into one paused disc (`AgentTokenSlot.paused`, `agentTokenSlotsLeftToRight`): a gray ring around two pause bars, with the count of running agents it holds in a capsule badge when there is more than one (`AgentPausedDiscView`, `pausedAgentCount`).
Its peek says "N agents paused" and "Say go ahead to let them go on", or "Answer the request first, then say go ahead" while one waits for the learner's OK, VoiceOver reads the same two lines, and a click opens the agent list.
The panel home's agent chips keep their color during a pause, and the keyboard focus a chip gives a disc does not move to the paused disc or come back to a disc after "go ahead", which PR 19 takes.
"Hold on" grays every disc over the crossfade time, then slides each disc's panel into the paused disc's place as it shrinks to `DS.Move.recallEndScale` and fades, on the Recall move's curve (`AgentTokenGather`), and the paused disc fades in.
"Go ahead" sends each disc from the paused disc back to the place it had, on the Send move along the menu bar with a tether in the agent's color, since the dock keeps each display's token positions unchanged through the pause, and a push-to-talk press, a new pause, or a Bring back lands the flights at once (`finishTheServeAtOnce`).
With Reduce Motion on, both are a crossfade over `DS.Move.fadeSeconds` (`agentTokenPauseMove`).
An agent started during a pause gets no serve, since its token has no slot of its own, and it folds into the paused disc's count.

**The serve**: when a tool starts an agent, that agent's own token (the same ring and face its disc shows, in its assigned or team color) flies from the blue cursor glyph into its slot in half a second, on a quadratic arc like the cursor's pointing flight but bowed sideways, with a 1 point tether line in the cursor's color from the cursor glyph to the token, so the learner sees the cursor hand the work off. This is the Send move (see **Tether tokens and the carrier**). The cursor stays with the mouse. It covers `start_background_task`, `build_model_in_background`, `build_simulation`, the direct "let me try it" path, and `make_study_guide` and `revise_study_guide`. A study guide refresh after a quiz miss (`StudyGuideJob.isAskedForByTheLearner` is false) has no request and no confirmation, so its token just appears.
`AgentTokenServe.swift` holds the pure rules, tested in `AgentTokenServeTests.swift`. `agentTokenServePlan(cursorPoint:slotPoint:slotDisplayCanShowDiscs:reduceMotionIsOn:)` returns `.fly(path)`, `.fadeInAtTheSlot`, or `.none`. `.none` covers an agent with no slot of its own (it went to "+N") and a display where no disc can show (no free space, a hidden menu bar, or a full-screen space). Reduce Motion gives `.fadeInAtTheSlot`, which fades the disc in over 0.2 seconds and leaves a still ring in the tether's color around it for 1 second (`agentTokenServeStillRingSeconds`), the move's Reduce Motion form. `agentTokenServePath` bows the arc sideways to the straight line by a fifth of the distance at its control point, at most 80 points, toward the top of the screen (to the right for a flight straight up), and never above its higher end, so a cursor near the menu bar does not send the token off the display. The token eases with smoothstep, like the cursor's flight. The start is the cursor glyph, which `BlueCursorView` places 35 points right and 25 below the mouse (`agentTokenServeStartPoint`). The token flies to the display the mouse is on (`agentTokenServeDisplayID`), and the discs on other displays fade in as usual.
`AgentTokenDockController.startServingTheToken(of:)` runs in the same turn as `backgroundAgentStore.start`, before the dock has made the disc. It makes the discs at once, with the disc on the mouse's display as an open slot, a dashed outline (`AgentOpenSlotView`) that stays hidden until the plan is known. The dock plays `CarrierFlightPlan.send` on the shared carrier (`TetherCarrier`) of the mouse's display, carrying the same disc view the dock shows (`AgentTokenDiscContent`), with the shown theme's cursor color for the tether. Core Animation keyframes move the disc, so the render server plays it: the build reads Chrome through Apple Events on the main thread right after the agent starts, and a main thread timer froze the token mid-flight and then jumped. When it lands, the open slot becomes the token, and the carried token leaves 0.05 seconds later. A disc ahead of the slot can leave while the token flies, so the discs behind it close the gap by sliding left. The disc under a flight, or under the carried token in that 0.05 seconds, waits and slides once the carried token has left (`agentTokenDiscMustWaitForItsFlight`), so no copy of the token shows at the old slot while the disc slides away. With Reduce Motion on, the disc fades in at its slot over the Send move's Reduce Motion time (`DS.Move.send.reduceMotionForm`, 0.2 seconds), with no ring yet.
The router's spoken confirmation, and the line the direct "let me try it" path says (`experimentStartedSpokenLine`), start after the landing (`waitForTheServeToLand`), and with no serve they start at once. A push-to-talk press during the flight lands the token in its slot at once (`finishTheServeAtOnce`), and the press also cancels the line that was waiting. The log says `Agent token serve:` for each case and how long the spoken line waited.
**Skills, MCP servers, and approvals**: a skill is `<name>.md` in `leanring-buddy/Skills/` (bundled, which Xcode's synchronized folders copy to the top of the app's `Resources`, so the app reads bundled skills from there) or `~/Library/Application Support/Clicky/Skills/` (the learner's, which wins). It starts with front matter between `---` lines, `name` (the file's name), `description` (one line that says when to use it), and an optional `tools` list. `start_task` with a `skill` puts the file's text in the task's first message, and a `tools` list limits the task to those tools plus `report_step` and `finish`. A missing or malformed skill, or a `tools` name the agent lacks, fails the task with a reason. The harness answers a call to any tool outside the set the task was offered with an "is not a tool" error, before it reaches the app or a server. `flashcards.md` is the bundled skill.
The voice router starts a skill through `start_background_task`, with no separate tool. `TaskAgentSkillCatalog` (`TaskAgentSkills.swift`) reads every `*.md` in the two skills folders each time the router builds its tools, so a skill added later shows up with no relaunch. It parses only `name` and `description`, by the same rules as `parseSkill` in `harness/skills.go`, and skips a file with no front matter, no closing line, a line that is not `key: value`, no description, no text after the front matter, a `name` that is not its file name, or a name that is not 1 to 40 lowercase letters, digits, and hyphens with no leading hyphen. The learner's file wins on a name, and a learner's file that is not whole still claims its name, since the harness reads that folder first and would fail the task. A description is cut to 200 characters, and at most 10 skills are listed, sorted by name. With skills, `start_background_task` lists each as `name: description` in its description and takes `skill`, an enum of those names (`AppVoiceTool.descriptor(skills:)`). The router prompt in `worker/src/voiceCommand.ts` tells the model to pass a skill when the request matches one, and the Worker accepts tool descriptions up to 4096 characters for the list. The app reads the catalog again when the call arrives, and a name it does not list is dropped with a log line and the task runs with no skill (`skillNameToRun`). A skill task starts with the step line "Using the flashcards skill" (`startingStepText`), which the agent list shows before the agent's first reported step. `TaskAgentSkillsTests` and `harness/skills_test.go` both read the real `flashcards.md` and assert the same name and description.
`~/Library/Application Support/Clicky/mcp.json` has the shape of Claude Code's `mcpServers`: `command`, `args`, and `env` for a stdio server, or `url` and `headers` for a streamable HTTP server, with `type` optional. The harness (the official Go MCP SDK, v1.3.1, no cgo) connects once when the first task starts, in parallel, skips a server that fails with a log line, and offers each tool as `mcp__<server>__<tool>`, skipping any name Anthropic would refuse. A stdio server gets only the baseline variables and its own `env`, never the Worker's secret. A stdio server still runs as the learner's user, with Clicky as its responsible process, so it can read the app's defaults and the pasteboard and may use Clicky's privacy grants, and adding one trusts it as much as Clicky. A server's descriptions and results are data, and the prompt says so. A result keeps its text up to 50,000 characters and at most 4 images of up to 3.75 MB each, since Anthropic refuses an image over 5 MB of base64, and a task keeps at most 12 MB of server images in all (`imageByteBudget`), since every result stays in the history and Anthropic refuses a request over 32 MB.
Each tool has a capability (`harness/approval.go`): `none`, `sends`, `deletes`, `spends`, or `reads_private`. `take_screenshot` and `read_clipboard` are `reads_private`, because a page-steered agent could read them and then send the data out. `open_url` and `write_clipboard` are `sends`, because a link can carry data in its address and a planted clipboard text can be pasted later. `report_step`, `read_video_captions`, `look_at_video` (cropped to the video), and `write_notion_page` (a private page in the learner's own Notion) are `none`. An MCP tool takes `reads_private` from `readOnlyHint`, since a read only tool can read the learner's files or mail and its input can carry what another tool read, `deletes` from `destructiveHint`, and `sends` otherwise, so no server tool runs without approval, and a server's annotations are its own claims. Before a tool with a capability runs, the harness sends `approval_request` and the call waits in the state `approvalAwaitingAnswer`, which ends in given, declined, or abandoned when the task ends. Approvals run one call at a time in the turn's order, and a tool with no capability starts first. `always` covers that tool at that capability for the rest of that one task, so a later task asks again, and the harness drops the task's grants when it ends, and a no is never remembered. A declined call gets an error result that says the learner declined. The harness reads each call's input once, keeping numbers as their digits, and the app or server receives that same object encoded again, so the length check, the summary, and the run read the same bytes even when a key is written twice. The summary is built by the harness from the call's own input and shows all of it: a link's host in Punycode on its own line and then the whole link, clipboard text whole, and every MCP argument as a `key: value` line with the required ones first. A newline shows as `⏎`, a tab as `\t`, and any format or unprintable character as `\u{XXXX}`. A clipboard text over 500 characters, an MCP input over 1,500, or an input that is not a JSON object is refused with an error result and no approval request, since it could not be shown in full.
The app holds each `approval_request` in the approval desk (`HarnessApprovalDesk`) until the learner answers it, its task ends, or 120 seconds pass, which declines it with a log line.
Requests wait in arrival order, and the learner answers the oldest first.
A request whose task already ended, such as one that crossed a cancel, is dropped (`taskIsRunning`), and a request for a capability the app does not know is declined at once.
The log names only the approval ID and the capability, since a summary can hold clipboard text or data from the screen.
When one arrives, "Pop" plays once, nothing is spoken, and the agent list opens next to the mouse (`AgentListPanelController.openForAnApprovalRequest`) at the first corner around the mouse where it does not cover the pointer (`approvalListAnchorTopLeft`), unless it is already open or the menu bar panel is open on its home, whose banner shows the card.
The oldest request shows in an approval card pinned under the list's header, above the agent rows, so no scroll position hides it (`ApprovalCardView`, the Tether approval card).
The card's title is the agent's name and the verb of its capability ("Hinata wants to send something out"), on an amber disc with one icon per capability, over a line that says what that kind of action can do (`approvalCardVerbPhrase`, `approvalCardKindLine`).
Under it, "You said, when it started:" quotes the learner's own push-to-talk transcript, which `startTheTaskAgent` stores on the agent (`BackgroundAgent.learnerWords`), cut to two lines. It never shows the router's goal, which page text can steer, and a card for an agent no spoken request started has no "You said".
The card shows the harness's whole summary as plain monospaced text, one row per line (`approvalSummaryRows`), never cut, scrolling inside the card past 200 points.
A rail beside the summary fills in amber each part that has been in view, gaps included, so a jump to the end does not look read.
Below it, a status line says where the card is (`approvalCardStatusText`), then, while the request waits, three equal buttons with the safe one first: Don't allow, Allow once, and For this task (the tool at that capability for the rest of this task), with no red or green, over the line "Or say yes or no. For this task lasts until the agent finishes.", since the panel is never key and a tooltip would not show. "Allow for this task" does not fit at the list's 300 points, so the third button says "For this task".
Don't allow counts at once, before the summary was read (instant No). A wrong no costs the agent a retry, while a wrong yes can send data out. It is never dimmed, because the buttons go once an answer is on its way.
Until a yes would count, Allow once and For this task are dimmed and ignore clicks, and the status line says "Read the whole request to allow it" while the summary has not been shown whole, then shows a ring that fills over the 1 second with "All read · one moment", or "Ready in 1 s" in words under Reduce Motion, then "You can answer now" (`approvalAnswerReadiness`, `approvalReadyClockProgress`, which the desk's own check calls too, so the buttons never look ready when a click would be ignored).
VoiceOver hears "Ready to answer" when the yes buttons enable, and the outcome when the card ends. The buttons use plain `.focusable()`, so Tab reaches them on a surface that takes the keyboard with Full Keyboard Access off.
The card records each range of the summary that has been inside its scroll area (`ApprovalSummarySeenRanges`), measured with `onGeometryChange` (a preference reported only zeros on macOS 26 in the Tether prototype), and tells the desk once those ranges cover the summary from top to bottom (`approvalSummaryWasShownWhole`, with 1 point of tolerance between ranges), so a learner who drags straight to the end of a long summary has not seen the middle and does not reach that state.
Each surface that shows the card (`ApprovalCardSurface`: `agentList` and the menu bar panel's `menuPanel`) keeps its own seen ranges and its own clock (`wholeSummaryShownSinceBySurface`), which starts when that surface's card has shown the whole summary and stops when the surface closes or another request becomes the oldest, the same moments the seen ranges start over. The list builds a new card each time it opens, so a reopened list measures again from the top.
A yes by click counts only once that surface's clock has run for 1 second (`secondsTheCardShowsBeforeAnAnswerCounts`, `answerByClick(approvalID:approved:scope:on:)`), and a yes by voice only when any surface's had at the press (`approvalAtPress(at:)`), since the list opens next to the mouse, a click meant for the page could otherwise be taken as Yes, and a summary on screen for an instant is one nobody read.
The card shows only what the desk knows (`cardToShow(on:at:)`, `ApprovalCardPhase`): waiting, then "Sending your answer…" with a spinner until `approval_settled`, then how it ended for 2 seconds when no other request waits (`lastEndedCard`, `ApprovalCardEnding`): allowed once, allowed for this task, declined, declined after 2 minutes with no answer (the desk's own timeout), or "Sent, but the agent never confirmed it." (no `approval_settled` within 15 seconds). A request waiting behind it shows at once instead, and an ended card goes when its task ends.
An agent whose request is not the oldest says "Needs your OK after the one above" in its row, and an answer moves the card to the next request.
When a request becomes the oldest while neither the list nor the menu bar panel's home shows the card, such as after the one before it timed out, the list opens for it (`onOldestRequestNeedsShowing`), since until then no screen shows its summary.
When the harness process stops, every request is dropped at once (`dropAllRequests`), so the list never opens for a request that is about to go.
While a request waits, the agent's row says "Needs your OK", and the chip above the cursor says "<agent> needs your OK · say yes or no" in the agent's color until it is answered.
A task that ends, fails, or is cancelled drops its requests with no answer, since the harness already abandoned them (`HarnessTaskRouter.onTaskEnded`).
The voice tool `answer_approval` (`approved`, `always`) answers the request the card showed at the push-to-talk press (`approvalAtThePress`, with a rehearsal step standing in for the press), only if that request still waits, and only in the model's first reply, since a later turn has read tool results. A no counts at once, and a no for an unread request needs a word of no in the transcript. A yes counts only when a card had shown the whole summary for a second at the press.
On the harness path (the default) the harness decides all of this but the press-time fact, as described under the harness flag, and the rest of this paragraph describes the old path's Swift checks, which `harness/voiceapproval.go` repeats word for word and which stay until the plan's last PR.
The router takes the command, offers `answer_approval`, and names the capability only for that request (`approvalAtThePressStillWaiting`), and a yes for a request not read at the press fails with a result that tells the model only a no counts yet.
A yes counts only when the whole transcript is a short answer, with a word of yes, no word of no, and no word outside the answer's small vocabulary, so "okay, what is this video about" is not a yes, and an always only when the learner said always (`approvalVoiceAnswerTheLearnerSaid`), since the router also reads page text such as a video title. An answer the words do not support is refused, not changed, so the chip never names an answer the learner did not give.
It is offered only while such a request waits, and the voice command router takes every command then, even with no video or scene in front (`approvalIsWaiting`), except a quiz answer or a scene still building.
"Yes", "sure", "go ahead", or "do it" sets `approved` true, "no", "don't", "stop", or "cancel" sets it false, and "always" sets `always` true, which counts only with a yes, and a bare "always" is a yes (`ApprovalVoiceAnswer`).
The router's scene state holds only `approvalWaiting`, the capability word (`sends`, `deletes`, `spends`, `reads_private`, or `unknown`), and never the summary, the tool's input, or an MCP tool name, since a web page or an MCP server may have written the summary and could talk the router into approving (`voiceCommandApprovalWaitingState`).
The chip says "Allowed it", "Allowed it for this task", or "Declined it" after the answer.
A Debug build's debug server `debug_answer_approval` tool (`approvalID`, `approved`, `scope`) answers through the same desk as the buttons.
**Menu bar panel** (Tether PR 8, `CompanionPanelView.swift`, `PanelHome.swift`, `SetupChecklist.swift`, `PanelControls.swift`, `ThemeCardView.swift`): the menu bar icon opens a 340 point Tether Card under it, the only Clicky window that takes the keyboard, so it is the keyboard and VoiceOver home for the agents, the waiting approval, every setting the settings dial offers, and the themes.
The header shows the shown theme's cursor on a dark tile, "Clicky", and a state line (`panelHeaderStatus`): "Ready · hold ⌃ ⌥ to talk", "Ready · 1 request needs you", Listening, Thinking, "Speaking · esc stops it", "Quiz question · answer out loud", "Quiz · 3 of 4 right" with a Stop quiz button while a quiz runs between questions ("Quiz · watching" before the first answer, `VideoQuizProgress`, which `VideoQuizSession.onProgressChanged` sends at the start, after each finished question, and as nil at the stop, and which `CompanionManager.videoQuizProgress` holds), or, ahead of all of those, "Needs attention" for a required setup step that is off and "Offline" for a Worker that cannot be reached.
Under it, one banner slot shows what needs the learner (`panelBanner`): the approval card (`ApprovalCardView` on the `menuPanel` surface, so Tab reaches its summary and buttons), then a required permission turned off after setup (Fix, which opens the setup page), then the Worker unreachable (Retry).
The banner slot is pinned under the header, above the page that scrolls, so the card never scrolls out of sight. The panel's card keeps its own seen ranges and clock, which the panel's close and a page change away from the home both discard, and a request still waiting then opens the agent list, since no other screen shows its summary (`CompanionManager.theMenuPanelStoppedShowingTheApprovalCard`). Each open makes a new card (`PanelPresentation.openCount` in its identity), so it measures the summary from the top again. A closed panel reports nothing to the desk. While the panel is open on its home, a new request plays its sound but opens no agent list over it, and the desk counts the panel as a surface that shows the card (`CompanionManager.menuPanelShowsTheApprovalCard`, read by `listIsShowing`).
`WorkerReachability` sends a GET to the Worker's root when the panel opens, and every 10 seconds while it gets no HTTP reply and the panel is open, and any HTTP status counts as reachable.
The agents row shows while an agent runs or a result waits (`panelAgentsSummary`, "2 working · 1 result waiting"), with a chip for each agent that has a disc, and its words open the agent list next to the mouse.
The discs in the menu bar never take the keyboard, so the chips stand in for them: a chip that holds the keyboard focus puts a light blue focus ring on its disc and shows the disc's peek card at once (`AgentTokenDockController.showTheKeyboardFocus(onTheDiscOf:)`), and a picked chip shows its peek lines and the disc's right-click menu items as buttons (`agentDiscMenuItems`, run through `AgentTokenDockController.run(_:forTheDiscOf:)`, which runs them for an agent with no disc on screen too, such as the fourth chip, and hands only Review request and Open agent list back to `CompanionManager`, which opens the list next to the mouse). The panel closes after the item runs, so its close finds a list the item opened. The disc takes the ring only when the keyboard moved the focus, and a chip that loses the focus takes the ring and the peek card off its own disc only (`endTheKeyboardFocus(onTheDiscOf:)`), so a peek card the mouse opened stays. The row's one-second clock runs only while the panel is open.
Then the Model row (Sonnet fast, Opus deeper), the Cursor row's Show Clicky switch, the Theme row with "All N themes ›", up to five theme cards and a "Design one" card that opens the Theme Studio with the request "a new theme", the shortcut hints, and Send feedback, Setup, Replay intro, and Quit.
The home's cards are the three newest saved themes and then the bundled ones, with the selected theme always shown in the last card (`panelHomeThemes`), so a click never moves a card.
