A theme card draws the theme the way the Theme Studio's tiles draw an idea: its cursor at 1.6 times its size (the triangle at its pointer angle), running its thinking motion and pulsing streaks while the card is hovered or keyboard-focused, its name (one line in the title type, a long single word in the caption type, or two lines), its accent and agent colors as swatches, and a small agent token. The chosen card has the accent border, and the others sit at 75 percent until hovered or focused.
All themes shows the "Shuffle agents across themes" switch and every theme as the same cards, three to a row, in a scroll area.
In a card grid the arrow keys move the focus (`themeGridCardIndex`), and Return or Space picks. A focused card shows its full name in a pill over its swatch row.
Every control joins the Tab order with plain `.focusable()`, since a SwiftUI button joins it only with Full Keyboard Access on, runs on Return or Space, and shows a 2 point light blue ring (`DS.Colors.keyboardFocusRing`) only when the keyboard moved the focus (`theKeyboardMovedTheFocus`), so a click leaves no ring. Escape closes the panel unless a rename field takes it (`KeyablePanel.cancelOperation`), and the app's own Escape handler (`closeLiveSceneOnEscape`) then does nothing else, such as skipping a quiz question or clearing a scene: it returns while the panel holds the keyboard or for half a second after the panel closed on that Escape (`menuPanelTookTheEscape`), since the panel and the event tap see the key in either order.
VoiceOver reads the header as a heading and the state line in words, the banner as a group, each card as its name with "Selected theme" or "Theme" and Rename and Delete actions on a saved one, each setup row as "Step N of 6, <name>, <state>", and each agent chip and disc as its peek card's lines (`agentDiscAccessibilityLabel`). A chip at the cursor, which never takes the keyboard, is announced to VoiceOver as it shows (`announceForVoiceOver`).
Motion: the card Glides down from its top (scale 0.96 to 1) as it opens, with its rows rising 8 points 30 ms apart, the banner Glides open, and picking a theme Pops the new cursor into the header. A page change and the card's size follow on the Glide move, and the window refits to the card each time its size changes, keeping its top under the menu bar. With Reduce Motion on, the card and banner crossfade, the rows only fade, and the cursor appears.
Each open starts on the home, or on the setup page while setup is not finished. The home and setup pages scroll when they are taller than the screen allows under the header and the banner (`panelTallestPageHeight`), so the footer never runs off the bottom of the screen, and All themes scrolls its own grid instead, so no page nests two scroll areas. A theme card's motion stops while the panel is closed (`menuPanelIsOpen` in the environment), since a closed window sends no mouse exit.
**Setup**: a first run opens on "Set up Clicky", six steps in order (`SetupStep`): Microphone, Speech Recognition (Not needed when the speech provider does not use it), Screen Recording, Screen test (the one capture through the screen picker), Accessibility, and Chrome control (optional, so it never holds setup back).
Above them, before any macOS prompt, a paragraph names every capture and both places it goes (`setupCaptureDisclosure`), and a bar fills with the share of steps done.
Each row's state comes from what macOS reports with no prompt (`setupStepStates`): done, not needed, to do, turned off, or needs Chrome open. Screen Recording and Accessibility report only granted or not, so one the learner pressed this launch (`setupStepsAskedForThisLaunch`) that is still not granted reads as turned off.
A turned-off row turns red with the way to fix it (`setupStepButtons`): Open Settings, plus Quit and reopen for Screen Recording (which relaunches Clicky a second after it quits) and Find App for Accessibility, and Try again for a failed screen test.
The theme's cursor points at the next step's button and glides to the next row when that step is done (`nextSetupStep`), and a done step's number turns into a check drawn in 220 ms (shown whole with Reduce Motion).
Once every required step is done, a green box suggests a first question, and a first run's Start Clicky starts the intro (`finishSetup`, which calls `triggerOnboarding`).
After setup the footer's Setup opens the same page with a Home button, so Chrome control stays reachable.
A Debug build launched with `--show-setup` (`verify-clicky.sh app-launch --show-setup`) opens the panel on the setup page at launch as a first run does, without clearing a permission. On a Mac that already finished onboarding, its Start Clicky only hides it again and writes nothing (`showsSetupForThisLaunch`), and on one that never did, its Start Clicky is the real first run and writes `hasCompletedOnboarding`.
The shared hover view (`HoverAndPointerNSView` in `BackgroundAgentListPanel.swift`) reports a hover as soon as its tracking area is laid out under a still pointer, so a panel or card that opens under the mouse shows its hover at once instead of after the next mouse move.
**Settings dial**: Holding shift + option for 0.3 seconds, with no other key, modifier, or mouse button pressed, and no mouse button already down from a drag, opens a round dial centered on the mouse (`SettingsDialShortcutRecognizer`, `SettingsDialController`, `SettingsDialView`).
The dial replaces the old shift + option agent list shortcut, so settings no longer need the menu bar panel, which the menu bar icon still opens for clicks and the keyboard.
It is a 224 point plate, an opaque Card disc (the Card's surface gradient, its 8 percent edge and two-layer shadow, and a 1.5 point rim in the theme's accent that is strongest at the top), with five 44 point tokens on a 78 point radius and the cursor glyph in the center (`SettingsDialGeometry`).
The tokens are Team at -90 degrees, Model at -18, Agents at 54, Show at 126, and More at 198, each labeled below it (above for Team).
Every label sits on its own dark pill sized to the text (`SettingsDialLabel`), so no text shows on the bare page, and the Team fan's name for the highlighted ball sits just outside the arc, away from the center, so it never covers the next ball.
The cursor glyph is the theme's own cursor, and Agents shows the count of running agents.
The cursor is drawn still, with no motion and no streaks (`SettingsDialCustomCursor`, a static `CustomCursorView`), at its natural size in the center and at most 22 points long in the Team token, where the triangle keeps its 16 points.
The selected token fills with the cursor's accent color and draws its icon in the team's ink color (`cursorContrastColor`) so it stays visible.
The cursor on the selected Team token sits on a dark disc inside the accent ring, since its shapes are often filled in the accent and would vanish on the accent fill.
The recognizer sends `opened` when the hold completes, `released` when shift or option comes up, and `cancelled` when any key (Escape included), mouse button, control, or command arrives while the dial is open, or when the event tap stops or macOS cuts it off (`eventTapStopped`), since a release may be lost then.
A main thread stalled past the tap's timeout cuts it off too, so the learner holds the keys again.
The monitor publishes these as `settingsDialEventPublisher`, and `CompanionManager.handleSettingsDialEvent` runs them.
Escape reaches `closeLiveSceneOnEscape` first, which returns while the dial is open, so Escape only cancels the dial.
`SettingsDialController` owns the panel, a borderless nonactivating panel that never becomes key, like the agent list, so the app under it keeps the keyboard.
The panel ignores mouse events, so every click passes through to the app under it, and a 60 Hz timer reads `NSEvent.mouseLocation`, since a global monitor skips events that go to this app's own windows.
The picking rules are pure and live in `SettingsDialModel.swift` (`SettingsDialSelection.afterPointerMoved(to:fans:)`, with the pointer's offset from the dial's center, y growing down).
`SettingsDialFans` holds what the fans offer, fixed when the dial opens, so every pick and every commit goes through the same Team fan, whatever the learner saved.
Inside the plate, the pointer previews the slice nearest its angle, and a pointer within 32 points of the center picks nothing.
Team and Model fan their choices on an outer arc, 152 points out.
Team shows the bundled themes, Clicky blue first, then up to 3 saved custom themes, the most recently saved first (by the modification date of `theme.json`).
Each theme is its own cursor, drawn still on a dark disc so it reads over any page, with its name on the pill, and the triangle keeps its 16 points and its pointer angle (`SettingsDialCustomCursor`).
The fan spans 156 degrees across the top whatever it holds, with Clicky blue at the left end and the last item at the right end (-12 degrees), so 14 items sit 12 degrees apart where 11 sit 15.6 (`SettingsDialFans.teamFanSpanDegrees`).
At 14 items the balls are 31.8 points apart, which leaves room for an enlarged ball next to an ordinary one.
A saved name can run to 24 characters, wider than the window has room for at the fan's right end, so a pill that would stick out shows the start of the name and an ellipsis (`SettingsDialLabel.fanLabelText`).
Model shows Sonnet and Opus as pills on either side of its token's angle.
Beyond the plate, the pointer picks items in the open fan by angle.
Once a fan opens it stays open while the pointer travels outward, even across another slice's angle, and the slice changes only when the pointer comes back inside the plate.
With no fan open, a pointer beyond the plate still picks the slice at its angle, so a flick that overshoots lands.
Past 230 points nothing is highlighted, though an open fan stays open, so a stray release far away cancels.
Releasing the keys commits the highlighted item (`SettingsDialChoice`).
A Team item sets `themeSettings.theme`, so the cursor turns into that theme's cursor at once, and a Model item calls `setSelectedModel`.
Agents opens the agent list at the mouse, and that list stays open until Escape or a click outside.
Show toggles Show Clicky (`setClickyCursorEnabled`), and More posts `clickyShowPanel`, which `MenuBarPanelManager` answers by showing today's menu bar panel.
Releasing in the center, or on Team or Model with no item highlighted, commits nothing.
Opening the dial closes an open agent list.
The dial and its fans shift inward near a screen edge, so nothing is cut off (`SettingsDialPlacement.dialCenter`, which keeps the whole 552 by 360 point window inside the screen's visible frame, reaching 300 points right of the center so a saved theme's name at the fan's right end has room, and wide enough for the longest built-in fan label pill).
A dial shifted off the pointer picks nothing until the pointer has moved 10 points (`SettingsDialPicker`), so a release with no movement never commits by accident.
The first time the dial opens, a hint reads "hold ⇧⌥ · flick, release to pick" under it, once (`settingsDialHintWasShown` in UserDefaults).
With Reduce Motion on, the dial and its fans appear with no scale or stagger.
The dial opens with the Glide spring, its fan items pop in 14 ms apart, and hover and selection are a Snap (`DS.Move`).
The highlighted Team fan ball's name is a pill in that theme's own accent, with its contrast color for the letters.
A release over an item flies that item into the center while the dial shrinks into the cursor at 0.7x of the Glide, and a cancel, or a release over nothing, fades the dial out in 120 ms (`SettingsDialClosingForm`).
With Reduce Motion on, every close is a 150 ms fade.
A dial that is leaving is not open (`SettingsDialController.isOpen`), so Escape and a new dial ignore it, and a new dial removes it at once.
The list is a Tether Card that Glides out of its origin and closes back into it, on the shared carrier panel with the 1 point tether line.
Its origin is the disc it was opened from (the disc's center), or, when the dial's Agents slice or an approval request opens it, the mouse or the cursor glyph.
It closes back into that origin at 0.7x on Escape, on a click outside, and on a second click on its disc (`closeWithGlide`), and with no move when another surface opens over it or it moves to another disc (`close`).
With Reduce Motion on, it crossfades in place in 150 ms.
Its window is the Card grown by a transparent margin where the Card's shadow draws (`AgentListWindowMargins`, 12 above, 24 on each side, 40 below), and every placement rule places the Card, so the margin may reach past the screen's edge.
A click in the window's shadow margin is outside the Card and closes the list, whether it reaches another app or lands on the panel (`AgentListWindowMargins.cardFrame`).
The approval card's one second clock starts only once the real panel is on screen and stops when the list starts to close: the hidden panel and the copies that play on the carrier report nothing to the desk (`AgentListUIState.deskIsListening`).
Rows rise 40 ms apart while the Card grows out of its origin, and a row expands with the Glide spring while its steps' check marks draw themselves in.
A row shows the same disc the agent has beside the notch (`AgentTokenDiscView`), with the same badges, a title that never wraps, and a live line under it: the agent's own `report_step` text and the site it reads when the app knows it, the approval wait, why a failed agent failed, or "Cancelled" (`agentListLiveLine`).
The site comes from the app tools only: the video's site for `read_video_captions` and the host of an `open_url` link, and the agent's next step clears it, since Anthropic's own web search and fetch never tell the app their sites (`BackgroundAgent.readingHost`).
The header counts the agents running and the agents that need the learner's OK.
A failed row offers "Try again" once (`BackgroundAgent.canBeTriedAgain`, `CompanionManager.tryTheBackgroundAgentAgain`): it starts the same task, 3D model, experiment, or study guide as a new agent with its own row, under the rules the voice tools keep (`backgroundAgentMayBeTriedAgain`: no quiz question open, an experiment only when none is building, waiting to be shown, or on screen, and a 3D model only with a video in front), and a study guide only retries for the video it was for (checked in the retry closure), and the failed row keeps the button only when the work could not start.
The list is a nonactivating panel that never becomes key, so Chrome keeps the keyboard, and each row expands to its steps with a Cancel button while it runs. A finished row shows, without expanding, Show model or Show experiment for a waiting scene, Hear answer for a waiting task answer, and Open page when the result has a page (`AgentListActions`). Show and Hear wait while a video quiz question is open, as the voice tools for them do (`agentListMayDeliverAWaitingResult`).
Cancelling a guide cancels its task in `backgroundAgentTasks` and shows no chip. A cancelled new guide keeps no record, but a cancelled revision or refresh whose reply already arrived is saved, since the Worker already trashed the page it replaces (`StudyGuideJob.replyIsSavedEvenWhenCancelled`), and the tab showing the trashed page loads the new one.
A missed or skipped video quiz question on a video that already has a saved guide spawns a "Study guide update" agent (`BackgroundAgentKind.studyGuideUpdate`, `StudyGuideJob.refresh`, `refreshTheStudyGuideAfterAQuizOutcome`), which sends a refresh, so the miss reaches "Review these first" in a few seconds without the learner asking, and its chip says "Study guide updated". A miss while that video's guide is being written queues one refresh of the quiz video's guide for when that guide ends (`studyGuideRefreshIsDueForQuizVideoURLByVideoKey`), since the running guide read its quiz results when it started. The queued refresh runs only when that guide was written, not after a failure or a cancel, and only while the quiz video is still in front, since a guide is written from the video in front; otherwise the miss stays in the quiz log for the video's next refresh or revise. A cancelled guide whose video already has a newer guide agent leaves the queued refresh to that agent (`queuedStudyGuideRefreshStep`), since the refresh may have been queued for it. The app cuts each quiz result's text to the Worker's 2,000 UTF-16 units before logging it. The page is checked in Notion before each refresh, so a miss never brings back a trashed guide, and a skip after the answer, which records nothing, rebuilds nothing. Quiz results are logged under the video's key, as guides are saved, and each saved guide keeps the quiz results it was written with (`StudyGuideRecord.quizResults`), so a revision or refresh after a relaunch still lists the earlier misses (`studyGuideQuizResults(saved:logged:)`). Nothing happens for a video with no guide.
Each answered or skipped video quiz question counts once toward the guide (`StudyGuideQuizLog`, `studyGuideQuizOutcome`): a right name on the first try is got it, and help, a right name after a wrong one, a second wrong answer, or a skip is reviewed first.
While a quiz question is open the hand-off tools, `close_scene`, and `explain_part` are left out, because the old path would send the transcript back to the judge and the scene belongs to the question, and a failed `/voice-command` request then speaks a short line instead of falling back.
Only tools the turn offered run (`runVoiceTool`), and only the page tool names `scene-tools.js` defines, which `pageVoiceToolNames` lists, are taken from a page (`voiceToolsOfferedToTheModel`), so a generated page cannot shadow an app tool, and a name a page lists twice keeps its first entry, since the Worker refuses a request with repeated tool names. Spoken text from the model is cut to 300 characters.
The video tools act on the largest video on the front tab's page (`ChromeVideoTab.frontTabOfTheFrontWindow`), so they work on Vimeo, Coursera, or a Wikimedia file as well as YouTube, but not on a video inside a frame from another site, which a page script cannot reach. The quiz, the pop-out frame grab, and the landmark scan stay on YouTube, because they copy the frame through a page canvas, which Chrome blocks for a video served from another site.
Finding a YouTube tab reads each window's tabs in three batched Apple Events, and the front-tab check reads only the front tab, because one event per tab took about 640 ms with 28 tabs open.
A voice pause or play on a tab Clicky paused drops Clicky's pause record, so Clicky never presses play on it later.
A voice play counts only once the video is not paused and holds data to play (`playing` in the video state), checked for about 1.6 seconds (`learnerVideoPlayOutcome`), because Chrome refuses a video with sound on a page never clicked, and some pages show an empty placeholder video until their own play button is clicked. The failure goes back to the model, which tells the learner to click the play button once.
`find_moment` and `seek_to_moment` are offered only while the video in front is on youtube.com or one of its subdomains (`urlIsOnYouTube`, read from the tab's URL, never the page), since the captions reader keeps only YouTube's json3 format. Both score the captions (the quiz's, or yt-dlp's, kept per video for the session) with `videoCaptionMomentsMatching`. `find_moment` is a lookup: it moves nothing and returns the best 3 lines, and the model then calls `seek_to_time`, which costs a second model turn. `seek_to_moment` is an action: it seeks to the best line in one call and returns `changed` with `movedTo` (the line's seconds and caption text), so the model can follow it with `pop_out`, `set_speed`, or `seek_relative` in the same reply and the router speaks its confirmation with no second turn. Words from how the learner framed the request (explain, mention, jump, back, goes, and their forms) never make a line count on their own while the query has another word, so a teaser that says "explains" cannot win, and a query with only framing words, as in "the jump", uses them as its topic. A tie between lines that say the topic words equally goes to the line whose captions within 30 seconds say the topic words more often (`secondsAroundALineThatBreakATie`), since an explanation repeats its terms and an intro teaser does not, then to the line that also says the query's framing words, so "the long jump" prefers the line that says both words, and only a tie there too goes to the earliest line, so the tool descriptions send "the last time", "the second time", a question about when, and a correction after a wrong landing to `find_moment`, where the model sees every candidate (`SeekToMomentTests`).
Each call shows `VoiceToolCallChip` above the cursor in plain words (`VoiceToolCall.chipPhrase`), such as "Turning it left" or "Going back 10 seconds", for 1.4 seconds and pops in and leaves with a fade and a 6 point rise. An action's chip shows once the call has run, and only when it worked, so a call the page or app refused never shows one; page tools finish in a few milliseconds, so it still appears with the change. `seek_to_moment` shows "Jumping to “<query>”" once the seek worked. A lookup (`find_moment`) changes nothing on screen, so its "Finding …" chip shows while the caption search runs (`VoiceToolCall.chipPhraseToShow(at:offeredToolNames:)`, which shows nothing for a tool the turn did not offer, since `runVoiceTool` refuses it unrun), and a push-to-talk press during a call shows no chip afterwards. A hand-off tool (`pop_out`, `build_new_scene`, `answer_with_screen`) shows no chip, since its own status line or thinking dots follow at once, and `build_simulation` shows none when it only shows a ready experiment, and neither does a call that changes nothing, such as a zoom factor of 1 or a seek that rounds to 0 seconds. A Debug build with the "Log tool call JSON" switch on shows the call as code instead, such as `rotate_model(yawDegrees: -45)`.
A failed call stops the later calls of its reply on both paths, since a later call that depends on it, such as the `pop_out` after a `seek_to_moment` that found no caption, would act on the wrong screen, and a hand-off would end the command with the learner never hearing the failure.
On the old path the loop is `runVoiceToolCallsInOrder`. Each skipped call gets the result `Not run: an earlier call in this reply failed` (`notRunAfterAFailureResultText` in Swift and Go), so every `tool_use` has a `tool_result`, and the model is asked again as it is for any failure.
A call to a tool the command did not offer counts as a failure here too.
`seek_to_moment` reads the captions and then seeks only when the front tab is still the tab and the page whose captions it read (`seekToTheCaptionMoment`, `ChromeVideoTab.isTheSamePage`), since the yt-dlp download can take seconds, and it fails with "The video in front changed while I was reading its captions, so I didn't move it." otherwise.
When every tool changed the screen, Clicky speaks the confirmations the model put in the calls, after the change (`voiceCommandNextStep`).
A lookup or a failure goes back to the model for up to 3 turns (on the harness brain, a fourth turn on Sonnet follows the third, see the harness brain section above), and when the last turn still needs the model, Clicky says "Sorry, I couldn't do that one." (`voiceCommandGaveUpLine`) and never the model's last text, since the Worker tells the model that text next to a tool call is never spoken and it can claim a success that failed, and the last 5 tool calls go with each command, so "no, the other way" is read against them.
Outside a quiz question, a failed `/voice-command` request falls back to the keyword path.
Pressing push-to-talk stops Clicky's audio at once, as before.
Debug builds add a "Log tool call JSON" switch to the panel (`logsVoiceToolCallJSON` in UserDefaults, ignored in Release), and a Debug build launched with `--voice-tool-debug-server`, which `verify-clicky` passes, has `VoiceToolDebugServer` serve the same tools, chosen by the router's own `appVoiceToolContext`, as a loopback MCP endpoint at `http://127.0.0.1:8790/mcp` (`initialize`, `tools/list`, `tools/call`), leaving out the three tools that need a spoken request (`answer_with_screen`, `build_new_scene`, `build_simulation`).
It also takes an unlisted `debug_seed_agents` call, which with `kinds` (such as `["task","task","task"]`) starts one running agent for each kind named, to show their colors. Adding `"serveTheToken": true` flies each new token from the cursor, as a tool-started agent's does, so a take can show the serve with no model call. A serve in flight lands at once when the next starts, so seed one kind per call. Without `kinds` it puts up to three running agents (`runningAgentCount`, 3 by default), one finished with its ending chip, and one failed straight into the agent store with no Worker call, so screenshots and demo takes can show every token and agent list state at once. Three running agents fill the three token positions, so pass `runningAgentCount` 1 to see the finished and failed tokens too. Adding `"waitingForApproval": true` to a `kinds` call puts those agents in the state the approval desk gives a waiting agent, with the `sends` capability, so their discs show the "!" face and the peek card names it. The unlisted `debug_click_menu_bar_icon` call clicks the menu bar icon's button.
The seeded agents carry volleyball subjects, and under a team theme the team's best-known player leads the list (`BackgroundAgentStore.seedAgentsForScreenshots`).
It refuses any request with an `Origin` header, without a JSON content type, or with a body over 1 MB (a request is cut off past 1 MB of body plus 16 KB of headers), so a web page cannot reach it, and it closes a connection whose request has not fully arrived after 10 seconds, while a tool call that has started, such as a slow `find_moment`, runs to its reply.
Every script Clicky runs in a Chrome tab (`ChromeVideoTab.runJavaScript`) has a 2 second Apple Event timeout, the quiz clock read and the pause and play calls included, and a frame grab, which draws a canvas, has 5 seconds, because a page with an `alert()` would otherwise freeze the main actor for about 2 minutes.

**Video Quiz**: A voice request to watch a video and be quizzed starts `VideoQuizSession`: it reads the YouTube tab through AppleScript, gets timestamped captions with yt-dlp, asks `POST /video-quiz-plan` for the moments, and pauses at each one through JavaScript in Chrome. Saying only "watch this with me" starts nothing. Saying "stop the quiz" (or end, cancel, quit, or "done with the quiz") stops it, along with "stop quizzing", "stop watching", and "can we stop" or "let's stop" when speech recognition drops the last word. Push-to-talk keeps the microphone on for half a second after the shortcut is released, so the last word is not cut off, and Apple Speech gets Clicky's command phrases as contextual strings. While Clicky reads the captions and plans, the status label says "Reading the transcript" and then "Picking spots to quiz you on". A scene or pop-out asked for over a playing video in Chrome's front tab, on any public https site (`captionSearchMayReadTheVideoPage`), pauses the page's largest video, with or without a quiz, and resumes it when the scene clears. A video inside a frame from another site, such as an embedded player on a blog, keeps playing, since a page script cannot reach it. These page reads use a 2 second Apple Event timeout, as the voice tools do. During a quiz it pauses only the quiz video, when its tab is the one in front. `VideoQuizSession` keeps one record of a video Clicky paused (a quiz question or a requested scene, with its tab), and presses play only for that record, so a video the learner paused stays paused. Saying "stop the quiz" while a question is up clears its pop-out and plays the video again once the pop-out has landed. A question whose pause a requested scene took over is skipped instead of asked (`quizQuestionMayStillBeAsked`). A scene or pop-out asked for during a quiz question sets the question aside and clears its pop-out, so the new scene replaces it and Escape still works. Six seconds before each pause, while the video plays, it grabs the frame from the `<video>` element through JavaScript and asks `POST /video-frame-parts` for the subject and its labels. At the pause it uses that early grab when it found a subject, because the pop-out covers the video with its own frame. It grabs the paused frame only when the early grab found nothing or never ran. When a subject was found, `frame-popout.html` lifts it out of the frame in 3D, starting exactly over the video on screen, and Clicky asks the question when that tour ends. As it asks, the blue cursor flies to the "?" label and its speech Callout shows the question, brightening its words along with the voice (`pointTheBuddyAtTheQuizQuestion`), so a long question can be read again while answering. The label is found by the part `/video-frame-parts` marked `isQuizAnswer` (`quizQuestionLabelScreenPoint`), since its name can differ from the planner's `answerLabel`. The bubble stays through a first wrong answer and is released when the question ends. Otherwise the question is voice only. On a quiz pop-out the "?" label is a Callout in the theme's accent: the Callout's solid `#14171e` surface, a 1.5 point accent rim and halo, and a 17 point "?" (`--clicky-accent` in `scene-style.css`). A video's own printed labels could name the answer, so for a quiz pop-out Vision's text recognition finds the printed text in the frame (`VideoFramePrintedText`), and `frame-popout.html` smooths each text box out of the frame behind and the lifted subject, by shrinking it to 3 rows of pixels and stretching it back, until the pop-out returns to the video. The answer alone does not bring it back, since a correct name leads to a follow-up question the text could answer. The log shows "Video quiz: hiding N printed text boxes until the pop-out returns".
`applyJudgedLiveSceneAnswer` maps the verdict to a `LiveSceneRevealOutcome` in one place (`LiveSceneRevealOutcome(grade:wrongAnswerCount:)`) and calls `LiveSceneOverlay.revealQuizLabel(outcome:)`.
For a frame pop-out the page's `answerMoment` receives the outcome string.
`celebrateCorrectName` (a correct name, even name only) turns the label green (`DS.Colors.success`, mirrored as `--clicky-success-green`) with the real name and a check, and a one-shot ring burst at the part's anchor, and Swift plays the "Glass" system sound once.
`shakeAndKeepTheQuestionMark` (a first wrong answer) shakes the label once (the Shake move, 0.45 seconds dying out from 7 points) and keeps the "?", and Clicky follows the judge's sentence with "Say hint if you want a clue." unless that sentence already offers a hint or a clue (`firstWrongAnswerSpokenLine`), since a bare "yes" to "Want a hint?" would reach the judge as a second answer.
`revealWithoutCelebration` (asked for help, or a second wrong answer) shows the name in the normal color.
Reduce Motion skips the shake and the ring and keeps the color change.
For a generated page, `revealQuizLabel` still calls plain `reveal()`, and a first wrong answer reveals nothing.
When a quiz ends with at least one question finished, whether every moment was asked or the learner said "stop the quiz", a summary Card shows over the quiz video (`VideoQuizSummary`, `VideoQuizSummaryPanelController`): "Quiz done", the score ("3 of 4 right"), and one button per missed or skipped question with its timestamp, in video order.
It Glides out of the video's top edge on the shared carrier and closes back into it, or fades with Reduce Motion on, and it joins full-screen spaces, so it shows over a full-screen video. Its window is the Card grown by the agent list's transparent margin (`AgentListWindowMargins`), where the Card's shadow draws. The score is green only when every answer was right, and more than 5 questions scroll inside the Card.
**End-of-video recap**: the same Card also recaps a video when it ends (`VideoRecap`, `VideoRecapWatcher`, `VideoRecap.swift`), with no new model call.
The Card gains a "What you learned" section of at most 3 lines (`VideoRecap.learnedLines`): the saved study guide's `summary` first, then the explanation of each question the learner got right in video order, each cut at a word to 140 characters (`recapLine`) with repeats dropped, and a "Study guide" button beside Close when the video has a saved guide on an https Notion page.
The title is "Quiz done" when a quiz ended it and "Video done" when the video ended or no quiz ran, and a video with a guide and no quiz shows no score.
The button closes the Card and opens the guide as "open my study guide" does (`openTheStudyGuidePage`), after `POST /study-guide-status` confirms the page is still in Notion, and a trashed page shows the failure chip "That study guide was deleted in Notion".
A video counts as ended when its own `ended` state is true (`FrontVideoEndReading`, the largest video of Chrome's front tab, read with one Apple Event that runs the page script in the active tab and returns `<0 or 1>|<page address>`).
`VideoRecapWatcher` polls every 3 seconds (`videoEndWatchSecondsBetweenReads`), only while Chrome is the front app and Clicky is free (`clickyIsBusyForAVideoRecapCard`: listening, thinking, speaking, a quiz or its question, a scene, a Card, the Theme Studio card, the settings dial, the agent list, the room art panel, the "Add theme?" card, or an approval waiting).
It starts only once a quiz has ended or a guide was written for some video since launch (`startWatching`), so until then it makes no Apple Event.
After that each poll reads the front tab's address, which runs no page script, and runs the `ended` script only when that address is a watched video.
A failed read, such as a page with an alert, a `chrome://` page, or Chrome refusing scripts, backs the watch off for 30 seconds (`videoEndWatchSecondsToWaitAfterAFailedRead`).
A video from an earlier launch that has a saved guide is not watched until one of those happens in this launch.
`videoEndWatchStep` decides: an ended video with something to recap shows once, and is marked handled until the video plays again, since YouTube leaves `ended` true for the seconds before its next video autoplays.
A recap equal to the one a Card last showed for that video shows nothing (`videoRecapToShowAtTheEnd`), so the Card that ended its quiz does not show again at the video's end, a replay shows nothing new, and a guide written since changes the recap and shows it.
Only a Card that showed counts as seen (`recapWasShown`), so a recap held back shows at the next ending.
At a quiz's end the Card Glides out of the video as before, with the recap's lines added.
At a video's end the discs beside the notch gather first (`AgentTokenDockController.gatherTheDiscs`): each disc on the display the mouse is on flies to the cursor glyph as a ghost on the carrier, in a Send's arc backward, while the real disc is hidden, and when the last ghost lands the Card Glides out of that same point, or out of the video's top edge when the point is off the Card's display.
A push-to-talk press, Escape, or a scene starting during the gather ends it (`cancelTheVideoRecapGather`): the flights land at once, the Card stays away, and the discs go back (`videoRecapCardMayShowAfterTheGather`).
The gathered discs show again at once (`gatheredDiscsMustShowAgain`) when a new disc appears or an agent waits for the learner's OK while the Card is open, since a hidden "!" badge would hide the request.
Closing the Card in any way (`VideoQuizSummaryPanelController.onClosed`) sends the discs back (`sendTheDiscsBack`), each ghost flying from the cursor to its slot, with the "+N" disc's current count.
With no disc showing, the Card unfolds at once.
With Reduce Motion on, the discs fade out and in over `DS.Move.send.reduceMotionForm.fadeSeconds` and the Card fades in.
The log says `Recap gather:` for each case and `Video recap:` for the watch.
An ending can be missed when the next video of a playlist starts within a poll, and a YouTube ad on the same `<video>` element may report `ended`, which was not checked.
A click on a question brings the quiz tab forward, moves the video to where the question paused, holds the pause, and shows that question's pop-out again with every label named and the narration a requested pop-out has (`VideoQuizSession.goBackToTheMissedQuestion`, `GoingBackToAMissedQuestion`), and clearing it resumes the video. A question asked by voice alone had no pop-out, so its video plays again from 10 seconds before it.
When the tab no longer shows the quiz video, or the pop-out cannot open, Clicky says "Sorry, I couldn't go back to that question.", and a pop-out that could not open plays the quiz video again. A scene still building is cancelled first. A video Clicky held paused on another tab stays paused, and Clicky forgets that pause, so a go-back never presses play on another tab. A close during the Card's Glide lands the carried Card at once, so it shows at full size for 0.05 seconds before it goes.
Close, Escape (even while the Card Glides in), a scene starting to show, and the start of a new quiz close the Card.
**Scene stylesheet**: `LiveSceneAssets/scene-style.css` is the one stylesheet for what Clicky draws inside a live scene, and the overlay adds it to every page, bundled or generated, before the page's own markup, with the shown theme's accent as `--clicky-accent` (`sceneStyleUserScriptSource`, `LiveSceneOverlay.accentHexForTheNextPage`). Text on an accent fill is the Tether ink, `DS.Colors.inkOnAccent`, on every accent, as on the Callout.
Its rules start with `:root`, so they win over a generated page's own `.scene-label` rule, and they set only how things look, never where they sit.
Part labels are dark glass (`rgba(16, 19, 26, 0.86)` with a blur and a hairline edge), 13 point semibold, at most 220 points wide with wrapping, in four states: resting, hovered (an accent ring and the pointing hand), narrated (filled with the accent), and a right quiz answer (green with a check).
On a frame pop-out each leader line ends at its part in an accent dot, and the labels Pop in 120 ms apart the first time they show. The leader's part end is clamped into the window like its label, so a window that shrinks after the scene loaded never sends a line off screen.
The landmark page's paused frame shimmers inside the media rect until the scan rises.
With Reduce Motion on, the Pop, the Shake, the ring burst, and the panel's rise are off, and the colors and text carry each state.
The pop-out lands back in the frame, then clears before the video plays again. Saying "pop this out" (or "pop out", or up to 3 words between, as in "pop this heart out") lifts the subject of the frame the YouTube tab shows right now out in 3D with every label and no question, using the same frame grab, `POST /video-frame-parts`, and `frame-popout.html`; it pauses a playing video first, with or without a quiz. When that tour ends, Clicky speaks two or three short sentences about the parts, one part each, written for a curious 11-year-old, and highlights each part as it names it. The Claude call for those sentences starts when the pop-out starts, with the paused frame and the part labels from the `/video-frame-parts` reply, so the speech is ready when the camera stops. For an image on a web page, the prompt and the attached image say picture instead of video (`FramePopoutSource`, read from whether the grab has a video time). A quiz pop-out asks its question instead, and a quiz question that arrives during a narration stops it before the question's pop-out plays. After the tour of a pop-out with no quiz, its labels take clicks: a click that moves 4 px or less posts `partTapped:<label>` to the `liveSceneEvent` handler, and a longer press stays a drag that turns the model. Clicky accepts the tap only from the bundled frame pop-out, only in `.allLabelsShowing`, outside a video quiz question, and only for a label in the pop-out's own parts list (`partLabelToExplain`). It then turns the camera to that part and answers "what does the <label> do?" through the same narration. As the narration names each part, the blue cursor flies on its bezier arc to that part's label, and its bubble shows the sentence Clicky is speaking, since the label already names the part. The bubble follows the voice: 30 times a second it shows the share of the sentence that matches the share of the line's audio played so far (`ElevenLabsTTSClient.spokenFraction(ofLine:)`, `SpokenLineProgress`), running two letters ahead, so a bubble that arrives late catches up at once and never races ahead. The bubble is the speech Callout (`SpeechCalloutView`, see **Callouts** under **Tether tokens and the carrier**): the whole sentence is on it from the start, spoken words are white, words not yet spoken are 60% white, and a 2 point accent underline sits under the word being spoken. A sentence that names no part, or names the same part again, replaces the words where the Callout already is. `LiveSceneOverlay.partLabelScreenPointsOnceTheCameraSettles()` reads each `.scene-label` box with getBoundingClientRect once the camera glide ends, and `partScreenPoint(of:overlayScreenFrame:)` turns it into a global AppKit point under the label. That point is held (`BuddyPointingHold.untilReleased`) until the narration that set it ends, and then the cursor flies back to the mouse. `labelRectsOnceTheCameraSettles` waits on timers, not animation frames, which stop once the window hides, and returns no labels once the return has started. The cursor overlay windows sit one level above `.screenSaver`, above the live scene window and the status label, so the triangle draws over the scene; they ignore the mouse, so clicks still reach the scene. The overlay never becomes key, so `LiveSceneWebView` adds an always-active tracking area to give the page the mouse moves that drive its label hover. When Chrome's front tab is not a YouTube video (`urlIsAYouTubeWatchPage`: a youtube.com host with the path `/watch`, so a Wayback Machine copy of a watch page is not one), it lifts the largest visible video or image on the page instead. Clicky captures the screen under it at the display's pixel density, up to 1280 pixels on the longest side, showing only Chrome's windows, because a page canvas cannot read an image served from another site. Apple's subject lifting cuts the subject out of the frame. For a painted scene, such as The Starry Night, `POST /video-frame-parts` returns `wholePictureIsSubject: true`. The painting then lifts out whole, sky included, since subject lifting would keep only the cypress. Art of one animal or object on a plain background, such as Dürer's Young Hare, still lifts out only the animal. A flat illustration or a painting lifts out puffed up, with its thickness taken from the distance to the mask's outline. A picture that already shows depth, such as the Turbofan article's 3D cutaway or a photo of an engine, gets `pictureShowsDepth: true`, and `frame-popout.html` lifts its cutout as a flat card, because puffing up a drawn perspective stretches it into smears. A dragged card only tilts, up to 0.75 radians sideways and 0.45 up or down, since a flat card turned edge-on shows it is paper thin. Its tour ends almost facing the camera (0.12 radians, where a puffed-up model turns 0.6), and "reset the view" returns it there, since a card seen from the side looks squeezed, and a round subject like a globe would look oval. The log line then ends with "as a flat card". An animated page image, such as the Turbofan article's GIF, keeps playing on the lifted subject and on the frame behind it, with no model call. The Chrome script returns the image's URL only when the image sits wholly inside the viewport and is stretched to its box, and the URL is https and under 2048 characters, and the app uses it only when the screen capture was not cut off at a display edge, since only then do the image's own frames line up with the capture. The script also returns `imageFileURL`, any visible https image file under 2048 characters, cropped or partly scrolled out of view, which only room art reads. `PageImageAnimation.swift` downloads the image while Sonnet reads the frame, over https only (redirects included), with no cookies, cache, or stored credentials, a 200 reply, at most 8 MB, and never from localhost or a private address, and it decodes only a GIF, a WebP, or a PNG (`animatedImageTypeIdentifiers`), since any open page names the URL. It runs off the main actor, skips the download with Reduce Motion, and gets 1.5 seconds after Sonnet's reply before the still frame plays. It refuses a frame more than 4096 pixels wide or tall before decoding, and ImageIO splits the rest into frames (up to 150, and at most 60 million pixels across all frames at the capture's size) at the capture's size, drawn over the capture's top-left color and timed as a browser times them. Every frame reuses the capture's subject mask, and `frame-popout.html` clears mask pixels that show that background color in the current frame but did not in the capture, so a part that moves away, like an arrow, leaves no blob. Reduce Motion keeps the still frame. An animated image lifts out with no part labels, since Sonnet places them on the first frame and the moving picture drifts away from them, and its narration talks about the motion the animation shows, such as a globe's spin, or for an animated artwork about the art, with no part named, since the image Claude sees is only the first frame. The log shows "Frame pop-out: playing the page image's N animation frames, with no labels". Saying "animate this", "make it move", or "make it flow" (`LiveSceneKind.animatedFramePopout`) pops the picture out the same way and, while the tour plays, asks `POST /picture-flows` for the streams that flow through it, with the frame and the part labels. `frame-popout.html` draws them with `window.liveScene.showFlows(flows)` once the tour ends, on a canvas above the model and below the labels, and fades them out with the labels on the return. The picture itself never changes. Each `style` in the page's `flowStyles` table draws particles that travel along the path on the lifted surface: soft smoke puffs that grow as they rise, flickering sparks, glowing lava blobs, droplets and air streaks in the flow's palette color, zigzag current, or beads. A particle is a pure function of the clock, so nothing piles up between frames. Reduce Motion stops each stream's clock once its particles cover the whole path, so they stand still but stay visible. Over a pop-out already on screen after its tour, the same request animates that one without popping it out again, and during the tour it pops the picture out again. The status label above the cursor says "Animating it" (teal, with streams flowing on its icon) instead of "Popping it out" while an animated pop-out builds, and while the flows for a pop-out already on screen come back. Asking to animate an artwork already on screen gets the artwork line at once, with no label and no "Sure, let me animate that." When nothing flows, as in a bicycle diagram, Clicky says "I don't see anything flowing through this picture to animate." An artwork, such as The Starry Night or The Great Wave, still pops out but is never animated, and Clicky says "This is a work of art, so I'll leave it just as the artist made it." A failed call gets "Sorry, I couldn't animate that one." Each line comes after the narration (`PictureFlowsOutcome`), and the log shows "Frame pop-out: streaming N flows" when it worked. "Animation" alone triggers nothing. When "show me" or "render this in 3D" is said over a YouTube frame that `POST /landmark` recognizes as a famous place, `landmark-tiles.html` shows Google's real 3D scan of it instead, cut down to the structure and about 130 m of its surroundings and floating over the video. The overlay fetches the tiles from `clicky-asset://tiles/`, which the asset handler forwards to the Worker, so the page never holds the key or reaches the network, and only this bundled page gets the policy that allows its WebAssembly Draco decoder. A 3D model built from scratch appears only on request: "show me" or "in 3D" pauses the video and builds one from what the video just said, and clearing it resumes the video.

## Key Files

| File | Lines | Purpose |
|------|-------|---------|
| `leanring_buddyApp.swift` | ~98 | Menu bar app entry point. Uses `@NSApplicationDelegateAdaptor` with `CompanionAppDelegate` which creates `MenuBarPanelManager` and starts `CompanionManager`. No main window. The app lives entirely in the status bar. |
| `LiveSceneOverlay.swift` | ~1000 | Reusable transparent window and page on the screen it is given: the mouse screen for generated scenes, the screen showing the video or image for pop-outs and landmark pages. Serves the generated page and bundled three.js at `clicky-asset`, blocks network requests, resolves the quiz label, passes label taps from the page, reads where each part label sits on screen, hands picture flows to a frame pop-out (`showFlows`), tells CompanionManager when a page starts to show (`onASceneStartedToShow`), adds `scene-style.css` with the theme's accent (`sceneStyleUserScriptSource`) and `scene-tools.js` and lists, reads, and calls the page's voice tools, and clears the scene, playing the return first for a frame pop-out (`LiveSceneOverlayPhase`). Counts the scenes it has played (`scenesPlayedCount`), which the voice command's screen read and the router's scene state (`sceneNumber`) name the scene on screen by, and ends a tour its page never ended within 30 seconds (`LiveSceneTourEnd`). |
| `VoiceCommandTools.swift` | ~1120 | The voice command fast path's data: the tool descriptor, the app tools and when each is offered (`AppVoiceTool`, and which of them hand the request off to a look at the media), the parsed `/voice-command` reply, each call's chip text in plain words (`chipPhrase`) and as code (`chipText`), tool outcomes and the next step after them, the message the model reads, the router rule, the one brain flag (`BrainTakesEveryRequest`), the harness flag (`BrainRunsInTheHarness`, on by default) and where a launch's commands run (`VoiceCommandBackend`, `nextStepAfterTheHarnessFailedACommand`: no app secret or a refused one means the old path) and the keyword branches it turns off (the Theme Studio's `design_theme`, `pick_theme_idea`, `save_theme`, and `close_theme_studio` are here too, with `themeStudioIsOpen` in the router rule and `designATheme` among the branches), the harness flag (`BrainRunsInTheHarness`), caption moment matching (`videoCaptionMomentsMatching`, with its tie-break window `secondsAroundALineThatBreakATie`), the old path's ordered call loop, which skips the calls after a failed call in a reply (`runVoiceToolCallsInOrder`, `notRunAfterAFailureResultText`), the instant replay's `replay_explanation` and `replay_step`, and the Worker client. |
| `HarnessVoiceCommand.swift` | ~281 | The app's side of a voice command on the brain in the harness: the early line's rules (`voiceCommandLineToSpeak`, the one cut of a line the harness sent, `VoiceCommandLineAudio` and `voiceCommandLineAudio`, which plays the early audio only for a finished line with exactly its words, and `earlyLineIsWorthFetching`, which skips a turn that hands off or looks something up), `HarnessVoiceCommandLines` (the one line each call sends back, the hand-off mark, the outcome of a call that finished before a press, and the cancel handler's lines, behind a lock, since the cancel handler runs off the main actor), `AppVoiceToolStep` and `voiceToolOutcome(of:handOffMark:)`, the one place a hand-off starts its new path after the mark, `HarnessVoiceCommandEnding`, and `runTheVoiceCommandEvents`, the loop over a command's harness events with the 10 second wait for each (35 seconds after the planning line, `secondsToWaitForAPlanningTurn`), which ends the command once a hand-off's result is sent and runs no call queued after the wait ran out. |
| `VoiceCommandTiming.swift` | ~123 | The moments of one voice command (`VoiceCommandTiming`, with the log line's formatting as a pure property, ending with `line_audio=` and `speculation=`), `PushToTalkRelease` (which press was released and when), `PushToTalkPressAcceptance` (whether a release ends an accepted press or one ignored during the microphone's tail), and `VoiceCommandTimingRecorder`, which collects the marks, keeps the first of each kind, and prints the line once. |
| `VoiceCommandScreenRead.swift` | ~160 | What a voice command reads from the screen at push-to-talk release: `FrontTabReadAtRelease` (Chrome's front tab, read at most once for one release and shared by the cursor loop's media read, the screen read, and the study guide check), `FrontVideoURLSource` (the release read's URL, or a fresh read for a command with no release read), `VoiceCommandScreenRead` (the press it belongs to, the page identity at release, the front video state, and the page read's task), `VoiceCommandPageRead` (the page's tools and state, the study guide check, and when they finished), `VoiceCommandPageIdentity`, `screenReadForTheTranscript`, which hands a transcript only the read of its own press for the page still on screen, `screenReadStartsAtRelease`, `frontVideoStateSourceForTheTranscript`, and `pageReadForTheVoiceCommand`, which awaits the page read inside a cancellation handler and returns nil when a press cancelled the command during it. |
| `VoiceCommandSpeculation.swift` | ~333 | Starting a voice command on the partial transcript: `TranscriptRoute`, `TranscriptRouteContext`, and `transcriptRoute`, the one place a transcript's route is decided, `transcriptWordsForComparison`, `speculativeStartStep` and `speculationStep` (when a partial starts a command, and when the final transcript keeps it), `VoiceCommandInputs` (what a command sends, the approval at the press included, compared again at the final transcript), `StartedHarnessVoiceCommand`, `SpeculativeVoiceCommand`, `ReleaseScreenReadInHand`, `VoiceCommandSpeculationState` with its `end()`, `screenReadToStartFrom(whenThePartialWordsBecome:)`, `takeForTheFinalTranscript(ofPress:)`, and `endForAnEmptyFinalTranscript(ofPress:)` transitions, `VoiceCommandSpeculationOutcome` for the timing line, and `VoiceCommandSpeculation`, the switch that a Debug `--no-voice-command-speculation` launch turns off. |
| `VoiceToolCallChip.swift` | ~290 | The chip above the cursor that says each voice tool call in plain words (or as code in a Debug build with the debug switch on), on the Callout with a 1 point rim, and leaves with a fade-rise or a Recall. `VoiceToolCallChipSender` sets its accent and icon: the theme's accent with a hand for a voice tool call, an agent's own color with its triangle, its player's portrait under a team theme, or a custom theme's picture (`AgentFace`, `VoiceToolCallChipIconContent`) for an agent's ending, which has a quieter second part and a 4 second stay, and red with a cross and one shake for a failure. An approval request uses it too, saying "<agent> needs your OK · say yes or no" until the request is answered, when `hide(ifShowing:)` takes it down. `VoiceToolCallChipCallout` is the chip with no model, so a test renders it, and `voiceToolCallChipOrigin` places the panel above the status line while one shows. |

The Quick Look extension compiles it too, so it uses no app-only type. |

| `CursorLoopController.swift` | ~380 | Reads the front tab's media at push-to-talk release (the tab itself comes from the release's shared `FrontTabReadAtRelease`), feeds `CursorLoopState` the events, ends the loop when an idle voice state holds, polls the front tab once a second while a loop or an agent line shows, hears app switches, takes each agent's start and status (`agentStarted`, `agentStatusesChanged`, which times a finished agent's last lap), and converts the loop and the agent line to the overlay's coordinates for the screen that holds them. Its `waitFor...` methods return when a task it started has finished, so tests never sleep for a guessed time. It takes the wait between front tab polls, the wait for a finishing lap, and the center it hears app switches on (`appActivationNotificationCenter`, the workspace's by default) as parameters, so a test counts the waits and posts to its own center instead of timing them, and an app activating on a busy machine never ends its loop. |

It exposes the press's partial transcript (`partialTranscript`, `partialTranscriptUpdates`) for the speculative start, and `BuddyDictationSessionCallbacks` ends each session at most once, with its final transcript or with `endWithNoTranscript` when the final is empty or the session is cancelled. |

Shares a single URLSession across all sessions. |

| `ElevenLabsTTSClient.swift` | ~410 | ElevenLabs TTS client. Asks the Worker for streamed 24 kHz PCM and plays it through an `AVAudioEngine` player node in fifth-of-a-second buffers as it arrives. `startFetching` starts a line's download into its own buffer (`SpokenLineFetch`, whose `chunks` stream holds the decoded samples until they are played), `play` plays a fetch as soon as its first audio is in and owns it from then on, so every way out cancels the download, and `speakText` is `play(startFetching(text))`, so a line fetched early, such as a voice command's early line, and a line fetched now play through the same code. `speakText` returns the line's `SpokenLineToken` once audio is playing, and `isPlaying` stays true while audio plays or is still arriving. `stopPlayback(ifTheCurrentLineIs:)` stops only when the token names the line playing or arriving, so a caller can end the line it started without stopping a newer answer (`SpokenLineTokenLedger` holds the rule, apart from the audio engine, so tests need no audio). `spokenFraction(ofLine:)` says how far through the playing line the voice is, which the speech Callout brightens its words along with. The engine pauses between lines, an output device change ends a line that is already playing, and a reply that is not PCM, such as MP3 from a Worker deployed before streaming, throws. `PCM16StreamChunker` turns the streamed bytes into samples. |

| `worker/src/videoQuizPlan.ts` | ~335 | `POST /video-quiz-plan`. GPT-6.2 Sol at Ultrafast returns the plan as schema-checked JSON and decides how many moments to pick, through `requestAnthropicMessage`'s OpenAI path, since it beat Sonnet 5.5 in the 2026-11-19 bakeoff (4.7 s against 14.9 s). `parseVideoQuizPlan` moves a pause between two transcript lines to the next line start, moves a source time to the line playing then, lowercases function words, and checks the rest (tested in `worker/src/videoQuizPlan.test.ts`). Up to 2 retries name the broken rule, and each rejection logs "[/video-quiz-plan] attempt N rejected". |
| `worker/src/liveScene.ts` | ~475 | `POST /live-scene` asks GPT Ultrafast (GPT-6.1 Sol for a 3D model, GPT-6.2 Sol for a simulation) for a page and a quiz, checks the reply, and retries once. A GPT request that gets a 401, 403, 404, 429, or 5xx, or whose fetch throws, falls back to Sonnet 5.5, and the retry stays on Sonnet. `POST /judge-answer` asks Sonnet for a verdict and one spoken sentence as schema-checked JSON. |
| `worker/src/voiceCommand.ts` | ~155 | `POST /voice-command`. `toolsForAnthropic` adds the confirmation property and the tool-list cache breakpoint without changing the app's tools, `voiceCommandReply` keeps only text and tool_use blocks, and one non-streaming Haiku call answers (tested in `voiceCommand.test.ts`). |
| `worker/src/videoFrameParts.ts` | ~185 | `POST /video-frame-parts`. Records total time, time to first output, turns, tokens, cost, and the answer, in `bench/results/` and `bench/replies/task-agent/`. See `bench/README.md`. | `bench/brainEvals.ts` | ~1820 | Evals of the voice command router, on two backends picked by `--backend worker\|harness\|both`. The `worker` backend posts each case in `bench/fixtures/brain-evals.json` (the learner's words, the scene state, and the tools offered in 17 screen contexts, two of them for the Theme Studio, closed and showing ideas, four for background agents that run or are paused, and two for an instant replay, offered and open) to a Worker's `POST /voice-command` (Haiku 5.5). The `harness` backend builds the Go harness's brain (`harness/brain.go`) into `bench/.harness`, drives it over its protocol on one process, and answers each `tool_call` with the outcome the app would give that tool (`standInToolResult`: a hand-off tool gets `handedOff`, a lookup such as `find_moment` gets `lookedUp` and a second turn, and a case may script a tool's answer in `toolResults`). Both score the first turn's tool calls against the case's accepted answers: pass, wrong tool, wrong input, missed tool, or spurious tool, with p50 and p95 latency and cost. The harness backend reads the first turn's calls from the names in its `Brain: turn` line, since the app never sees a call after a hand-off or a refused call, and scores such a call by name with no known input (`firstTurnToolCalls`). A model call to `undo_last_change` is scored as the inverse call the harness sent the app under the model's call ID plus `-undo`, since that is what the learner sees, and one the harness refused is scored as a call with no known input (`toolCallsOfTurn`). It also reports the full command's time and cost, sends `cancel_task` for a command it gives up on, and records a harness that exited as a failed command. The harness decides voice approvals and handles `answer_approval` without sending it to the app, so the harness backend seeds a waiting request for a case in the `approvalWaiting` context (`seed_approval_request`, named in `approvalIDWaitingAtPress` and `approvalIDSeenAtPress`, with no `approvalWaiting` in the scene state) and scores that case from the answer the gate settled (`approval_settled`, or the `Brain: approval` log line): a case that expects an answer passes only when the gate took it, and an unrelated command passes only with its own tool call and no settlement (`scoreApprovalCase`).
The harness keeps a session, so the harness backend sends `reset_session` before every case and seeds a case's `earlierCommands` (and `interruptedBefore`, for a command the learner cut short) with `seed_session` (`sessionLinesFor`, `seedMessagesFor`), and the driver starts the harness with `CLICKY_BENCH=1`. The two cases that carry `recentActions` for the Worker backend carry the same actions as `earlierCommands` for the harness, and a test checks that they agree. A case may change keys of its context's scene state (`sceneStateChanges`), so its own scene matches what the earlier commands did.
`fixtures/brain-sessions.json` holds the cases that need the session (an undo after 7 commands, a correction after a fold, a correction after an interrupted command, a correction after a `seek_to_moment`, an injection in an earlier tool result, and an undo after a zoom, after a seek, behind a pop-out, and after a cut-off turn), which only the harness backend runs and `both` scores apart from the parity gate's cases, and a 10 command session that `session-run` measures against the same commands run with a reset before each (cache read share, p50, and cost). `fixtures/brain-evals.json` also holds 10 planning cases, marked `scoreWholeCommand`, whose steps depend on each other (a `find_moment` that feeds a `seek_to_time`, a hand-off that must come last, actions in a fixed order). They are scored on the ordered calls of every turn (`scoreWholeCommand`, `wholeCommandToolCalls`), with each accepted answer an ordered sequence, an optional `{ calls, input }` form for a sequence with input of its own, and a seek's input checked against the timestamp the case scripts in `toolResults`. A case may also list `mustNotReachTheApp`, tools the harness must never send to the app in that command (read from the `tool_call` lines, since the turn line names calls the harness skipped), which `planning-seek-fails-then-pop-out` uses to pin that a failed seek stops the pop-out after it. Only the harness backend runs them, since the Worker backend answers one turn and cannot feed a lookup's result back, and the parity gate leaves them out (`suite: "planning"`); the run prints each one's pass count, full-command p50, and full-command cost. `both` alternates backends case by case, checks the parity gate (`harnessParityGate`: every harness run passes, p50 within 100 ms, first-turn cost within 1.5 times), and exits nonzero when the gate fails. A case may list `input` the called tool must carry (a subset match, with `null` for a key that must be absent), `inputContains` and `inputOmits` (whole lower case words that a string input, such as the `query` of `seek_to_moment`, must hold or must not hold, so a query that is empty or repeats the whole request fails), and the fixture's `skills` list is added to `start_background_task` the way the app adds it (`withSkills`). `fixtures/brain-evals-tools.json` holds the tool descriptors, and `sync-tools` copies their descriptions from `VoiceCommandTools.swift` and `scene-tools.js`. Tested in `brainEvals.test.ts`, which fails when a description differs from the app source or when a tool's hand-off or lookup kind in `appVoiceToolStep` differs from `handOffToolNames` and `lookupToolNames`, or when `AppVoiceTool.mayHandOff` differs from the tools whose step can hand off, or when `appToolBranchesIn` meets a branch it cannot read (a label that is not exactly one `AppVoiceTool` case, or a branch with no `return .handOff` or `return .outcome`) or an `AppVoiceTool` case with no branch, replays recorded harness line streams, pins the `Brain: turn` line against `harness/brain.go`, and drives `bench/fixtures/fake-brain-harness.ts`, a stand-in harness. See "Brain evals" in `bench/README.md`. |
