| `bench/judgeRender.ts` | ~490 | What the judge shows its models: each case's request read from the saved file, each reply drawn over its frame, a description of it, the criteria per route, and the spot-check page. Tested in `judgeRender.test.ts`. |
| `bench/judgeModels.ts` | ~155 | The two judging models and their calls (keys from `worker/.dev.vars`, retried on 429 and 5xx). Tested in `judgeModels.test.ts` with a fake fetch. |
| `bench/cases.test.ts` | ~125 | Checks the benchmark cases: request bodies build, the new quality checks pass and fail the replies they should, and the README runbook names only real case IDs. |
| `tools/fetch-haikyu-portraits.py` | ~220 | Downloads each bundled roster player's page image from the Haikyu!! wiki and crops it with ffmpeg to a 160 by 160 pixel JPEG in the gitignored `leanring-buddy/HaikyuPortraits/`. It reads each player's `fullName` and `portrait` from `themes/*.clickytheme/theme.json`, and keeps per-player crop overrides for faces the default crop misses. |
| `tools/app-job-needed.sh` | ~25 | Reads changed file paths on stdin and prints `true` when the CI `app` job must run, or `false` when every path is on its list of folders and files outside the app build and its tests. The CI `changes` job runs it, after its test `tools/app-job-needed.test.sh` (~30 lines), which pins the list. |
| `tools/swift-check.sh` | ~70 | Builds the app's Swift sources and runs `leanring-buddyTests` without xcodebuild, in a throwaway Swift package that links the sources in place, with the settings in `tools/swift-check/`. It skips the tests that read a page from the app bundle, which only Xcode's test host has. Its `Package.swift` mirrors the Xcode targets' Swift settings, so change both together, along with the flags of the Quick Look type-check in this script. The app target is default main actor, and the test target is not, so a test that calls main actor code says `@MainActor` itself, as the Xcode test host needs. It also type-checks the Quick Look extension's shared files on their own, the list read from the project's membership exception set for `ClickyThemeQuickLook`. |
| `tools/app-icon/render-app-icon.py` | ~175 | Draws the app icon and renders every size the `AppIcon` set names: the cursor arriving along a dotted arc into its landing ring (the Tether spec's Point move) on a deep blue squircle, 824 points inside the 1024 canvas, with a soft shadow in the margin at 128 pixels and up. Sizes of 64 pixels and below use a simple art, a large white cursor on the field, since the arc and ring turn to mush there. It writes the four full-canvas layers (`layers/1-field.svg`, `2-arc`, `3-ring`, `4-cursor`) for import into Icon Composer, without the tile shadow and the cursor's shadow, since Icon Composer draws its own, and rasterizes the PNGs with headless Chrome in sRGB, so run `python3 -I tools/app-icon/render-app-icon.py` after changing a shape and commit both outputs. Icon Composer is a GUI tool, so the shipped asset is the rendered `AppIcon` set, the dark, tinted, and clear modes wait for an import of the layers, and the menu bar template icon is not part of it. |
| `tools/app-icon/check-app-icon.py` | ~110 | Checks each file the `AppIcon` set names: it exists, has the exact pixel size its entry needs, is an 8-bit RGBA PNG with transparent corners and an opaque center, and has the Mac idiom. CI runs it in the `bench` job. |
| `bench/report.ts` | ~185 | Turns benchmark result files into the CSV tables a chart page reads. CI runs it on every committed result. |
| `bench/bakeoffGraphics.ts` | ~150 | Reads the 2026-11-19 bakeoff's results into `bench/graphics/bakeoff-data.js` for the twelve posting cards in `bench/graphics/`, which `bench/graphics/render.sh` draws to PNGs. |
| `demo/` | ~2935 | A Remotion edit of real Clicky recordings for the demo video. `scripts/record-shot.sh` records a Chrome window on the MacBook's Retina display while the rehearsal flag drives the main checkout's Debug build, `scripts/track-cursor.ts` finds the blue triangle in each frame, `scripts/drive-trebuchet-shot.sh` drags the experiment's sliders with the real mouse (`scripts/mouse/mouse.swift`, with `scripts/experiment-panel.py` finding them in a screenshot), `src/timeline.ts` holds the shots, captions, and camera moves of the demo, the follow-up quiz clip, the study guide clip, the quiz miss clip, the parallel agents clip, and the "let me try it" experiment clip, `src/theme.ts` gives each clip its own fonts and colors, and `scripts/mix-music.ts` mixes a ducked music bed under the narration, with an optional voice gain per clip, and normalizes to -14 LUFS. Recordings, the music file, and renders are gitignored. See `demo/README.md`. |

## Build & Run

```bash
# Open in Xcode
open leanring-buddy.xcodeproj

# Select the leanring-buddy scheme, set signing team, Cmd+R to build and run

# Cmd+U runs leanring-buddyTests (Swift Testing) hosted in Clicky.app. CI runs the same tests.

# Building the app needs Go (brew install go), because a build phase compiles harness/ into the app bundle.
# The harness's own tests and checks run without Xcode:
cd harness && go vet ./... && go test ./...

# Without Xcode: build the sources and run leanring-buddyTests in about 15 seconds, in a throwaway
# Swift package (never xcodebuild, see below)
tools/swift-check.sh
tools/swift-check.sh --filter voiceCommand

# Known non-blocking warnings: Swift 6 concurrency warnings,
# deprecated onChange warning in OverlayWindow.swift. Do NOT attempt to fix these.
```

**Do NOT run `xcodebuild` from the terminal.** It invalidates TCC (Transparency, Consent, and Control) permissions and the app will need to re-request screen recording, accessibility, etc.

## Depth service

The room art's `/room-wall` and `/room-floor` need it. It loads a 1.9 GB checkpoint that is not in git.

```bash
cd tools/depth-service
uv sync
curl -L -o checkpoints/depth_pro.pt https://ml-site.cdn-apple.com/models/depth-pro/depth_pro.pt
uv run server.py          # 127.0.0.1:8801, about 14 s per wall on a MacBook Air
uv run pytest -q
```

## Cloudflare Worker

```bash
cd worker
pnpm install

# Add secrets
pnpm wrangler secret put ANTHROPIC_API_KEY
pnpm wrangler secret put ELEVENLABS_API_KEY
pnpm wrangler secret put OPENAI_API_KEY
pnpm wrangler secret put GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY
# Only for study guides: a personal access token from notion.so/developers
pnpm wrangler secret put NOTION_API_KEY
# With NOTION_API_KEY: a random string, such as from openssl rand -hex 32, that the app also holds.
# Set it in the app with: defaults write com.crodris.clicky-watch-along clickyAppSecret <the same string>
# verify-clicky.sh app-launch copies it from worker/.dev.vars into the Debug app's defaults.
pnpm wrangler secret put CLICKY_APP_SECRET
# Only when Info.plist selects the assemblyai speech provider
pnpm wrangler secret put ASSEMBLYAI_API_KEY
# Only for the furniture try-on's 3D models (/try-it-model): a key from fal.ai
pnpm wrangler secret put FAL_KEY

# Deploy
pnpm wrangler deploy

# Local dev (create worker/.dev.vars with your keys)
pnpm wrangler dev

# Test runs: build scenes with Sonnet (about $0.04 each) instead of GPT (about $0.37)
pnpm wrangler dev --var LIVE_SCENE_MODEL:sonnet

# Token use: every model call except the streamed /chat and the /v1/messages passthrough logs "[/route] usage {...}".
# The task agent's tokens show in the app log as "Task agent: turn N ... tokens" lines from the harness.
pnpm wrangler tail

# Tests and typecheck
pnpm test
pnpm run typecheck
```

pnpm 12 refuses to install a dependency with an unapproved build script.
`worker/pnpm-workspace.yaml` and `tools/three-tiles/pnpm-workspace.yaml` list each dependency build script under `allowBuilds`, approved with `true` (esbuild, workerd) or denied with `false` (sharp).

## CI

`.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs on every pull request and on pushes to main.
The `worker` job runs `pnpm test` and `pnpm run typecheck` in `worker/`.
The `harness` job runs `go vet ./...` and `go test -race ./...` in `harness/`, with Go from `actions/setup-go` and the version in `harness/go.mod`.
The `bench` job typechecks `bench/`, runs its unit tests with `pnpm test`, and checks that the committed results in `bench/results/` still parse.
It also runs `tools/app-icon/check-app-icon.py`, which checks the app icon PNGs for their pixel sizes and transparent corners.
It also runs `verify-clicky.test.sh`, which checks how `verify-clicky.sh build` tells that the app is older than a Swift file, how `app-launch` tells that a Clicky is already running, how `app-launch` moves an earlier launch's files aside, how `app-launch` reads its brain flags and keeps `--every-permission`, `--no-app-secret`, and `--wrong-app-secret` out of the app's own arguments, that the two secret flags pass the app's `--app-secret-mode none|wrong` argument and never write or delete the defaults the everyday Clicky shares, that `doctor` reports a missing, refused, or matching app secret without printing it, that `doctor` and `app-launch` warn and name any headless Chrome, since it answers the Apple Events meant for Chris's Chrome, that `lock-take` and `lock-release` keep the app check lock to one owner and refuse to release another owner's lock, that `app-launch` warns and names `grant` when the app starts without Accessibility, which start lines open the app's permissions panel that `app-launch --every-permission` stops on, that `worker-down` given a port stops only that Worker, and that `scroll` lists its usage and refuses a value that is not a whole number of at most 6 digits.
The `app` job installs Go with `actions/setup-go`, which the app's "Build Agent Harness" phase finds on PATH, so the Mac runner needs no Go of its own.
On the Mac runners its Actions cache is off, since Go's build and module caches stay in the runner user's home between jobs, and saving the cache took 2 to 9 minutes a job before it failed.
The `app` job then builds the `leanring-buddy` scheme with Xcode 26.5 and `CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO`, then runs `leanring-buddyTests` hosted in the built `Clicky.app`.
A `changes` job lists the files a pull request or a push to main changes with `git diff --no-renames`, against the merge commit's first parent for a pull request and against the commit before the push for main, and runs `tools/app-job-needed.sh` on them after `tools/app-job-needed.test.sh`.
The `app` job is skipped when every file is outside the app build and its tests: `bench/`, `worker/`, `demo/`, `docs/`, `.claude/`, `.ship/`, `tools/three-tiles/`, `README.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `LICENSE`, `.gitignore`, and `clicky-demo.gif`.
Any other path runs it, and so does a push whose earlier commit the remote no longer has.
Each push to main runs in its own concurrency group, since GitHub cancels a pending run when another joins its group, and a cancelled push's app changes would then never be diffed.
A newer push to a pull request still cancels that pull request's older run.
Pushes and same-repo pull requests run the `app` job on the self-hosted Mac runners, every one labeled `clicky-mac`, because GitHub's macOS queue can take over 10 minutes.
Each runner takes one job at a time, so the Mac has several runner processes, and the jobs run side by side.
The runners all run as one separate macOS user, so jobs that run at the same time are not isolated from each other.
Each Mac runner keeps its DerivedData between jobs in `~/Library/Caches/clicky-ci-derived-data/<runner name>/<Xcode build version>`, with the runner name cleaned as in the bundle ID suffix, so Xcode compiles only what a change touches.
Test results go to the job's temp folder, so the kept folder does not grow with every run.
A job whose `xcodebuild` step was cancelled, by a newer push or the job's timeout, deletes its runner's folder for the current Xcode build, so the next job builds from scratch.
A compile error or a failing test keeps the folder, since Xcode builds on from it.
Each job deletes the folders its own runner kept for other Xcode build versions.
To make one runner build from scratch by hand, delete its folder there as the `clicky-ci` user.
GitHub's runner for fork pull requests builds in the job's temp folder.
Each build runs at most 4 build tasks at once (`xcodebuild -jobs 4`), since three builds at Xcode's default of one per core made the 32 GB Mac swap about 37 GB.
The developer's Screen Recording and Accessibility grants belong to the `Clicky.app` their Xcode builds, and CI never replaces that app.
The test host only checks those permissions and never requests them.
macOS keeps one Accessibility grant per bundle ID for the whole Mac, across users, and an unsigned app with that ID that checks it resets the grant, so CI builds with `CLICKY_BUNDLE_ID_SUFFIX=.ci-<runner name>`, with each character other than a letter or digit turned into `-`, and the app of the runner named `clicky-mac-2` is `com.crodris.clicky-watch-along.ci-clicky-mac-2`.
The runner name in the suffix keeps test hosts that run at the same time from sharing the app's own defaults, and a test that reads a suite back names it after the bundle ID, the checkout, and the test process too (`perRunTestDefaultsSuiteName`, with `removeTestDefaultsSuite` deleting its preferences file and refusing any name `perRunTestDefaultsSuiteName` did not make), so two runs of one checkout never share it.
`tools/swift-check.sh` runs every checkout's tests under one test runner's bundle ID, so that name also carries a hash of the checkout's path, and parallel worktrees never share a suite.
The project's app bundle ID is `com.crodris.clicky-watch-along$(CLICKY_BUNDLE_ID_SUFFIX)`, and the suffix is empty everywhere else.
Before that, on 2026-11-20, each CI test run on the Mac runner could turn off the developer's Accessibility grant.
The developer's home folder must stay mode 700, so CI jobs cannot read their files or tokens.
The hosted tests need a desktop session to reach `testmanagerd`, so after a restart, log in to the runner's user once through fast user switching, then switch back, and the runner keeps running in that background session.
While the Mac sleeps or the runner's user is logged out, these jobs wait, and GitHub cancels a job that waits over 24 hours.
On the Mac runner, a step fails the job when `xcodebuild -version` is not Xcode 26.5.
After updating Xcode, update every `26.5` in `ci.yml`, this file, and `README.md`.
Fork pull requests run on GitHub's `macos-latest`, and the repo requires approval before an outside contributor's workflow runs.
A fork can change `runs-on` or add a workflow, so never approve a fork run whose diff touches `.github/`.

## Verification

The `verify-clicky` project skill (`.claude/skills/verify-clicky/`) proves behavior on the real Worker and Debug app.
It drives Worker routes with curl and the app with the DEBUG-only `--live-scene-rehearsal` flag, and saves responses, usage lines, app logs, and screenshots under `.verify-clicky/runs/`.
Its feature map in `features/` lists each feature's recipe, and a change to a mapped feature updates its file.

## Code Style & Conventions

### Variable and Method Naming

IMPORTANT: Follow these naming rules strictly. Clarity is the top priority.

- Be as clear and specific with variable and method names as possible
- **Optimize for clarity over concision.** A developer with zero context on the codebase should immediately understand what a variable or method does just from reading its name
- Use longer names when it improves clarity. Do NOT use single-character variable names
- Example: use `originalQuestionLastAnsweredDate` instead of `originalAnswered`
- When passing props or arguments to functions, keep the same names as the original variable. Do not shorten or abbreviate parameter names. If you have `currentCardData`, pass it as `currentCardData`, not `card` or `cardData`

### Code Clarity

- **Clear is better than clever.** Do not write functionality in fewer lines if it makes the code harder to understand
- Write more lines of code if additional lines improve readability and comprehension
- Make things so clear that someone with zero context would completely understand the variable names, method names, what things do, and why they exist
- When a variable or method name alone cannot fully explain something, add a comment explaining what is happening and why

### Swift/SwiftUI Conventions

- Use SwiftUI for all UI unless a feature is only supported in AppKit (e.g., `NSPanel` for floating windows)
- All UI state updates must be on `@MainActor`
- Use async/await for all asynchronous operations
- Comments should explain "why" not just "what", especially for non-obvious AppKit bridging
- AppKit `NSPanel`/`NSWindow` bridged into SwiftUI via `NSHostingView`
- All buttons must show a pointer cursor on hover
- For any interactive element, explicitly think through its hover behavior (cursor, visual feedback, and whether hover should communicate clickability)

### Do NOT

- Do not add features, refactor code, or make "improvements" beyond what was asked
- Do not add docstrings, comments, or type annotations to code you did not change
- Do not try to fix the known non-blocking warnings (Swift 6 concurrency, deprecated onChange)
- Do not rename the project directory or scheme (the "leanring" typo is intentional/legacy)
- Do not run `xcodebuild` from the terminal, since it invalidates TCC permissions

## Git Workflow

- Branch naming: `feature/description` or `fix/description`
- Commit messages: imperative mood, concise, explain the "why" not the "what"
- Do not force-push to main
